Meanwhile, the Sword King, who had come up to the surface, was flustered by the sight he had not expected. Instead of those who should have been there, the Demon Venerables were present.

All around, there were only traces of a fierce battle; the Fist King was nowhere to be seen. Judging by the situation, it seemed that he, his disciple, and the masters who had accompanied him had all died.

The Fist Demon asked in a low tone,

“Where is the Young Cult Leader?”

The Sword King could feel it. The Fist Demon before his eyes was stronger than he had expected. The same went for Ilhwa Sword Venerable standing behind him. That explained why the Fist King was not here.

The gaze with which the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign looked at the Sword King also grew grave. He possessed a presence greater than any enemy she had seen thus far.

She, too, revealed her aura and spoke in a cold tone.

“I asked where the Young Cult Master is.”

Saaaaaaaaah!

In an instant, their auras collided.

Not only the place where the Fist Demon, the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, and Ian stood, but the area around them as well was filled with sword blades. It felt as though even a single step would slice their flesh open.

Among those countless blades was one single blade aimed at the Sword King.

Wind blew between those blades. It was not a fierce wind, but it was one that chilled the heart and soul.

As the auras of the three clashed like this, a hair-trigger situation unfolded, as though they might attack one another at any moment.

The taut cord of tension seemed as if it would snap if someone so much as took one loud breath.

It was right then.

Srrrrrk.

The chair began to descend again.

And then the chair rose once more, carrying someone they were all too glad to see.

“I knew this would happen.”

Feeling the terrifying auras spread around him, Geom Mugeuk spoke with a smile.

“That is why I said I should go up first, didn’t I?”

At that sight, the Fist Demon, the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, and Ian finally felt relief. Geom Mugeuk had returned, unchanged as ever.

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes met the Fist Demon’s. His face was frightening and blunt, but just by looking into his eyes, Geom Mugeuk could tell with what feelings he had waited for him.

The Ilhwa Sword Sovereign smiled with an expression that said, You’ve finally returned, and Ian, whose legs had given out as the tension left him, crouched on the floor and beamed brightly at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk looked around.

The surroundings were empty, as if the battle had been terribly fierce. And when he saw that only the chair connected to the underground remained, he was deeply moved inwardly. The moment he saw it, he felt that it was the Fist Demon’s consideration for him.

The Fist Demon stood in the center, with the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign and Ian to his left and right.

The three of them respectfully cupped their fists and greeted Geom Mugeuk.

“We greet the Young Cult Master.”

The Fist Demon had beaten down the Fist King and guarded this place for the sake of saying those very words. For this very moment.

Geom Mugeuk respectfully cupped his fists and returned the courtesy.

“I believed you would wait for me.”

For the Fist Demon, those words were enough. To answer that trust—this was the mission and joy of the man called the Fist Demon, passed down from the time of the Cult Leader.

Geom Mugeuk turned to the Sword King. The Sword King was looking at him with many emotions on his face.

Hoping that relief was among those complicated emotions, he said,

“Now then, please continue what you were saying.”

Chapter 606: I Have a Gathering, You See?
-----------------------------------------
What had they been talking about down below before coming up?

At the Sword King’s confidence, Geom Mugeuk had made him a proposal.

“Let’s make a wager. If the two of them are on the dueling platform!”

“On whether we become friends?”

That was naturally what he had expected, but Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“No. We can’t decide such an important matter through other people’s affairs.”

“Then what are we wagering on?”

“The loser grants the winner one request. Separate from whether we become friends or not.”

The Sword King looked at Geom Mugeuk suspiciously, wondering what sort of scheme he was trying to pull this time, but how could he know what was inside that head?

“But you must grant it, no matter what.”

Normally, this was where the Sword King should have said something like this:

How can I make such a promise without knowing what you’ll ask?

He should have asked what it was, set conditions. I can’t grant this sort of request; don’t even bring up that sort of request.

But the Sword King asked nothing.

“Fine. Let’s do it.”

Perhaps that was why one was drawn to a man called the Sword King.

“Then tell me your request first.”

“Are you admitting you’ve already lost?”

“Of course not. I won’t have any occasion to grant your request anyway, Instructor, so I’m just curious what it is. If you lose the wager, you won’t tell me, will you?”

The Sword King snorted.

“Then shall I make one request in advance?”

“Go ahead.”

Geom Mugeuk had said it in jest, but the Sword King answered seriously.

“When you confirm the Demon Venerables’ deaths, don’t try to take revenge. Just leave today.”

The once-light atmosphere grew heavy.

“Are you afraid you’ll end up killing me?”

The Sword King did not answer and changed the subject.

“Let’s go up. Ah, before we go, there’s something I need to take.”

The Sword King walked over to the bookshelf, chose one secret manual to give to Yeon Baegin, and tucked it into his robe.

“You even keep your promises. I told you, you’re admirable.”

“Is that really so?”

For some reason, the Sword King’s gaze carried an unfathomable meaning.

The two of them left the final room. They left the secret chest for the time being. They could bring it out after matters above were settled anyway.

The two retraced their path through the trials they had passed and the places where they had dismantled mechanisms, until they arrived beneath the chair that led up to the surface.

“I’ll go up first.”

At that, the Sword King stopped Geom Mugeuk and stepped forward.

“No. I’ll go up first.”

“Surely you’re not worried about me?”

“I’m worried about our people.”

But Geom Mugeuk could tell. The Sword King was worried about him. If there truly was an expert up there capable of killing two Demon Venerables, as he was so certain, then he would not be worrying about them, at least. He was worried that Geom Mugeuk might rampage in revenge and attack him.

Without another word, the Sword King activated the mechanism.

“Ah, I should be the one going up first. Don’t fight—just greet each other!”

And so he sent the Sword King up first.

Geom Mugeuk followed after him and, once he had reunited with the three, turned to the Sword King and said,

“Now then, let’s continue the conversation we were having.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Fist Demon and Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked at each other. The two of them felt much the same.

As expected! The Young Cult Leader is the Young Cult Leader!

They had learned more than enough from the aura he had shown moments ago just how extraordinary a master the Sword King was.

And yet he had come up together with such a formidable opponent and was asking to continue the conversation they had been having? It seemed they had been fighting with their mouths down below rather than with swords.

Ian expressed exactly what the two of them were feeling.

“At the very least, our Young Cult Leader has not had his soul stolen by anyone.”

At her quiet words, the Fist Demon and Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled faintly.

At last, the Sword King opened his mouth.

He readily admitted that he had lost the wager.

“What is your request?”

It had clearly seemed like a wager made because Geom Mugeuk had something to ask of him. But Geom Mugeuk did not answer right away.

“It is a request I will make of a friend.”

It meant he would say it after they became friends.

At that, the Sword King spoke as though all the rapport they had built had crumbled and they had returned to the starting point.

“Then you’ll never be able to make it.”

Geom Mugeuk understood. Yes, it must feel different now from when they had been alone.

His comrades had died, and Demon Venerables were standing in that place. He must feel as though he had returned to reality.

The remaining persuasion had to be done within this reality.

Persuading him in the presence of Demon Venerables would be twice as difficult. After all, becoming friends with someone so ridiculously young while others were watching would truly not be easy.

Geom Mugeuk sent a sound transmission to the Sword King.

—I would like to send those three away and speak with you alone, Instructor, but because they are worried about me, they will never leave.

It meant that they would have to finish the conversation they had been having with the Demon Venerables present.

It was right then.

“I need to go wash up.”

The Fist Demon turned away first.

Geom Mugeuk shouted at him urgently.

“Master! You’re leaving me here by myself?”

At the title “Master,” the Fist Demon now treated Geom Mugeuk not as the Young Cult Leader, but as his disciple.

“I haven’t washed for days waiting for you. I feel utterly grimy.”

With those words, he strode off.

Ilhwa Sword Venerable followed behind him.

“Young Cult Leader, I’ll go on ahead to the safe house. Finish up here and come.”

“What if someone else appears while I’m here alone?”

Ian, who was following after them, answered that.

“Then that someone will have a hard time too.”

As Ian passed by, she bowed politely to the Sword King.

“You have it hard.”

With an expression that seemed to say, So you understand how I feel, the Sword King silently nodded.

Geom Mugeuk shouted at Ian.

“You should be saying that to me.”

Ian pretended not to hear and followed Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

The Fist Demon, Ilhwa Sword Venerable, and Ian knew well what kind of person Geom Mugeuk was.

If he said he wanted to continue the conversation he had been having, then they now understood that giving him space was the way to help him.

“Bring wine and good side dishes when you come!”

With Ian’s final words, the three of them moved away.

Watching them, the Sword King said to Geom Mugeuk,

“We should have wagered on whether they would leave or not.”

Though he said that, the Sword King could tell.

They understood this Young Cult Leader better than anyone. At the same time, they truly, sincerely cared for him.

How could he not know? He had seen the momentum the Demon Venerables had shown earlier, and the loyal way they had behaved when reunited with Geom Mugeuk.

And so, in that place, only Geom Mugeuk, the Sword King, and Yeon Baegin remained.

In truth, the person most pleased by the Sword King’s appearance was none other than Yeon Baegin.

He had no idea how long he had waited for this moment. For this moment, he had even cast aside heavenly bonds of kinship.

Even after the Demon Venerables had left, Yeon Baegin stood awkwardly, watching the two men’s expressions. The Yellow Dragon Martial Hall had collapsed, he had made enemies of Demon Venerables, and in truth, he was no longer in his right mind. Now his only remaining hope was the secret manual.

Since he had learned that Geom Mugeuk was the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, he did not dare even meet his eyes.

At that moment, the Sword King took the secret manual from his robe and tossed it to him.

“What I promised you. Here it is!”

Like something thrown with a here, take it, Yeon Baegin flung himself forward and caught the secret manual.

Yeon Baegin’s mouth split wide in delight.

‘I endured everything for this moment. Once I learn this peerless divine art, I will repay this humiliation without fail. You’re all dead now!’

The moment Yeon Baegin saw the title of the secret manual, he flinched. He hurriedly checked the contents, and his face stiffened at once.

“Isn’t this a spear art manual?”

“It is. Once you achieve great mastery in that spear art, Hall Master Yeon, no one will dare treat you lightly.”

For someone who had practiced swordsmanship, learning spear arts from the beginning would not be easy. Moreover, at his age, when would he learn it anew and achieve great mastery?

“You! Are you mocking me?”

The Sword King paid no mind to him, even as he shouted so fearlessly.

“I am someone who does not keep promises. Do you want to become friends with such an untrustworthy person?”

When the Sword King asked as if testing him, Geom Mugeuk gave an inscrutable answer.

“This side is no pushover either.”

Flash!

Puaaaak!

The sword energy that flew in an instant struck Yeon Baegin, and he collapsed, blood spraying from his heart.

“What do you think? Of such a heartless friend?”

In truth, he had not killed him because he was heartless.

Yeon Baegin had committed many crimes worthy of death. Among them, the one that had especially enraged both Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King was this:

Fearing that the officials from the martial hall who had emerged from the underground would spread rumors about the underground treasury, he had tried to hire an assassination organization to kill them and silence them.

The moment he made that decision, his fate had already been sealed as death. It had merely been a question of when.

Now only two people remained there.

Only the Sword King and Geom Mugeuk stood atop the dueling platform.

Whoooooosh.

A gust of wind blew between the two of them.

Geom Mugeuk thought that perhaps standing face-to-face with him on this dueling platform like this was his fate with the man.

If this was fate?

But he would not accept it exactly as fate handed it to him. He would knock on it, pull at it, push it. He would shout that this was not right. Only after doing everything he could would he say it.

If this is fate, then I must accept it.

“In the end, I’ve come to stand alone with you on this dueling platform, Instructor.”

The Sword King nodded. Just when he thought the outcome of this fight would determine whether they became friends—

“Let’s use this dueling platform in a way that suits us.”

Geom Mugeuk desperately shoved aside the enormous wheel of fate that had come rolling toward him.

Because he knew better than anyone that even a slight change in direction here would lead it to arrive somewhere completely different when it rolled far into the distance.

“In a way that suits us?”

“Yes, in a way that suits us.”

Geom Mugeuk did not have the slightest desire to wage a life-and-death blood battle with him.

“We met as instructor and trainee, did we not?”

To the Sword King, who still did not understand his intention, Geom Mugeuk said something astonishing.

“Please teach me swordsmanship.”

The Sword King was startled. Earlier, during class, it could be dismissed as a class, but he had never expected Geom Mugeuk to ask him to teach him swordsmanship here.

“You are mad to the very end.”

“How bad must it be for the Demon Venerables to abandon the Young Cult Leader and all run away? Even my own heart has fled.”

The Sword King silently stared at Geom Mugeuk.

“Fine. I’ll teach you one move.”

The Sword King slowly walked toward Geom Mugeuk.

He approached to a distance where, if he thrust his sword, it would barely reach, then slowly drew his blade.

And then, at a speed neither fast nor slow, he thrust his sword forward.

Shhk.

There was no trick, no technique, no dazzling form contained within it. It was a movement found in beginner sword manuals read by those learning swordsmanship for the first time.

The Sword King had simply drawn his sword and thrust it forward.

As Geom Mugeuk watched the sword flying toward his heart, he shouted inwardly.

‘A perfect thrust!’

It was a single move containing the essence of the Sword King’s swordsmanship, and that one move seemed to say this:

All forms of swordsmanship originate from this movement.

Paat.

The Sword King’s sword stopped before Geom Mugeuk’s heart.

A brief silence flowed between them. He did not ask why the man had not killed him when he could have. They had already passed that stage.

Geom Mugeuk respectfully bowed his head to the Sword King and said,

“I have learned well.”

It was not an empty phrase. The single move the Sword King had just shown him would have an immense influence on Geom Mugeuk as he continued to learn swordsmanship in the future.

Clack.

The Sword King returned his iron sword to its sheath and said,

“Becoming friends with you is… a losing bargain for me.”

At last, the word “friend” came first from his mouth.

“Between friends, what loss or gain is there?”

“That’s not what I mean…”

He did not mean that Geom Mugeuk was lacking or insufficient.

“One person is your master, and another is a Demon Venerable. But I’m just a friend, aren’t I? I’m losing out too much.”

He was comparing himself to the Fist Demon and Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

Emotion surged in Geom Mugeuk’s heart. The man was saying it. Just a little more. Hold on to me a little more tightly.

“The Demon Venerables are losing out more.”

“Why?”

“Because no matter how long they live, they can never become friends with the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. They will probably be truly envious of you, Instructor.”

Geom Mugeuk took one step forward, gazed at him, and said,

“If fate is pushing us this far, should we not accept it?”

“It wasn’t fate… You were the one pushing.”

Then, in a small, indifferent voice, he said,

“…Let’s do it.”

“Yes? What did you say?”

It was slightly louder than before, but still no louder than the sound of an ant.

“I said, let’s be friends.”

Even after hearing the words he had so desperately wanted to hear, Geom Mugeuk asked again in a louder voice.

“I can’t hear what you’re saying. What was that?”

In the end, the Sword King shouted loudly.

“I said let’s be friends, you bastard!”

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly.

“Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

Geom Mugeuk knew full well how difficult a decision he had made. That was why he was so delighted he practically jumped for joy. He was so happy that he found himself wondering what he would have done if the man had refused.

“But even when you become the Cult Leader, we’ll still be friends.”

“When you think of it that way, it’s no loss at all, is it?”

The Sword King nodded.

“If I’m friends with the future Demonic Cult Leader, it doesn’t seem like a bad bargain.”

“But you’ll have to live a long life. My father is terribly hale and hearty.”

“Even if I have to return to youth, I’ll be friends with the Demonic Cult Leader.”

With a pleasant smile, the Sword King bid him farewell.

“I’ll be heading back now. I have to repay my debt first.”

“You’re not going to tell me where that place is, are you?”

“No.”

“And you won’t take me with you either.”

As expected, the Sword King nodded and began to walk away.

“I’ll retrieve the secret chest myself.”

He did not say he would come back. Nor did he say they would meet again.

Looking at the shoes dangling from the man’s waist, Geom Mugeuk brought up the words the Sword King had forgotten.

“Now that we’re friends, I’ll use the favor I’ve been saving.”

The Sword King stopped in his tracks and answered as if he already knew what the request would be.

“I absolutely won’t die. Don’t worry.”

Whoooosh.

A solemn wind blew between the two of them.

“That’s not the favor.”

The Sword King turned back with an awkward expression.

“Why not? Isn’t this exactly the situation where you’d ask for something like that?”

“My friend is this strong—how could anyone kill you? Just don’t go around killing people because they annoy you for no reason.”

At those words, the Sword King finally burst into laughter. It was a statement that showed just how much Geom Mugeuk trusted him.

“Then what is the favor?”

The request he had promised he could never refuse was this.

“I have a friends’ gathering, you see? If it’s held later, you must attend! It’s mandatory. We drink, sing, and even dance.”

Before he knew it, the Sword King had taken the shoes in his hand and was about to throw them.

“Just try inviting me!”

And so, as he walked away into the distance, the Sword King looked back at Geom Mugeuk. Had he ever turned back to look at someone like this while walking away?

His newly made young friend was standing there in silence, watching him go.

Was it because he stood alone atop the sparring stage? Those clear, deep eyes looked lonely.

The Sword King called out in a loud voice.

“My name is Ak Gunhak.”

Those two eyes that had seemed lonely lit up in a bright smile.

It was a name Geom Mugeuk already knew.

Ak Gunhak, the First King of the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

This was the Ak Gunhak he had known.

But in this new life, Ak Gunhak would live on with a different meaning.

As Geom Mugeuk’s friend, Ak Gunhak.

“It’s a truly wonderful name.”

Chapter 607: Think Just Anyone Can Be Killed to Silence Them?
-------------------------------------------------------------
Seojin was training in swordsmanship in the courtyard of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s safehouse.

The sword art she was performing was the Yellow Dragon Sword Art.

The Yellow Dragon Sword Art as reinterpreted by Geom Mugeuk was not difficult, yet it was profound.

It was the sort of sword art that revealed a different facet each time one’s skill improved. And so, the stronger she became, the more deeply she would come to admire it.

Over the past few days, she had devoted herself to training. During the day, she practiced swordsmanship, and when night fell, she cultivated ghost arts. It was the first time in her life she had ever trained in martial arts so earnestly.

Even before this incident, she had known well that the murim was a frightening and dangerous place. But as someone born of the Ghost Gate’s bloodline, she had never once felt true fear.

Yet through this incident, she realized something. The murim she had lived in until now had been a murim inside a greenhouse. A murim where one merely punished local ruffians.

The real murim was a place where a young man smiling brightly in front of you might be the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, and a barefoot martial hall instructor might be someone aiming for that Young Cult Leader’s life.

She had learned that once she opened the greenhouse door and stepped outside, it might not simply be a fierce wind waiting for her, but a sheer cliff of a thousand gil.

If Geom Mugeuk and Ian had not come to help her this time, would she truly have survived here?

Seojin’s gaze turned once more toward the entrance.

It felt as if the door might open at any moment.

The two Demon Venerables and Ian, who had left to bring back the Young Cult Leader, had not returned for several days.

‘You said that when you recruited me, didn’t you? That I should trust only you. I’m trusting you. So hurry back.’

When she thought about it, this whole affair had begun because of her, which made her worry all the more. It had started with the investigation into the death of Im Hyeon, the elder brother from her hometown.

Just then, Gyoseok and Yugwang of the White Dragon Class, who had come to the safehouse with her, emerged into the courtyard.

Now that they knew who she was, they treated Seojin with extreme caution.

“Were you training?”

Seojin smiled at the two of them.

“You can act comfortably around me.”

“We still can’t do that.”

To an ordinary martial artist, the demons of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult were terror itself.

Even while staying at the safehouse over the past few days, they had felt as though they were sitting on pins and needles. Afraid they might run into the demons managing the safehouse, afraid they might offend them, they had not even raised their voices.

“It’s stifling, isn’t it? Everyone should be back soon.”

Gyoseok, who had been watching Seojin’s expression, spoke as though half resigned to his fate.

“In the end, you’re going to kill us, aren’t you?”

Seojin asked back with a puzzled expression. They had been fine for days, so why now?

“Why are you suddenly saying that?”

At that, Gyoseok glanced at Yugwang beside him.

“He said his anxiety disappeared this morning.”

Yugwang, who had always gone around saying he felt uneasy, had suddenly said today that his anxiety had vanished.

“I don’t know why, but my heart feels at ease.”

At Yugwang’s words, Gyoseok let out a sigh.

“Thanks to that, I feel even more ominous.”

“I said the anxiety disappeared!”

“That’s why I’m asking why it suddenly disappeared!”

“Maybe something good is going to happen.”

“No, you have to keep feeling anxious! That’s what lets me feel at ease.”

Gyoseok was not saying it as a joke. His anxiety was sincere.

“I have one request. I wrote a letter in my room to send to my parents. If I die, please make sure it gets sent. Ah, don’t worry. I didn’t write a single word related to the Demonic Cult—no, the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. No one will ever know that we were silenced by the Divine Cult.”

It was right then.

“You think just anyone is worth killing to silence?”

The three of them turned in surprise and saw Ian entering.

“Commander! You’ve returned?”

Seeing that she had come back safely, Seojin was overjoyed.

Behind Ian, the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable followed in.

Seojin greeted the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable respectfully as well. Gyoseok and Yugwang retreated, lowering their heads and not even daring to breathe.

After the two Demon Venerables went inside first, Seojin asked Ian,

“What about the Young Cult Leader?”

“He’ll be here soon.”

Leaving Seojin to feel relieved, Ian teased Gyoseok and Yugwang.

“If you want to send a letter, go out and send it yourself. You brat, if you’re going to have useless worries like that, at least make them big enough to actually get you killed to silence you.”

At that, Gyoseok asked happily,

“You’re really sparing us?”

“If we were going to kill you, we’d have done it already.”

“Thank you.”

Then Gyoseok looked at Yugwang.

‘But why is your intuition so accurate?’

Thinking that his intuition had been right, Yugwang mustered his courage.

“I have a request.”

“What is it?”

“I want to follow you to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult too.”

Gyoseok was startled and tried to stop Yugwang.

“You! Are you insane?”

Ian quietly looked at Yugwang. It would not be bad to have at least one subordinate who could sense ill omens well. But he was far too young and innocent to take along for a reason like that.

“I refuse.”

“May I ask the reason?”

What was she supposed to say to a naive boy who had no idea what it meant to live as a demon? If war breaks out, you’ll have to point your sword at your friends? Could you kill Gyoseok beside you? Your parents, who sent you to a martial hall to learn martial arts, would faint. Are you still fine with that?

As Ian was wondering what answer she could use to convince him,

A refreshing reply came from the entrance.

“Because you’re not skilled enough yet.”

They turned and saw Geom Mugeuk entering the safehouse. This time, Ian and Seojin welcomed him together.

“Young Cult Leader!”

Ian had answered Seojin with conviction that Geom Mugeuk would arrive soon, but inwardly, she had still been worried. After all, his opponent had been who he was.

She had said, Who worries about whom? But if not her, then who else would worry about Geom Mugeuk?

“Become exactly ten times stronger than you are now, and then come find Ian.”

At that moment, Ian knew. That was the correct answer. No matter how many explanations they gave him, Yugwang would only be left with lingering regrets and a complicated heart.

But if he strove to become ten times stronger, he would grow into an excellent martial artist.

And by the time he became ten times stronger, this affair would have become a memory of his youth. If he still did not forget this matter and came to find them then, that could truly be called fate.

“Can you do that?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Yugwang answered with vigor.

“Yes! I’ll become ten times stronger and come see you again!”

After making that bold declaration, Yugwang’s expression darkened.

“Ah, I feel anxious again.”

At that sight, Gyoseok let out a sigh.

“So you’ll be anxious until you become ten times stronger. And I’ll have to live the rest of my life hearing you say you’re anxious.”

“You said I had to be anxious!”

Geom Mugeuk felt that the friendship between the two of them would continue just like this. Even if they came back after becoming ten times stronger, he had a feeling the two would come together. Yes, when you look back on this friendship, don’t lose it over something trivial. Protect it well.

Just then, Yeon Baekjin walked out to them. When he appeared, Gyoseok and Yugwang withdrew to their quarters and gave them space.

“You’ve returned?”

Yeon Baekjin was curious about what had happened to his older brother, but he did not dare ask first.

How could Geom Mugeuk not know what was in his heart?

“From now on, you are the hall master of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall.”

With those words, he understood. His brother was dead. He was about to ask who had killed him, but stopped. That would be no different from asking the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult, “Did you kill my brother?”

He was not as shocked as he had expected, nor did he feel as relieved or delighted as he thought he might.

“He went without pain. He died without even realizing he was dying, so do not be too grieved.”

Though it was the brother who had tried to kill him, the thought that it was fortunate he had died without pain came to him. Blood was blood, after all.

“From now on, the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall will change.”

His brother had run solely toward making it the greatest martial hall in the Central Plains. Yeon Baekjin had no such ambition.

“Even if I raise only one person, I will raise a martial artist with proper character.”

“You may start with the two fellows who just went inside.”

Yeon Baekjin nodded and looked at Geom Mugeuk, Ian, and then Seojin.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. In the way he looked at Seojin, there was no sign of the tragic fate that had gone all the way to the end.

After all, Geom Mugeuk had begun this affair to change the fate of those two.

‘I did it.’

Thus, the matter of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall was completely concluded.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk told the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable about what had happened with the Sword King.

“I’ve decided to get along with that man like a friend.”

He spoke honestly, thinking the Demon Venerables ought to know as well.

If the one who had said this had not been Geom Mugeuk, neither the Fist Demon nor the Ilhwa Sword Venerable would ever have believed it.

The aura of the Sword King that they had seen.

There had been no room between those densely packed sword blades for another person to step in.

“What kind of man is he?”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable showed her curiosity. Since the other party was a swordsman, her interest naturally arose.

“To be honest, I don’t know him deeply either, but I did get this impression. He is someone who wants to be freer than anyone, yet is bound hand and foot by them.”

At that, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable said something unexpected.

“Like you?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at her and smiled. In the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s eyes, it seemed he too appeared to be someone tightly bound and longing for freedom.

“Since there is someone who understands me this well, I think I can endure even if the ropes that bind me hurt.”

“Who in this world could bind the Young Cult Leader? You must have bound yourself.”

Because of that, she thought the freedom Geom Mugeuk longed for must be even more desperate.

“That’s right. I am someone who wants to roam this murim freely. The reason I took Father on a journey was because I hoped Father would also live a free life.”

Another layer of honesty was added to that.

“But as much as I desire freedom, I also have exactly that much desire for power. It is not some other position, but the seat of Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult. Freedom is fine for a day or two, but what sort of grand freedom would make me kick away this position without hesitation? I certainly have the desire to inherit it and manage it well.”

Of course, before that, he first had to persuade his father to give up on unifying the murim within the five years he had promised him.

Then the Fist Demon, who had only been listening, spoke calmly.

“You will become a good Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the Fist Demon. At times like this, he could feel just how much power the silence of taciturn people possessed.

If he said something like this to someone, they would say, “More flattery?” But a single sentence like this from the Fist Demon, who spoke little, flew in with great force.

“Of course, a better Cult Leader than Father, right?”

Of course, to the Fist Demon, his father’s existence was an impregnable fortress.

“That will be difficult.”

Geom Mugeuk asked with a smile. Now was the time to express his gratitude.

“The chair that led underground—you left it behind on purpose, Master, didn’t you?”

The person the Sword King had trusted must truly have been a master. Perhaps one of the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings. The Fist Demon had left the chair behind even while fighting such an expert.

The Fist Demon merely nodded slightly and did not boast about it.

The more someone did not boast, the more one ought to express gratitude.

By recognizing his intention and voicing his thanks, the difficult battle the Fist Demon had fought could be brought to a proper close.

Geom Mugeuk respectfully bowed his head and put a period on it.

“Thank you very much.”

* * *

That night, Geom Mugeuk opened the Spacetime Transposition Art.

For the first time in a while, it was not for training, but for rest.

A white sandy beach stretched out before an endless blue sea. Beneath a tree with broad leaves stood a comfortable chair.

“Ah! This is wonderful!”

As he leaned back comfortably in the chair, the words came out on their own.

“Now, you rest a little too.”

He placed the secret coffer on the spot he had made for it beside him.

Then, for a while, he gazed at the blue sky and sea.

As the sea breeze blew gently, carrying warm heat, his mood lifted on its own.

“I learned a great deal about you on this trip.”

As the energy in his body became his own, his feelings toward the secret coffer also grew more familiar.

“Where are the other three energies?”

For now, there was one place that came to mind.

The Murim Alliance.

The secret coffer had come from the main cult, and he had obtained orbs from the Heretical Alliance, the Pungcheon Cult, and the Northern Sea Ice Palace. In that case, would the Murim Alliance not have one as well?

“There is one in the Murim Alliance too, isn’t there? Right?”

If only you could speak.

Even if it was being kept in the Murim Alliance, he could not very well go to the alliance leader or Jin Hagun and tell them to hand over the orb. Like the things he had obtained until now, he would gain it when fate connected it to him.

Geom Mugeuk resolved not to hurry. It had been the same when he first obtained the secret chest. He would leave it to fate and keep his mind at ease.

Geom Mugeuk emptied his head and savored the time alone.

He dozed off in his chair, woke, strolled along the beach, then squatted down to watch the crabs crawling about.

He lay down again and fell asleep, then woke once more and gazed at the sea. Then he shouted.

“I really don’t want to leave!”

* * *

The next morning, the carriages returning to the main cult were prepared.

Three carriages.

The Fist Demon rode at the front, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable in the middle, and Geom Mugeuk, Ian, and Seojin rode in the last carriage. Out of consideration for their comfort, the two Demon Venerables had been given separate carriages.

Seojin expressed her gratitude to Geom Mugeuk, who sat across from her.

“Thanks to you, I was able to avenge my brother. Thank you, Young Cult Master.”

Considering what you did for me in my life before the regression, this is nothing.

With a meaningful smile, Geom Mugeuk asked her,

“How does it feel, going to the main cult?”

She seemed to ponder what to say for a moment.

Then Seojin abruptly held out her hand, palm facing upward.

Sssss.

Black smoke rose from her palm and formed a black wolf. It was small enough to fit in her hand, but it was a handsome wolf, and its gaze was dignified.

“When the Unit Leader first recruited me, this was how I was.”

Sssssss!

More black smoke rose in front of the wolf, forming the face of a huge tiger.

The moment that tiger opened its jaws wide—

The fine wolf burst with a pop and turned into a terrified dog with its tail tucked between its legs.

“And this is my current state.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed as he looked at the dog. So you knew how to do tricks like this, yet you never showed me once when you were a wandering warrior.

“That’s exactly what I look like in front of my father.”

With an expression of shared suffering, Geom Mugeuk stroked the dog, and the dog made of black smoke rubbed its face against his hand.

As he petted the dog, Geom Mugeuk said,

“It doesn’t matter whether you’re a tiger, a wolf, or a dog.”

“Then what does matter?”

The same wish Geom Mugeuk held for Ian was conveyed to Seojin as well.

“What matters is whether you’ll be happier than you are now when you go to the main corps. I don’t want an unhappy tiger.”

Seojin had not known the Young Cult Master for long, but she could tell, at the very least, that those words were sincere.

Pop!

The dog disappeared, and in its place, the wolf from before formed again.

“Even if I can’t become a happy tiger, I’ll do my best to become a happy wolf.”

Yes, the woman he had met as a wandering warrior before his regression had been like a lone wolf.

Live just like that, Seojin. But this time, be a wolf who lives with a bright smile.

Now that he thought of returning to the main cult, the faces he wanted to see came to mind one after another. Faces he had not properly seen because he had left the cult in such haste.

Geom Mugeuk stuck his head out the carriage window and shouted loudly.

“All right, departure for the main cult! This time, we’re going as fast as possible!”

Chapter 608: What Can You Do with Those Tiny Hands!
---------------------------------------------------
The carriage raced swiftly toward the main branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Except when they stopped for meals, rested the horses, or slept at inns at night, the carriage ran without pause.

Today, too, the carriage was speeding down the road.

As it passed in a cloud of dust, the sparrows perched on a scarecrow all flew up at once.

“They’re just like me.”

At Ian’s words, Geom Mugeuk raised his head.

“What is?”

Beyond the sparrows that had just taken flight and ridden the wind into the sky, a single crane stood on the edge of a rice paddy.

Its aloof, elegant figure seemed just like Geom Mugeuk, and she herself felt like one of the sparrows that had just fluttered up. The thought had simply slipped out.

By the time Geom Mugeuk looked out the carriage window, the scenery had already changed, as if to say everything had only been a fleeting instant.

“There is such a thing. Something that chirps.”

“How bland.”

“By the way, what have you been thinking so hard about?”

All throughout the carriage ride, Geom Mugeuk had been sunk in deep thought.

“Whether it’ll be cut off or not.”

“Pardon?”

Not understanding what he meant, Ian’s eyes went round. Seojin, who was with them, also looked puzzled.

Geom Mugeuk stuck his head out the window and called to the carriage ahead.

“Let’s stop for a moment!”

At that, the two carriages running ahead came to a halt.

Geom Mugeuk got down and went to the carriage carrying the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“We only have three or four days left until the main branch, so from here on, let’s travel together.”

Thinking there must be a reason for him to do this when everything had been going smoothly, the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable readily got down from the carriage.

Geom Mugeuk sent back the two carriages that had been leading the way, along with all three drivers.

“From now on, I’ll drive the carriage myself.”

There was indeed a reason Geom Mugeuk was doing this.

After letting the horses loose to graze in a nearby field, Geom Mugeuk gathered the two Demon Venerables, Ian, and Seojin in one place.

“Please come over here for a moment!”

The four of them gathered around Geom Mugeuk. Everyone wondered what he was up to.

“Ian, draw your sword.”

Being told out of nowhere to draw her sword, Ian did as she was told and pulled it free. The sharp energy of the Sun-Moon Sword flowed along its blade.

‘Is he trying to spar with me?’

But her guess was completely wrong.

Geom Mugeuk rolled up his sleeve and held out his arm.

“Strike my arm with that sword.”

A brief silence passed.

“Are you testing your protective aura?”

“No. I’m going to block it without protective aura.”

At those words, not only Ian but the other three were startled as well. Everyone’s eyes fixed on Geom Mugeuk’s arm and the Sun-Moon Sword.

“This is the Sun-Moon Sword. The Sun-Moon Sword you gave me as a gift, Young Cult Leader. The blade is far too sharp to test whether it’s genuine or not, isn’t it?”

“I’m not trying to test whether that is genuine. I’m testing whether something else is genuine.”

What on earth was he talking about?

“My arm is getting tired. Hurry and strike.”

“If I strike, that tired arm will be gone.”

“Hurry!”

Ian looked at the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. As if those two would know Geom Mugeuk’s intentions any better.

At last, the Fist Demon nodded to his daughter. It meant she should do as she was told.

“I’m really going to hit you.”

“Hit hard!”

Fine. If it were anyone else, maybe not, but since it was the Young Cult Leader, he must have some plan. Ah, whatever. I don’t know anymore.

The Sun-Moon Sword split the air. Since he had said he would not use protective aura, she did not infuse her sword with inner energy either. Even so, it was not a sword one could block with bare flesh.

Ssshhk.

Thwack.

For an instant, silence settled over the place.

Everyone froze as though time itself had stopped.

The Sun-Moon Sword lay directly against Geom Mugeuk’s forearm. Astonishingly, despite having struck down hard with the Sun-Moon Sword, it had not cut off Geom Mugeuk’s arm.

“Are you all right?”

“No. It hurts like hell. Were you trying to cut off the Young Cult Leader’s arm?”

“You told me to strike hard.”

“I didn’t mean that hard. I think the bone broke.”

Of course, unlike his fussing, Geom Mugeuk’s arm was perfectly fine.

“You raised your protective aura just now, didn’t you? You did, right?”

“No.”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

A strange light flashed through the eyes of the Fist Demon, who had been watching.

“It was not protective aura.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk gave a faint smile.

“As expected. Master, you recognized it.”

Everyone was surprised by those words. Ian, in particular, was greatly shocked.

“Even if it’s you, Young Cult Leader, you endured the Sun-Moon Sword without protective aura?”

“I used this instead.”

Geom Mugeuk took a martial arts manual from his robes.

Heavenly Steel Body.

A body-protecting demonic art.

It was the very manual he had taken from the treasure vault where the secret coffer had been, the only one he had brought back.

The Fist Demon knew about this martial art.

“The Heavenly Steel Body is a demonic art that was lost long ago. Where did you obtain it?”

“I found it this time in the underground treasure vault.”

Geom Mugeuk explained what he knew about the Heavenly Steel Body.

“The good thing about the Heavenly Steel Body is that it can be used together with other protective arts. If used alongside protective aura in a moment of crisis, it will be of great help. Since I obtained it while dealing with the matter of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall, let us all learn this martial art together.”

At that, the Fist Demon declined.

“A martial art of such supreme level has its own destined master. Since it is tied to you by fate, you alone should learn it.”

“To be honest, I was planning to learn it by myself anyway.”

It was not because of fate or greed.

“To raise protective aura and deploy this Heavenly Steel Body at the same time requires an enormous amount of inner energy. If used clumsily, one might only waste inner energy and end up worse off than using protective aura alone. Even if one succeeds, it may strain the body.”

That was why he had intended to use it alone.

“But while practicing it, I found a way to use it with existing protective arts while minimizing the inner energy required.”

At those words, the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked at each other in surprise. They knew very well how difficult it was to modify and use a supreme demonic art like the Heavenly Steel Body. And he had corrected its greatest weakness at that.

“You figured that out during the journey here?”

“When it comes to martial arts, am I not rather genius-like?”

Of course, whenever they stayed at inns, he had used the Time-Space Transposition Art and activated the Time-Heaven Secret Art within it to ponder and train for a much longer time.

Even so, to newly improve and use a martial art like the Heavenly Steel Body was truly an extraordinary feat.

“Please allow this disciple to show off a little. And only if the two of you accept it can Ian and Seojin accept it as well.”

After all, they could not readily learn a demonic art that a Demon Venerable had declined.

When he put it that way, the Fist Demon no longer refused.

“I will now recite the formula.”

At that, Ian and Seojin were deeply overwhelmed.

“You are truly going to transmit such a precious demonic art to us as well?”

Ian was genuinely moved. The Flying Heaven Sword Art, the Sun-Moon Sword, and now the Heavenly Steel Body as well. The Cult Leader had even given her the Ten-Thousand-Year Snow Ginseng.

‘Just how much are you planning to work me to the bone?’

Seeing the tremble in her eyes, Geom Mugeuk shattered her emotion to pieces.

“It’s not for your sake.”

“Then?”

Geom Mugeuk drew the Black Demon Sword and said,

“That way, I can strike you too. See this bruise? You’re next!”

Ian flinched at the sight of the Black Demon Sword and stepped back.

“I’ll hold out my arm after I achieve great mastery. Please wait about thirty years.”

Though he knew perfectly well it was a joke, Geom Mugeuk played along.

“I won’t forget. I’ll test it then.”

Those words made Ian feel good. They also meant they would still be together thirty years from now.

The one who felt most overwhelmed among them was Seojin. She even waved her hands and stepped back.

“I am fine.”

Ian was Geom Mugeuk’s direct unit leader and someone called his heart, so that was one thing. But was she not merely a squad leader? She could not learn such an incredible demonic art alongside Demon Venerables.

“No, you need this martial art the most. You have to survive until the day you save us with your ghost arts, don’t you?”

Seojin truly wanted that. She hoped the day would come when she saved these people with her ghost arts.

“When a martial artist should be cautious is not when learning martial arts, but when using them. Right now, you just have to say, ‘Thank you.’”

Seojin felt it again. The Young Cult Leader’s excessive affection.

“Thank you.”

How could Geom Mugeuk not give them excessive affection?

Ian and Seojin.

They were the two people who had made his regression possible. They were people for whom nothing he gave would ever feel wasted.

And so Geom Mugeuk taught the four of them the formula of the newly revised Heavenly Steel Body. He repeated the formula over and over so that all of them could memorize it.

Afterward, for the several days it took to return to the main branch, everyone trained in the Heavenly Steel Body.

The Fist Demon, who had so strongly refused the Heavenly Steel Body at first, trained the hardest.

“What would you have done if I hadn’t transmitted it to you?”

Whenever Geom Mugeuk saw that huge frame training even late into the night, he teased him, but the Fist Demon silently focused only on his training.

In truth, it was understandable. From the beginning, a protective art like the Heavenly Steel Body was most useful to a fist fighter who fought with his bare body.

Had that fervor spread?

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable also trained properly in the Heavenly Steel Body. Since it was a protective art that suited demonic arts well, she too felt its necessity.

As a result, Geom Mugeuk and the two Demon Venerables naturally began sharing many discussions about the Heavenly Steel Body.

There were weaknesses the Fist Demon sensed in the Heavenly Steel Body, and there were things the Ilhwa Sword Venerable perceived as well. Since they had never spoken so openly about a single martial art before, the two Demon Venerables found these conversations extremely interesting.

The three of them, in particular, had recently made great achievements in martial arts. Therefore, their discussions contained deep implications and insights not only into the principles of protective arts, but into martial learning as a whole.

Ian and Seojin simply listened. There were many things they could not understand, but the two of them tried to remember as much as they could. Someday, a day would come when they understood what the things they heard today had meant.

As a result of the three of them discussing it like this, they made it possible to circulate the formula a little more smoothly.

“Thanks to you two, it is no longer the New Heavenly Steel Body, but the New-New Heavenly Steel Body! At last, the martial art is complete.”

But the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable knew. The reason they had been able to make various suggestions was because the form Geom Mugeuk had improved already existed.

If they had heard the original Heavenly Steel Body, they would never even have thought to improve it. They would simply have passed it off as, So this is that kind of martial art. That difference was far too great.

“Now, draw your sword.”

This time, the Fist Demon had Geom Mugeuk draw his sword.

The Fist Demon held out his massive fist and forearm.

“Strike.”

“You know, don’t you? My sword is the Black Demon Sword. A demonic sword two ranks higher. Oh, and the one striking is also two ranks higher. It’ll get cut. It really will.”

“Enough. Hurry and strike.”

“Then here I go!”

Ssshhhk.

Thwaaack!

The Black Demon Sword could not cut the Fist Demon’s thick forearm.

“Excellent.”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk also had not infused his sword with inner energy.

But considering that it had only been a few days since they learned it, by blocking the sharpness of the Black Demon Sword, the Heavenly Steel Body proved just how remarkable a protective art it was.

“Now, this time let us test blunt force.”

“Excellent. Where shall I hit you? Just say the word.”

“This time, I should be the one to strike. What kind of test could those bracken-like little hands of yours possibly make?”

Watching the Fist Demon clench that huge fist, Geom Mugeuk hurriedly fled backward.

“Thirty years from now, on the day I cut Ian’s arm, I’ll take that fist.”

The three women watching them laughed together.

Just then, Seojin suddenly said to Ian,

“Did I perhaps tell you?”

“Tell me what?”

“That I’m grateful you chose me.”

Seojin looked at Ian and spoke formally.

“Thank you for becoming my unit leader.”

The words came out of nowhere, but it was a proper and sincere expression of gratitude.

She felt that what she had learned and realized this time was more than everything she had experienced in her life until now combined.

She was grateful that she had received so comfortably and easily what others might have to suffer their entire lives to obtain.

“I learned a lot thanks to you too, Squad Leader Seo.”

She was not saying it merely to sound nice. Ian, too, had gained many realizations and lessons during this trip outside the cult.

Ian’s gaze naturally turned toward the Ilhwa Sword Venerable standing beside her.

Elegant and beautiful, yet stronger than anyone.

In the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, Ian saw the form she herself must pursue in the future.

She knew very well how important that was.

What mattered was always direction.

No matter how hard a person tried, if the direction of that effort was wrong, they could not obtain results equal to their labor.

Rather, that effort might become poison and swallow them whole. To think I tried this hard, only to get such a result. I was a fool, wasting my suffering for nothing.

At such times, one could simply turn back, but instead, the efforts made until then would catch at one’s ankles.

That was why direction mattered from the start.

Because only when effort met the proper direction did it become destiny.

Ian’s gaze turned toward the person who had taught her all of this.

As though they had never been joking around, Geom Mugeuk and the Fist Demon were engaged in a serious discussion about martial arts.

Ian conveyed to Geom Mugeuk the very words Seojin had said to her.

‘Thank you. For becoming my Young Cult Leader.’

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and the two Demon Venerables entered the Heavenly Demon Hall.

With Geom Mugeuk at the center, the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable followed behind him. It was customary not only for the Young Cult Leader, but also for the Demon Venerables, to seek out and greet the Heavenly Demon upon returning from outside the cult.

As Geom Mugeuk entered the Heavenly Demon Hall, he shouted loudly as always.

“Father, I have returned!”

But at the end of the Path of Blood, his father’s taishi chair was empty.

And instead of his father, Chief Strategist Sama Myeong was waiting.

“Young Cult Leader, you have returned?”

“Chief Strategist.”

The Demon Venerables and Sama Myeong exchanged greetings as well.

Once the greetings were over, Geom Mugeuk quickly asked,

“Where is Father?”

At that, Sama Myeong relayed unexpected news.

“The Cult Leader has entered closed-door training.”

Chapter 609: What Are You Thinking Sitting Here?
------------------------------------------------
Father entered seclusion?

Not only Geom Mugeuk, but the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who had come with him, all looked at Sima Myeong with tense expressions.

It had been quite a long time since his father had last entered closed-door cultivation.

“Did something happen, by any chance?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s worried question, Sima Myeong shook his head with a gentle expression.

“No. He entered seclusion for the sake of cultivation.”

Geom Mugeuk knew that after his regression, his father had changed as well. He also knew that, unlike in his previous life, his father had focused more on martial training.

For such a father to have entered seclusion clearly meant that he was on the verge of a major change in his martial realm.

Had his Nine Flames Demonic Art surpassed the great completion of the tenth star and entered the realm of the eleventh? Or was he trying to achieve the great completion of the twelfth star?

One thing was certain. His father before seclusion and his father after seclusion would undoubtedly be at different levels of strength.

“When Father emerges from seclusion, our cult’s power will become even stronger.”

At that, Sima Myeong gazed at Geom Mugeuk and asked,

“Are you uneasy?”

How could that be? More than anyone else, he was the one who wished for his father to become so strong that no one could ever dare look down on him.

But if that strength, as implied in the question just now, was directly tied to the unification of the martial world?

Since he had already officially stepped forward to stop his father’s unification of the martial world, Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“Overflowing power is bound to burst out someday.”

Geom Mugeuk looked back at the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

‘They really are overflowing with power.’

Of all people, the two Demon Venerables who had come with him today were precisely those whose loyalty to his father was the highest.

The Fist Demon, whom his father trusted the most.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who respected his father the most.

If his father commanded it, these two would leap into war tomorrow even if countless sacrifices followed.

“I shouldn’t have taught you the Cheongangsin.”

Knowing the meaning behind Geom Mugeuk’s joke, the two Demon Venerables merely smiled faintly.

When Geom Mugeuk looked back at Sima Myeong, he relayed his father’s words.

“He said that if the Young Cult Leader returned while he was away, I was to tell you this. While I am gone……”

Geom Mugeuk raised a hand, pretending to guess the next words.

“Take good care of the cult? I believe in my son!”

With an expression that seemed to say, You dream big, Sima Myeong imitated his father in a deep voice.

“Don’t cause trouble.”

Behind him, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s laughter rang out.

“He still doesn’t know me. I’m not someone who causes trouble—I’m a specialist in cleaning it up!”

Geom Mugeuk turned back and asked the Ilhwa Sword Venerable for support.

“You saw it, didn’t you? How well I handled this matter.”

At that, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked at the Fist Demon standing beside him and said,

“The Fist Demon handled it. Our Young Cult Leader only slipped out from underground after everything was already over. Am I wrong?”

As much mischief as flickered in the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s eyes, Geom Mugeuk felt wronged.

“Even you, Sword Venerable? This is truly too much! Do you know how much I suffered underground, breathing in dust? Don’t even get me started. Have you ever slept on a hard wooden bed? Have you ever been hit with a shoe? It’s only because I’m taciturn that I haven’t told you everything……”

When Geom Mugeuk’s words showed signs of growing long, the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable bade farewell to Sima Myeong.

“We will take our leave now.”

Sima Myeong also cupped his fists politely and received their farewell.

“Go and rest well.”

As the two turned away, Geom Mugeuk followed after them.

“Let’s go together. I’m not done talking yet. I’ll see you again, Chief Strategist.”

Just as Geom Mugeuk was about to follow them out, Sima Myeong called to him.

“Where do you think you are going, Young Cult Leader?”

“What do you mean, where? I’ve come back after so long, so now I should meet people and have some fun. Please believe me—I really suffered out there.”

“I do believe that, but there is something the Young Cult Leader must do.”

“Me?”

“Starting today, Young Cult Leader, you must take over the Cult Leader’s duties.”

Unexpected words flowed from Sima Myeong’s mouth.

“From this very moment, Young Cult Leader, you are the acting Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes went wide.

“You’re joking, right?”

“Before entering seclusion, the Cult Leader gave that order.”

“Until when?”

“Until the Cult Leader emerges from seclusion.”

No one could know whether closed-door cultivation would end tomorrow or ten years later.

“What if he comes out a year from now?”

“Then you must serve until then.”

It was at that very moment.

Ssssss.

Hwi, who had been hidden, revealed himself.

Step, step, step, step, step, step!

Behind Hwi, experts dressed in the same uniform appeared in a single line. They were the warriors of the Heavenly Demon Guard led by Hwi, and among them, the squad leaders permitted to enter the interior of the Heavenly Demon Hall. It was Geom Mugeuk’s first time seeing them all gathered in one place like this.

On the chests of their guard uniforms was the image of a shield, and within that shield was an evil spirit modeled after the Heavenly Demon Soul.

Hwi paid his respects solemnly and pledged his loyalty.

“Hwi, commander of the guard, shall henceforth serve the acting Cult Leader with my life.”

No, Uncle Hwi, why are you doing this?

Ssssss.

After formally offering their greeting, they vanished in an instant.

Once they disappeared, the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who had been turning to leave, turned back again and cupped their fists respectfully.

“We greet the acting Cult Leader.”

Their attitude was incomparably more respectful than before. Though he was not the actual Cult Leader, the two Demon Venerables showed him the courtesy due to the Cult Leader. They were not teasing him.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. He could tell how they regarded his father. There was an absolute wall between Young Cult Leader and Cult Leader that could never be crossed.

“No, isn’t your attitude changing too drastically in an instant?”

He hoped they would take it as a joke, but the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable were utterly serious.

“You two are discriminating against people!”

At that, the two Demon Venerables cupped their fists with a sharp motion and spoke together.

“Please give us your command.”

Since they refused to play along to the end, Geom Mugeuk sighed and spoke. Originally, he had intended to joke, Who was it that handled the cleanup? But in the face of their seriousness, he simply could not.

“Go and rest.”

“We shall obey your command.”

The Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable left the Heavenly Demon Hall along the Blood Path.

Far away, near the door, the Fist Demon looked back in his direction. His gaze said this:

‘It is an important position, so do well.’

With an expression devoid of mischief, Geom Mugeuk nodded once as well.

‘Yes, Master.’

Since it was an order given by his father, this matter could not have been a joke to his master.

Of course, that did not mean Geom Mugeuk would obediently accept the acting position.

Geom Mugeuk spoke to Sima Myeong with dignity.

“As acting Cult Leader, I command you. I entrust the acting position to the Chief Strategist. Please take charge of the cult from now on.”

Of course, it did not work.

“The Cult Leader said the Young Cult Leader would undoubtedly say that, and told me to relay that it is absolutely impossible.”

No, Father. Are you really doing this?

“There’s a line of people waiting for me!”

“Tell them to wait.”

“I haven’t even unpacked yet!”

“Do it after work.”

“This won’t do. I must enter seclusion as well. Honestly, I’m the one who needs to enter seclusion.”

This time too, Sima Myeong answered with ease.

“He anticipated that as well and said it is absolutely impossible.”

“Father! This is too much!”

Sima Myeong looked up at the Taishi chair and said,

“Please ascend to the Taishi chair now.”

“Do I have to work from Father’s seat? Can’t I just handle things comfortably from here?”

“You cannot. Even if it is in an acting capacity, you are standing in for the seat of the venerable Cult Leader.”

At Sima Myeong’s firm words, Geom Mugeuk had no choice but to climb the steps.

Geom Mugeuk did not sit down right away.

Standing beside the Taishi chair, he looked down at the empty seat for a moment. The image of his father, who had always sat here, rose in his mind.

‘Father. You should have sat here and sneered at me. Do you know how much I wanted to see that sneer?’

Because it was the absence of someone who had always kept his place, the sense of loss was all the greater.

Geom Mugeuk carefully sat in the Taishi chair.

Then, without a word, he looked around at the view visible from that place.

Not long after his regression, his father had once let him sit in this seat. When his father asked how it felt, he had answered that he felt nothing. In truth, that had been the case back then.

But now, it felt completely different from that time.

A strange tremor coursed through his whole body.

‘Father, why did you entrust this to me?’

Even if he had not specifically entrusted the acting position to him, there would have been no issue with leaving the work to Sima Myeong. Had he not once left it to Sima Myeong and gone traveling together?

And yet, the fact that he had entrusted the role of acting Cult Leader to him meant what?

‘Are you perhaps testing me?’

Testing whether he had the qualifications to become the Cult Leader.

At length, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to Sima Myeong.

“Looking down at you like this, Chief Strategist, makes me feel honored and apologetic.”

“Please do not say such things. Right now, you are standing in for the Cult Leader’s seat.”

“What should I do first?”

Sima Myeong walked toward a table set in the corner. Documents were piled high there.

“Is that the amount of work I’m supposed to handle over a lifetime?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Sima Myeong answered bluntly.

“It is the work you must handle today.”

Geom Mugeuk shouted into the air again.

“Uncle Hwi! Are you listening? Make sure you tell Father! I’m going to cause trouble! Please make sure to tell him there’ll be a huge uproar!”

Hwi was probably the only person who could enter the place where his father was undergoing closed-door cultivation.

Of course, that did not mean these words would be relayed. Unless war broke out, if it was not urgent news on that level, nothing would be allowed to disturb his father’s cultivation.

Sima Myeong began his report in earnest.

“First, I will report the major incidents and accidents in the Central Plains.”

Only the most important events among the many incidents that had occurred all over the Central Plains were reported.

Beginning with information related to the movements of the Martial Alliance and the Evil Dao Alliance, the trends of the great sects, even the flow of the merchant world—every kind of information was reported.

How and where the experts of the Martial Alliance and the Evil Dao Alliance were moving. Which sects had disputes with one another. Which merchant group and which sect had joined hands.

Once he had listened to all those reports, matters requiring decisions awaited him next.

“Next are the matters requiring your approval. First, it seems we must increase the forces at the Henan branch. This is in response to the Martial Alliance’s Henan branch increasing its personnel.”

“How many did the Martial Alliance increase by?”

“Ten.”

Henan was the sphere of influence of the righteous faction. For that reason, both their cult and the Evil Dao Alliance operated their branches and divisions there with relatively small numbers. They acknowledged each other’s spheres of influence.

“Is there any need to respond to a change of only ten people? Wouldn’t we needlessly provoke the Martial Alliance?”

He wondered if they were concerned because Henan was far from the main cult.

But Sima Myeong gave an unexpected answer.

“On the contrary, remaining still would provoke them.”

“What do you mean?”

“This response is closer to a kind of conversation. We are letting them know that we are watching their movements as well. You increased by ten—we know that very well. Therefore, we will increase by this much too. But if we show no reaction, that becomes a danger signal instead. It would mean that the cult’s internal intelligence network is not functioning properly.”

“Then isn’t that good news for them, not a danger signal?”

“No. They do not want the cult to change.”

He had not known that the Tongcheon Pavilion paid attention to such delicate matters.

Was it because this was his very first decision on his first day? Sima Myeong left that decision to Geom Mugeuk.

“How many shall we send?”

He was about to say, Let’s just send five.

“How about sending around a thousand and giving them a shock?”

“As you wish.”

As if he truly intended to do so, Sima Myeong picked up the next document, and Geom Mugeuk quickly spoke.

“I was joking!”

How could Sima Myeong not know it was a joke?

“What a shame. It was a golden opportunity to station a thousand men at the Henan branch.”

On the other hand, Geom Mugeuk knew very well that his words were not merely a joke.

“Do you wish for it too, Chief Strategist?”

“Wish for what?”

“My father’s unification of the martial world.”

“Why do you ask that all of a sudden?”

“Because I thought you would answer most honestly if I asked while sitting here.”

After a brief pause, Sima Myeong answered.

“I wish to see the Cult Leader’s jianghu.”

It meant that he wanted his father to unify the martial world.

Perhaps Sima Myeong, more than anyone else, wanted to aid his father in achieving the unification of all under heaven. That very deed would be the greatest achievement he could attain as a strategist.

Geom Mugeuk did not pry any deeper. It was not a topic to ask about while looking down from above.

“Now, what’s next?”

* * *

Was it because he was doing something he had never done before in his life?

Time passed in an instant.

“Time is up. Let us stop here for today.”

It seemed even the work handled with Sima Myeong had a set time.

“There’s still a lot left, though?”

They had not even finished a third of the work he had brought.

“You may continue tomorrow.”

“When you do this with Father, do you finish it all within the time?”

“Usually.”

Geom Mugeuk rose from the Taishi chair and said,

“Now I understand. I know why Father stayed glued to the Taishi chair like that. Even if he wanted to run away, there was too much work for him to do so.”

He had vaguely known that his father had a lot of work, but he had not known he handled this much.

Truly, they were matters that could not be attended to one by one without a tremendous sense of responsibility.

“For your first day, you did well.”

While working together today, Sima Myeong had seen a new side of Geom Mugeuk.

“You are far more meticulous than I expected.”

When Geom Mugeuk did not know something, he asked immediately, and he did not handle matters carelessly. He considered them twice, even three times.

“It’s not because I’m meticulous, but because I have anxiety. What if I make the wrong decision and things go wrong? What if Father or the Chief Strategist is disappointed?”

Sima Myeong gazed at Geom Mugeuk for a moment.

“The greater the anxiety, the greater the results tend to be. Though, of course, it is difficult for the person in question. Then please rest.”

After bowing respectfully, Sima Myeong left the Heavenly Demon Hall.

There was still time left before the day’s work ended, so after Sima Myeong left like this, his father must have remained here alone, keeping this seat.

‘What did you think about while sitting here? What did you think about the most?’

He had not realized it properly when his father was here.

Instead, he felt that his father’s absence had become an opportunity to understand him more deeply.

‘Still, I miss you, Father.’

Chapter 610: The Heavenly Demon Is Overworked
---------------------------------------------
At the sound of a distant bell, Geom Mugeuk rose from the armchair.

“Ahh! It’s finally over!”

It was his first day as acting Cult Leader.

Never before had a day felt so long. He would rather train or fight until he was covered in blood.

As he descended the stairs and tried to leave the Heavenly Demon Hall, Hwi dropped his concealment and revealed himself.

“Uncle.”

“From now on, you must address me as Commander Hwi.”

“When it’s just the two of us, I’ll call you Uncle.”

Hwi’s gaze as he looked at Geom Mugeuk was gentle. After the three of them had traveled together with his father, Hwi had become incomparably closer to him than before. They had also come to know much more about each other.

“I have prepared quarters for you in the inner garden of the Heavenly Demon Hall. You will have to stay there for the time being.”

Since it was the position of acting in place of the Cult Leader, treatment befitting a Cult Leader and guards naturally followed.

Walking through the inner garden following Hwi, he cautiously asked.

“Is Father alright?”

“He is fine.”

“That’s a relief.”

Geom Mugeuk had worried that there might be another reason for this seclusion. Fortunately, he could find no trace of worry from Hwi. The person from whom he could get the most accurate answer regarding his father would be Hwi.

“This is the room.”

The place Hwi guided him to was the room across from his father’s quarters.

“Isn’t this also a room that Father uses?”

“That is correct. He used to read books and contemplate here. He instructed that this room be given to the Young Lord.”

Though it was not as large as the room where his father slept, it was elegantly yet luxuriously decorated. The bookshelves and tea tables looked ordinary on the outside, but they were all crafted by master artisans.

This room also had a large bed, with clean, fluffy bedding laid upon it. Sitting on the edge of the bed, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“Now I understand why Father goes to sleep so early. With how much he worries and works during the day, how could sleep not come?”

The one who had watched that sight most closely, Hwi, was smiling before his eyes.

“I shall take my leave now. If you need anything, please call for me.”

“What I need is Father! Please, go and bring him here for me.”

Just as he was jokingly seeing Hwi out, something caught Geom Mugeuk’s eye.

“Huh? What’s this!”

Startled, Geom Mugeuk walked over to the display cabinet.

Something was placed on the display cabinet set to one side.

Surprisingly, they were the Demon Lord dolls sold in Magachon.

All Eight Demon Lords were there, and among them was a doll of Geom Mugeuk himself.

When Hanseol had visited the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult some time ago, a merchant selling dolls in Magachon had mentioned that a Young Lord doll would soon be released.

It seemed the dolls had come out while he was away handling matters at the Hwangnyong Martial Hall.

Geom Mugeuk was not surprised that a doll of him had come out.

“Why is this here?”

Demon Lord dolls in Father’s room!

Hwi simply gave a faint smile.

“Don’t tell me. Did Father buy these?”

Surprisingly, Hwi nodded his head.

“Before entering seclusion, the Cult Leader had once had a drink at a tavern in Magachon. On the way back that day, he saw the Young Lord doll and bought all of them.”

It meant that he had shown no interest until now but bought them upon seeing his son’s doll released. He must have bought all the Demon Lords’ dolls as well because it would have been awkward to buy just one.

The image of that day came to mind. His father, having had a drink at the tavern and returning with his face flushed red with intoxication, buying the dolls.

It was touching enough that he bought them, but it was even more moving that he had displayed them like this.

“Please wait here for a moment.”

Geom Mugeuk left the room.

Shortly after, Geom Mugeuk returned with something. What he had gone to buy in an instant using Rapid Step was the Heavenly Demon doll. There had been no doll of his father among the displayed ones.

He moved his own doll standing in the center to the side and placed his father’s doll in the middle.

“Now it’s finally complete.”

Watching that sight, Hwi’s smile deepened.

Originally, these dolls should have been put away before giving the room to Geom Mugeuk. The Cult Leader was not someone who wanted to show such things to his son.

But Hwi had deliberately not put them away. Because he wanted to let Geom Mugeuk know the Cult Leader’s heart. The Cult Leader he served was someone who, unless he did something like this, would never express his feelings to his son.

“Then please rest well.”

As he stepped out of the room and closed the door, he saw the sight of Geom Mugeuk gazing at the dolls.

It was good that he hadn’t put them away.

* * *

The next morning, Geom Mugeuk had Hwi gather the guards.

“Gather all the Heavenly Demon Hall guard squads. All of the Young Lord’s guards at my quarters as well.”

Hwi was inwardly nervous. It was not anything else, but a request to call all the guard squads.

“Understood.”

Hwi obeyed the order without asking the reason.

Shortly after, the guard warriors all gathered in the rear garden of the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Though there were many guard squads in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, those with the best skill were naturally the Heavenly Demon Hall guard squads.

In particular, the squad leaders who protected the Cult Leader inside the Heavenly Demon Hall alongside Hwi possessed tremendous skill. Though their positions were squad leaders, they were experts of pavilion lord level or higher.

There, the twelve guards including Jeogyeon, the captain of the Young Lord’s guard, were also called forth.

Since only the guards protecting the Cult Leader could enter and exit the Heavenly Demon Hall, they were extremely nervous. Needless to say about Commander Hwi, but the other guards were all great seniors to them as well.

Geom Mugeuk greeted them warmly first.

“Hey, my guards! It’s been so long I was about to forget your faces.”

“We greet the Young Lord!”

They all clasped their fists and shouted in booming voices.

Geom Mugeuk approached Jeogyeon.

“You’ve got a line of women following you around, haven’t you?”

Jeogyeon, who had worn an eyepatch because he felt awkward despite having achieved great success in the Ghost Eye Technique, was now without the eyepatch.

“No, sir!”

It had been so long since he had gone out to the streets claiming to train in martial arts that he couldn’t even remember!

Had they been alone, he would have given that kind of answer.

He could not give such an answer in a place where his seniors were watching.

Jeogyeon felt ashamed before Hwi and the seniors. Because they had been failing to properly carry out their duties.

Of course, everyone knew that Geom Mugeuk was inherently free-spirited and liked to go around alone, but nevertheless, it was true that they could not properly fulfill their duties.

“You’ve all trained hard in the meantime.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell from their stances alone how hard they had worked.

In fact, they had devoted themselves to martial arts training while waiting for Geom Mugeuk. Thanks to that, they had become much stronger than when they first met him.

Having gathered all the guards like this, Geom Mugeuk looked over everyone with a calm yet dignified gaze.

“Commander Hwi, I have a request.”

“Please speak.”

“During my time as acting Cult Leader, I will have my own guards protect me.”

At the unexpected words, Hwi as well as the existing Heavenly Demon guards were bewildered.

The Young Lord’s guards were, after all, the most junior class; yet he was saying he would entrust his protection to them.

“I wish to give them a chance to accumulate experience through this opportunity.”

Hwi, contemplating for a moment, answered politely.

“Understood. It shall be done.”

The guards including Jeogyeon were all delighted. They had finally been entrusted with properly guarding Geom Mugeuk. And it was a mission inside the Heavenly Demon Hall at that.

That was not the only reason Geom Mugeuk had gathered them today.

“I have one more request.”

“Please speak.”

“Except for my guards and Commander Hwi, I am granting leave to all the remaining guards.”

At the unexpected words, they were even more bewildered this time.

“This is also the first order I am issuing as acting Cult Leader.”

He hammered it in as an outright order. In truth, the person he most wanted to give leave to was Hwi, but he excluded him because he knew Hwi would never accept the order otherwise.

Hwi could understand. That Geom Mugeuk was trying to give the guards a chance to rest through this opportunity.

“I have the authority to refuse orders that conflict with guard duties.”

That was the greatest authority given to the Commander of the Guards.

“I am aware.”

“May I ask the reason for this?”
