Hwi had guessed the reason, but he deliberately asked so that his subordinates could know what Geom Mugeuk was thinking.

“Because opportunities like this do not come often.”

Even when the three of them had traveled together last time, the guards had been unable to rest. Though they did not move together openly, they had to move constantly, coordinating with Hwi and waiting in unseen places.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to the guards.

“Rest well while Father is away. Visit your hometowns, go on trips with your families. Catch up on sleep.”

Who could have imagined that the Young Lord would grant them leave? Everyone was bewildered, not knowing how to react.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned back to Hwi.

“Will you refuse?”

Had he been excluded as well, he would have refused. But he could not deny that this was a golden opportunity to let his subordinates rest.

“No. I gratefully accept the command you have given.”

As soon as the leave was decided, uncontainable joy flashed across the guards’ faces. Of course, it wasn’t as if they worked without a single day’s break. They had taken turns resting in shifts, but a proper leave like this was something they couldn’t even dream of.

“Here, take these and share them.”

Geom Mugeuk gave them the envelopes he had prepared. Inside was enough money to fully enjoy their leave. Regardless of the amount, they were moved even more deeply by his heart.

“Everyone, rest well and return.”

“Thank you!”

Their booming answers were filled with gratitude.

The Heavenly Demon Hall guards all withdrew.

Hwi went out with them. Given Hwi’s personality, he would not send them all off blindly. Adjusting schedules, checking emergency communication networks. He would have much to do.

Those remaining there now were Jeogyeon and the guards.

“Are you disappointed you can’t go on leave?”

At that, Jeogyeon answered on behalf of everyone.

“All this time, we’ve been disappointed that we couldn’t do our duty!”

Because the seniors were not present, they could be honest.

“You can protect me well, right?”

“Yes!”

“I’m counting on you alone.”

Not only Jeogyeon, but the other guards’ eyes blazed as well.

Someday these men would grow just like the seniors who preceded them. Just as Hwi was by his father’s side, this Jeogyeon would be by his side.

“Jeogyeon!”

“Yes!”

He had called out in a grave voice, but the business at hand was something that flustered Jeogyeon.

“You’ve taken off the eyepatch too, so shall we go pick up some women?”

Jeogyeon answered, his face reddening.

“We cannot.”

Geom Mugeuk slumped his shoulders and walked off, sighing deeply.

“Right, we can’t. The work from yesterday is still piled up like a mountain, after all.”

* * *

Sama Myeong had piled another stack of documents high beside the ones left from yesterday.

“Don’t tell me these are the tasks that need to be handled today?”

“Yes, that is correct.”

At Sama Myeong’s answer, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“You’re telling me all this happened in one day? No, right? You made all this up and brought it here, didn’t you?”

Sama Myeong took Geom Mugeuk’s joke as a joke.

“Yes, I couldn’t sleep a wink all night making them up.”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“How can so many things happen in a single day!”

The Tongcheon Pavilion strategists must have categorized information flying in from all over the Central Plains until late hours. Sama Myeong, too, must have been unable to sleep until late.

“So there were people you could send on leave after all.”

He didn’t ask what he meant; it seemed he already knew about the guards being sent on leave.

“If we leave our posts, the main cult’s affairs will be paralyzed.”

“So what?”

“Pardon?”

“It would be paralyzed for about ten days. Send an official letter to the Murim Alliance and the Heretic Alliance. ‘We’ll be resting for ten days, so absolutely do not touch us! Touch us and there will be war in ten days!’”

Sama Myeong smiled at the remark that they should declare war after finishing their vacation.

“We are taking turns resting well, so please do not worry too much.”

But Geom Mugeuk knew. That Sama Myeong himself was working without a single day of rest.

“I’m not worried about the young strategists. It’s the Chief Strategist I’m worried about.”

“I am fine. There is a mental technique the Cult Leader taught me long ago. I perform it once after waking and once before sleeping. Thanks to that, I have become very healthy.”

Indeed, his father must have been very concerned about Sama Myeong’s health as well. Sama Myeong was someone absolutely indispensable to the main cult.

Yes, the best way to care for Sama Myeong was to finish the work as quickly as possible.

“Come, let’s pick up the pace today.”

Certainly, the work processing speed had increased compared to yesterday.

While working together, Sama Myeong discovered one great strength in Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk asked questions well. He was not ashamed of what he did not know. He strove hard to remember what he had learned through asking.

Because Sama Myeong was a strategist, he regarded this point as an even greater strength.

He knew from experience that when one pretended to know what one did not, that pretense sometimes returned as a great disaster.

That was why he always told his subordinates. Do not pretend to know what you do not. It is fine to go slowly, it is fine to look ignorant, but absolutely do not pretend to know what you do not.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk, who was passing over documents, asked Sama Myeong.

“But did Father decide even this kind of matter?”

“Yes, he did.”

“The Cult Leader used to say that if you do not properly understand the small details within the cult, you cannot decide great affairs.”

He could hardly believe that his father had even said this. The more he worked, the more he learned new sides of his father.

“My father must be the most misunderstood person in the world. Even by his own son.”

When martial artists thought of the Heavenly Demon, they imagined him indulging in all manner of pleasures and revelry, enjoying blood-soaked banquets, but his father had been silently working for the cult. Even amid that, he had looked after the Demon Lords in his own way.

“The Heavenly Demon was overworked.”

If he had known, he would have let him win at fishing.

Chapter 611: Aren’t They Making More Money Than Us?
---------------------------------------------------
Several days passed that way.

Geom Mugeuk devoted himself to the duties of acting Cult Leader. In the past, once he finished his daily work, he would have gone to the Pungryu Tavern for a drink, then met with the Demon Venerables and fooled around.

This time, he did not. When he thought that his father had been struggling far more than he had imagined, that he must have been carrying so many concerns, he could not bring himself to play. The work entrusted to him was, after all, an immensely important duty.

And today, at last!

“What’s the next matter?”

“There is none.”

When Geom Mugeuk raised his head, Sima Myeong was staring at him in surprise.

“You finished all the work on time.”

He had known from long ago that the Young Cult Leader was a brilliant man, but he had not expected him to adapt to the work this quickly. It was not merely that he was intelligent. The Young Cult Leader knew how to see through people’s minds, and he understood precisely how an organization functioned.

“As expected, blood will tell.”

The Young Cult Leader’s deep insight truly made it feel as though he were working alongside the Cult Leader himself.

Until now, Sima Myeong had mostly seen the results the Young Cult Leader had achieved. But now, he personally felt how those results had come about.

“Impressive.”

“It’s not because I’m impressive. It’s just my will to get rid of this accursed work as quickly as possible coming into play.”

“If that is all it is, then you handled it far too well. Then I will see you tomorrow.”

Sima Myeong gathered the documents and was about to turn away.

“There’s one thing I’d like to ask.”

“Please ask.”

“The Demon Venerables all know I’m back, don’t they?”

“Not only them. The entire cult knows.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk could not hold back and shouted.

“And yet not a single one of them came to see me? Isn’t that too much!”

Whenever he left after finishing work, he had expected to find the Blood Heaven Saber Demon at the entrance of the Heavenly Demon Hall, his great saber thrust into the ground as he drank.

He had hoped the Extreme Evil Young Demon would be standing atop the giant evil spirit statue, looking down at him and laughing.

He had thought the Drunken Demon would come reeking of liquor and tease him, asking if the work was hard, whether this elder brother should help.

“And no one came!”

Sima Myeong consoled Geom Mugeuk in a gentle tone.

“Even originally, they are not the sort to come unless the Cult Leader summons them. They are people far removed from the Heavenly Demon Hall.”

“That’s because Father is difficult to approach, so of course they wouldn’t. How can Father and I be the same?”

At that, Sima Myeong asked meaningfully,

“Are you different?”

Geom Mugeuk asked Sima Myeong with a startled expression.

“Then are we the same? Am I not far easier to deal with than Father?”

Surprisingly, Sima Myeong thought the opposite.

“Would it not be more difficult for the Demon Venerables to deal with the Young Cult Leader than with the Cult Leader?”

“Me?”

Beyond surprise, Geom Mugeuk looked utterly dumbfounded.

It was something he had truly never imagined. Naturally, he wondered if it was a joke, but judging by Sima Myeong’s serious expression, he seemed to mean it.

“Did you not see them on the day I returned to the cult? Lord Fist Demon and Lord Ilhwa Sword Venerable walked out without even hearing me to the end. I’m probably the most easygoing Young Cult Leader in the history of our cult. And yet you say I’m difficult?”

Sima Myeong did not agree with Geom Mugeuk.

“I believe they find you more difficult.”

“Even though they play so many pranks and joke around so much?”

The Demon Venerables find me more difficult than Father? Impossible!

Then, with a sudden suspicion, he asked,

“What about you, Strategist?”

Instead of answering, Sima Myeong gave an inscrutable smile, bowed politely, and turned away.

“I will see you tomorrow.”

“No, you should answer before you go!”

But Sima Myeong simply left.

“You ignore me like this, and yet I’m difficult?”

Left alone, Geom Mugeuk spoke into the air.

“Jeogyeon, what do you think? Am I difficult for you too?”

Then an answer came from the empty air.

“…You are difficult, but not as difficult as the Cult Leader.”

“Right?”

“Yes.”

Then why had that clever Sima Myeong said such a thing?

After remaining seated for a moment, Geom Mugeuk sprang up from the grand preceptor’s chair.

“Where are you going?”

“I have to confirm whether that’s really true.”

“Which Demon Venerable are you going to see?”

He naturally thought Geom Mugeuk would go looking for one of the Demon Venerables.

“I’m not going to a Demon Venerable.”

“Pardon?”

Geom Mugeuk strode down the Path of Blood and said,

“There’s someone who won’t mind me and will tell me the truth.”

* * *

A dozen or so boxes were lined up on the floor.

Inside those boxes were different hidden weapons, some containing only three or four, others containing more than ten.

The person who walked up before them was Geom Mugeuk’s elder brother, Geom Muyang.

“Are these the ones?”

“Yes. These are the counterfeit hidden weapons recovered this time.”

Geom Muyang held several important responsibilities within the cult, and one of them was managing the weapons produced by the Iron Workshop.

His main duty was to send the arms forged in the Iron Workshop to branch altars and branch offices in various regions.

But recently, a new matter had arisen that required his attention.

It was identifying the counterfeit weapons being manufactured all across the Central Plains.

Various weapons made by the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult were being counterfeited and sold through the martial world’s black markets.

Among the counterfeit weapons, hidden weapons made up by far the greatest number.

In particular, various hidden weapons launched through mechanisms had become the main targets of counterfeiting. That was because they sold for high prices.

The mere fact that they were hidden weapons used by demonic practitioners gave these fakes value. Even though possessing them could lead to being accused of being a demonic practitioner, many people sought the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult’s hidden weapons.

And recently, the number of those counterfeit hidden weapons had sharply increased.

Geom Muyang picked up a hidden weapon from the first box. It was cylindrical.

“It is Salmang.”

Listening to his subordinate’s explanation, Geom Muyang fired it at the wall.

Puaaak!

Originally, it was a hidden weapon that sent dozens of projectiles flying like a net, sweeping the area ahead.

But only a dozen or so flew out and embedded themselves in the wall.

“Crude.”

Counterfeit hidden weapons were originally made in this crude fashion. If the makers had the skill to craft them identically, they would not have been making and selling counterfeit hidden weapons in the first place.

But as the days passed, hidden weapons that imitated the originals rather convincingly were being produced.

“They are mainly being distributed in Hunan, and we are tracking down the maker.”

Even though they knew they would die if caught, counterfeits continued to appear because the profits were enormous.

Judging by the quantity spreading through the Central Plains, it was clear they were not being made by an individual, but by an organization.

“The next item came in from Hubei.”

Geom Muyang picked up the hidden weapon in the next box and launched it at the wall.

Shuuuk!

Paaak!

“This one has become stronger than the one recovered last time. Did they improve it in the meantime?”

“It seems so. Their technique is advancing more and more.”

When Geom Muyang picked up a hidden weapon from the next box, the subordinate who had been explaining the weapons bowed his head politely to someone.

Geom Mugeuk was walking toward them.

“If you fire that this way, it won’t be an assassination attempt on the Young Cult Leader, but an assassination attempt on the acting Cult Leader. The crime will be at least twice as severe, wouldn’t it?”

Of course, despite those words, Geom Muyang fired it anyway.

Shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk!

Papapapapapak!

The hidden weapons flew and embedded themselves in the wall beside where Geom Mugeuk was walking. Yes, this was how his brother should be.

“Long time no see for me too, Brother.”

At the sight of the two of them, the subordinate beside them withdrew far away on his own.

Looking at the hidden weapons lodged in the wall, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Are you testing the power of hidden weapons?”

“They’re counterfeit hidden weapons modeled after our cult’s.”

Geom Muyang tossed the one he was holding to Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk caught it and examined it.

“Fake? It looks pretty convincing.”

“They are becoming more refined by the day.”

“How much do things like this sell for?”

“I hear they go for anywhere from dozens to hundreds of nyang.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk flared up.

“Don’t tell me these bastards are earning more money than us?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, the corner of Geom Muyang’s mouth rose slightly. He bent down, picked up a hidden weapon from the next box, and asked,

“What brings you here?”

His tone was still blunt, but Geom Mugeuk could tell. The look in his eyes as he gazed at him was softer than before.

“I came to see you, Brother.”

“Why?”

“Because I missed you.”

Geom Muyang snorted and aimed the hidden weapon again.

“Move. I’m busy.”

“Could you have been as busy as me?”

Geom Mugeuk sighed and said,

“If I’d known there was this much work, I wouldn’t have fought you over being the successor. I really would have yielded this position to the person I hated most. Ah, back then, that would have been you.”

At those words, Geom Muyang finally let out a small laugh.

Shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk-shuk!

Pak-pak-pak-pak-pak!

The hidden weapons flew again, grazing Geom Mugeuk’s shoulder before embedding themselves in the wall.

“Enough, enough! Stop pretending to be busy and talk to me.”

Geom Mugeuk stood in front of the wall and waved both hands from side to side.

“Talk about what?”

“Father has entered closed-door training, and the younger brother you haven’t seen in ages has become acting Cult Leader. If you have nothing to say even in this situation, why do you even carry that mouth around?”

Geom Muyang picked up a hidden weapon from the next box.

“Father came to see me the day before he entered closed-door training.”

“What did he say?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him expectantly, but—

“He told me to keep a close watch on whether you caused trouble or not.”

Geom Mugeuk raised his voice.

“He said that to you too? Father, that is too much! Just what do you think of this son of yours?”

Geom Mugeuk’s resentment changed direction.

“If you heard something like that, you should have come to help your younger brother.”

“As you can see, I have a lot of work too.”

In truth, Geom Muyang had gone to see Sima Myeong.

He had gone to ask if there was anything he could do to help Geom Mugeuk, but when he heard that Geom Mugeuk was adapting to the work more and more with each passing day, he had simply returned. He thought that not helping might actually be more helpful to his younger brother.

How could Geom Muyang not know? If their father had truly been worried that Geom Mugeuk would cause trouble, he would never have chosen him as successor in the first place. This was successor training.

Their father had come to him not because of Geom Mugeuk, but for his sake. Because he felt sorry toward his eldest son.

“Father, I am all right.”

Geom Mugeuk, by becoming the successor, was coming to understand their father more, and Geom Muyang, by not becoming the successor, was coming to understand their father more.

“What is there about you that’s so lovable that I should help?”

“Everyone is too much.”

“Everyone?”

“I heard something absurd from Strategist Sima today. He said the Demon Venerables might find me more difficult to deal with than Father.”

Geom Muyang let out a low hum.

“What kind of reaction is that?”

Geom Mugeuk asked with a doubtful expression.

“Don’t tell me you think so too?”

“To me, you’re a joke.”

“Right? That’s the proper reaction.”

Then Geom Muyang said something unexpected.

“But if it’s the Demon Venerables, they may indeed find you more difficult.”

Geom Mugeuk was startled and asked his brother again.

“You’re saying the Demon Venerables find me harder to deal with than Father? Are you serious?”

Geom Muyang nodded.

“Because you stir people’s hearts and grab them by the collar. They want to stay still, but when they’re with you, they have to be dragged onto the stage.”

That was how Geom Muyang saw Geom Mugeuk.

“When they’re with you, they feel the pressure that they have to do something well.”

Geom Muyang thought the Demon Venerables would be no different from him.

Of course, to him, their father was naturally more difficult. He had truly wanted to look good in their father’s eyes, and that feeling had not greatly changed even now.

But to the Demon Venerables, both his father and his younger brother were others. The way they saw the two of them would certainly be different from how he did.

“Father is someone who can be predicted, but you are unpredictable.”

Geom Muyang aimed the hidden weapon he was holding at Geom Mugeuk.

“I’ve said everything I had to say, so move now.”

Standing in front of the wall, Geom Mugeuk expressed his gratitude.

“Thank you. If not for you, I wouldn’t have been able to hear that from the Demon Venerables. You have no idea how precious you are to me, do you? I’ll make sure they produce a doll of you at Maga Village too. Of course, it won’t come out looking cooler than my doll, but…”

“Ugh, just get hit!”

Swaeeeeek!

Papapapapapak!

Geom Mugeuk’s scream burst out.

Geom Muyang, who had fired the hidden weapon, was startled.

The hidden weapon he had just fired had flown with truly tremendous force.

“Are you all right?”

At Geom Muyang’s shout, Geom Mugeuk, who had been awkwardly leaning against the wall, slid down and sat.

“Good grief, that scared me.”

Above his head, steel needles were embedded in the wall. He had truly dodged by a hair’s breadth. Since the distance was close and the force was tremendous, anyone other than Geom Mugeuk would have been struck. Truly, Geom Muyang, the one who had fired it, was even more shocked.

“You planned properly to kill me!”

With a dazed expression, Geom Muyang looked down at the hidden weapon he was holding. He had been absorbed in conversation with his younger brother and had fired it without even looking at what it was. No, even if he had looked, it would have been the same. There was no way his younger brother would be hit by a fake hidden weapon.

Jingyeok.

It was one of the hidden weapons that represented the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Its power was enough to pierce straight through shields and armor, so unless one was a fairly skilled expert, it was not easy to block.

The Murim Alliance had designated Jingyeok as one of the Ten Great Forbidden Hidden Weapons. Its overwhelming power was one thing, but the steel needles of Jingyeok were coated in deadly poison.

Therefore, even the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was extremely cautious in using Jingyeok. Because poison and hidden weapons were among the greatest causes of dispute in the martial world, Jingyeok was forbidden from use even within the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult unless in wartime.

Chiiiiik.

The poison was so strong that the wall foamed and melted. The exposed steel needles glimmered with a bluish light.

“It’s even coated with deadly poison? This looks real.”

“That can’t be.”

Geom Muyang shook his head with a grave expression.

Because Jingyeok was one of the Ten Great Forbidden Hidden Weapons, it was strictly managed.

Geom Mugeuk walked over to Geom Muyang.

“It’s fake, yet it has this kind of power?”

Geom Mugeuk took Jingyeok from Geom Muyang and examined it. It was far too exquisitely made to be called a fake.

“Rather than find and kill the person who made this, shouldn’t we hire them?”

Chapter 612: An Antidote That Does Not Exist
--------------------------------------------
“Where did you get this?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geom Muyang called over the subordinate who had withdrawn far away.

“Where did you acquire this counterfeit Jingyeok?”

At Geom Muyang’s question, the subordinate answered.

“There was intelligence. We obtained information that the Heuksu Faction, an unorthodox sect, was secretly procuring a Jingyeok, and this item was recovered.”

“The ones who sold it?”

“They sent the goods through an escort agency.”

Meaning they had used an escort agency to send the item without revealing their identities.

“Then what of the payment?”

“They said they would come to collect the money after the item was received.”

At those words, Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang looked at each other. They were no ordinary people. Intending to collect payment after delivery meant they had confidence they could collect.

“Collect the steel needles embedded there and identify what poison was applied to them!”

“Yes! I will send them to Cheondok Forest at once.”

Geom Muyang picked up the Jingyeok and moved to leave.

“Where are you going?”

“To the Iron Forge. Master Gwak might know who made it.”

“I’m coming with you.”

Geom Mugeuk followed after his older brother.

“Walk slowly!”

Whether because his heart was restless, Geom Muyang’s steps were hurried.

His expression was stiff. Not because a fake hidden weapon indistinguishable from the real one had appeared.

His heart pounded belatedly at the fact that he had nearly killed Geom Mugeuk. Surprise led to anger.

Hire the ones who made this? Not a chance. His younger brother had nearly died. He didn’t know who they were, but he wouldn’t let them be.

It was an unfamiliar emotion. To be this angry at the fact that his younger brother had nearly died.

There had been a time when he had wished for his younger brother’s death.

He thought that human emotions were truly like the wind. What emotion would he feel at the end of this wind?

Thus the two entered the Iron Forge.

With scorching heat came the sound of steel being hammered.

Kang! Kang! Kkang!

Inside, hundreds of men stripped to the waist were at work.

Smelting molten iron, hammering steel, carrying goods.

The smell of their sweat and the scent of iron rode on the scorching heat in waves.

The man at the entrance recognized Geom Mugeuk and respectfully bowed his head.

“We have come to see Master Gwak.”

“Please wait a moment.”

While the man went to call Master Gwak, Geom Mugeuk waited there with his older brother.

“I won’t die to such a shallow scheme.”

Next time, I’ll kill you properly.

It was a joke expecting such a reaction, but Geom Muyang’s stiff expression did not ease. Geom Mugeuk could feel that his older brother was truly surprised and angry.

He was grateful to his older brother. Because it let him know that nearly killing him was such a big deal to his brother.

And one more lesson.

He always had to be careful. Because accidents like the one from before burst forth when you least expect them, when you are careless.

Because all of it happens in an instant, he had no choice but to live paying even more attention, even if life was exhausting.

Shortly after, Master Gwak came out.

“You have both come together.”

“We greet the Hall Master.”

After exchanging greetings, Geom Muyang handed the Jingyeok he had brought to him.

“A Jingyeok.”

Master Gwak received the Jingyeok. The organization that designed hidden weapons was separate within the sect; the Iron Forge here manufactured it according to the blueprints made there.

Master Gwak tilted his head, then examined the Jingyeok again.

“It’s a bit light.”

He immediately noticed the difference in weight. When Master Gwak inspected it closely, he discovered several places where it differed.

“It is a counterfeit Jingyeok.”

“That’s correct.”

“It has been crafted so elaborately that one couldn’t tell at a glance.”

Master Gwak called a subordinate to bring disassembly tools. Then he skillfully dismantled the Jingyeok and examined its interior.

Master Gwak, having looked inside, was even more surprised.

“The exterior as well as the interior have been reproduced almost perfectly. The power was likely almost the same as well.”

It meant that it had been made exceptionally well.

“That’s right. I nearly died.”

Then Master Gwak asked with a grave expression.

“Were the steel needles poisoned by any chance?”

“Yes, they were poisoned.”

“Preventing the inside of the hidden weapon from being damaged by poison is the core technique of the Jingyeok. This is almost believable as something we made ourselves. For someone with this level of skill to be making counterfeit hidden weapons?”

Master Gwak let out a sigh that could have been either lament or anger.

“Do you have any idea who it might be?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Master Gwak listed several people.

“There are a few who come to mind, but they are all people of great renown. Master Hong of the Martial Alliance, Master Ju of the Heretic Alliance, Master Yang of the Continental Iron Forge. However, they are not the type to make such counterfeits.”

They were all famous craftsmen. In other words, it meant the counterfeit had been made by someone possessing that level of skill.

“There could be someone unknown.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Master Gwak nodded. If the martial world had as many experts as grains of sand on a beach, the same was true for craftsmen. There would be those who could shock the world with innate dexterity, hammering away somewhere without yet knowing their true worth.

“If you ever have any suspects later, please let me know.”

“Understood.”

Thus, after parting with Master Gwak, Geom Mugeuk exited the Iron Forge with his older brother.

“How are you going to handle this?”

“I’m going to handle this matter personally. I’ll take care of it, so you don’t need to concern yourself.”

Geom Mugeuk’s older brother strode off. After walking some distance, he turned around and warned.

“Don’t even think of meddling needlessly!”

Because he had nearly killed Geom Mugeuk, he intended to handle this matter alone at least this time. Having nearly killed him and then receiving help as well? That didn’t suit Geom Muyang’s personality.

“I’m busy too. I wish I had the time to meddle.”

Geom Muyang started walking again.

Watching that retreating figure, Geom Mugeuk moved in the opposite direction. His steps were just as urgent.

* * *

“Absolutely not. You cannot go out, especially with the Eldest Young Master.”

When told that he would go out with his older brother and return after handling this matter, Sama Myeong firmly opposed it.

There was a clear reason for the opposition.

“It is because those who are to become successors cannot leave the sect together for such a matter. It is also written in the sect rules.”

It was to consider the case where both met with misfortune.

“Then the reason I have been able to move about freely until now is because my older brother was at the main sect.”

Sama Myeong made an expression as if to say, “Is that really why?” but did not deny it.

“But why do you wish to go out with the Eldest Young Master?”

“This is entirely due to my anxiety.”

Sama Myeong asked cautiously.

“Do you think they are behind this matter?”

“Because right now, my heart is trembling as if it has seen a monster.”

Geom Mugeuk had repeatedly escaped death intertwined with them. In a situation where he didn’t know where or how they would appear, he could not send his older brother out alone.

Of course, this matter might simply be the work of an organization that made and sold counterfeit hidden weapons.

“Can’t I go find out whether it’s a soft-shelled turtle or a pot lid?”

“You cannot.”

Persuading his father would have been easier, but persuading Sama Myeong seemed impossible.

“If you let me go, Father must have felt this way too.”

“Forgive me for saying so, but the Sect Leader did not worry about the Young Sect Leader at all.”

Given his father’s disposition, he simply hadn’t shown it; how could he not have been worried?

In the past, he would have secretly escaped, but now he couldn’t.

Sama Myeong offered a solution. For how could he possibly neglect the Eldest Young Master?

“Send the Demon Troops along with him.”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk had been thinking the same thing. But there was a problem.

“My brother will refuse. He even warned me not to get involved.”

“So you will not send them?”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“That won’t do. I will send the Demon Troops.”

“If you give the order, any of the Demon Troops will go.”

Geom Mugeuk had already decided whom to send.

“The person I am sending is someone who will not listen to my orders.”

* * *

The place Geom Mugeuk arrived at was Cheondok Forest.

The Poison King was in the forest near his dwelling. Standing alone with his hands behind his back, he was looking at a tree.

To others he might look lonely, but Geom Mugeuk knew that to him, it was an incomparably happy time.

“Poison King! I’m here!”

Geom Mugeuk shouted cheerfully. The last time he had returned, he had failed to see the Poison King and left the sect immediately. Thus, this reunion was quite a long time coming.

“Shh!”

The Poison King signaled for quiet, then looked back at the tree.

Geom Mugeuk quietly approached and stood side by side with the Poison King.

Two venomous bugs were stacked on top of each other on the tree. They appeared to be mating.

Geom Mugeuk turned his head to look at the Poison King. He still looked like a beautiful youth.

‘You’re wasting that handsome face. Isn’t the Poison King the one who should go out into the world and find a woman to be his lover?’

But to the Poison King, the increase in the number of venomous bugs in Cheondok Forest seemed more enjoyable than such pleasures.

“On the way here, Sangseon told me. The poison applied to the hidden weapon is called Hwageolsindok.”

According to Sangseon, Hwageolsindok was a poison developed to apply Hwageolsan to hidden weapons. Made by blending various poisons with Hwageolsan as the main ingredient, it was a terrifying poison that melted the struck area and everything inside in an instant upon impact.

As much as its power, Hwageolsindok was also extremely difficult to handle. Because it had to reveal its effects only when discharged externally, without melting the hidden weapon inside the weapon chamber.

The Poison King still stared silently at the venomous bugs without a word.

“This time, my older brother is leaving the sect personally to investigate the counterfeit hidden weapons. I would like the Poison King to secretly follow him.”

That was why this request was even more difficult. It was a request to secretly guard him, not to go together.

“Because those who use Hwageolsindok might use other poisons as well.”

Thinking he wasn’t listening, the Poison King refused outright.

“No.”

“The opponent might use poison!”

“Tell Sangseon to bring an antidote.”

He hadn’t thought the Poison King would readily leave. He was someone who truly hated going outside Cheondok Forest.

“Now, now. I came prepared, knowing this would happen.”

Geom Mugeuk cleared his throat and spoke in a grave voice.

“As Acting Sect Leader, I command it. Poison King, obey the order of this Sect Leader!”

The Poison King turned toward Geom Mugeuk. For a moment, it seemed he would politely receive the command.

“But I don’t want to?”

Whish. A breeze swept past between the two.

“It is a Sect Leader’s order.”

“You’re only Acting Sect Leader.”

“It’s treason, treason!”

“Then arrest me and take me.”

The authority of the Acting Sect Leader didn’t work at all.

“You’re being too much. If Father told you to go, you would go, wouldn’t you?”

“But you’re not the Sect Leader.”

The Poison King’s gaze returned to the tree. The pair that had finished mating had already disappeared somewhere.

“Then I shall bring out the second hidden ace I prepared.”

A faint smile crossed the Poison King’s lips. This was why he could say, “But I don’t want to.” Because he knew Geom Mugeuk would flexibly move past it like this.

“This time, there is a reason you absolutely must go.”

“What reason?”

“I nearly died because of the Poison King.”

The Poison King turned his head back to Geom Mugeuk.

“Do you remember? The one who fled after being afflicted by your poison.”

He hadn’t yet told the Poison King that Tu Wang had come back alive. Because the last time he returned, he had to leave the sect again before meeting the Poison King.

“He came back alive.”

Surprise flashed across the Poison King’s face.

“That can’t be.”

He also knew well that Geom Mugeuk wouldn’t lie about such a thing.

“The impossible happened.”

A brief silence passed.

“What happened?”

“It seems they performed great techniques several times and barely saved him. Of course, even so, he wasn’t completely cured, so when we fought, a foul poisonous aura billowed out from him.”

It was a moment when a crack formed in the Poison King’s pride.

Naturally, Geom Mugeuk didn’t clumsily try to spare his pride.

“He came back stronger after surviving. Thanks to that, I nearly died. So this time, you must grant my request.”

Of course, he was forcing the matter, but it worked on the Poison King. After all, a man he had naturally been certain was dead had come back alive.

“You can’t trust me, so why are you trying to send me? Send out that Supreme Evil Little Demon you’re so fond of.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk answered.

“I think I’ll feel more at ease if you go, Poison King.”

“Are you wounding me and then applying the salve?”

“A mistake is a mistake, and skill is skill.”

The Poison King’s eyes gleamed sharply.

“The Supreme Evil Little Demon would be hurt if he heard that.”

“Soma-nim would understand. Not just Soma-nim, but the other Demon Venerables as well. None of the Demon Venerables would be able to deny what I just said.”

He had spoken indirectly, but in the end, this was what it meant.

The Poison King was the strongest.

Geom Mugeuk could feel the Poison King’s mood easing a little.

“Phew.”

The Poison King let out a sigh and sat down on a small rock beneath a tree.

“So they brought that bastard back to life?”

Geom Mugeuk sat down beside him.

“They say it cost an enormous sum of money. In the fight with him, I nearly died along with the Supreme Evil Little Demon. This time, too, even though Fist Demon-nim and Ilhwa Sword Venerable-nim went with them, there were dangerous moments. That is why you must go, Poison King. I cannot overlook even the slightest possibility.”

Still, the Poison King gave no answer. So Geom Mugeuk brought in another person.

“If you don’t feel like going alone, why not go with Demon Buddha-nim? While you’re out, you can dig up some rare poisonous herbs as well. Have you been gathering poisonous herbs with Demon Buddha-nim lately?”

The Poison King shook his head.

“He hasn’t come by at all lately. So things are awkward with him too.”

“What, you’ve already become awkward with him?”

Geom Mugeuk burst out laughing, and the Poison King, finding his own words funny after saying them, laughed along with him.

“You told me to guard him secretly, didn’t you? But do you think I can follow someone like Demon Buddha in secret?”

After all, that golden radiance and secrecy were very far apart.

“That’s why I came to ask you, Poison King.”

“I might just kill them all and come back.”

“As long as you bring my brother back alive, that’s enough.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke from the heart.

“You have no idea how much I want to go out with you, Poison King.”

And not to watch poisonous insects mating, but to see just how much fun I could have mingling with heroic ladies by your side, Poison King.

The Poison King, who had been staring at Geom Mugeuk, finally nodded.

“I’ll go.”

That was enough. Now he no longer worried about his brother.

“Then I’ll consider the debt repaid.”

“What debt?”

“The debt for nearly dying because that bastard came back alive.”

“How is that my fault!”

The words Geom Mugeuk had wanted finally came out. Yes, that was not the Poison King’s fault.

The more sensitive the matter, the more pride was involved, the more necessary it was to speak about it openly like this. To bring it up over and over, tease him, speak of it again, and tease him again.

Because trust with another person was something built up for moments like these.

That was why such matters had to end in bad feelings while they were still together. One could not gloss over them clumsily and let them linger in the mind after parting.

“Do you know this? The antidote that does not exist in this world is held by you alone, Poison King.”

Looking at the man beside him, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and added,

“You, Poison King, are the antidote to my anxiety.”

Chapter 613: Can You Handle the Awakened Cult Master?
-----------------------------------------------------
If the Poison King had been looking up at a tree, Mabul was looking up at a fiercely sculpted golden Buddha statue in the shrine.

At that moment, a composed greeting came from behind.

“It has been some time since we last met.”

As Geom Mugeuk entered the shrine, Mabul asked without turning around.

“What brings you here?”

“I came because I wished to see you, Lord Mabul. It has been quite a while since I last saw your face.”

Even seeing just that ill-natured back of Mabul’s head filled him with such joy; it seemed it truly had been some time.

“Have you been well?”

Mabul turned his back on the statue and faced Geom Mugeuk. Behind the diminutive Mabul, the massive golden Buddha statue made for a peculiar contrast.

Whenever Geom Mugeuk saw Mabul, he was struck by the sense that not everything was as it appeared. He knew well that Mabul, this small giant, was a far greater man than that golden Buddha statue.

“Is the position of temporary Cult Leader manageable?”

“Oh, please don’t even mention it.”

Geom Mugeuk waved his hands in dismissal, putting on a show of hardship.

“I had thought my father took little interest in internal cult affairs. That he would only decide on one or two important matters. But I was wrong. He oversaw every single troop movement.”

“The Cult Leader has overseen internal affairs more meticulously than any of his predecessors.”

Geom Mugeuk could see that Mabul was well-informed about his father’s activities. Naturally, given his deep interest in politics, he would be the one most knowledgeable about the situation in the Cult Leader’s quarters.

“Do you know why he is so concerned? Because he is meticulous? Not at all.”

“Then why is he doing this?”

Then, a surprising answer flowed from Mabul.

“For the unification of the martial world.”

“!”

“The Cult Leader believes that when one can firmly rule the cult, one can also rule the world.”

Perhaps he had once heard such words from his father.

His father had shown different sides to each of the Demon Lords. It was because of this that the facets of his father seen by the Demon Lords were all different.

“In the end, five years from now, you will stand at the vanguard of the martial world’s unification.”

He had been like this in the past as well. Saying that Geom Mugeuk’s father would not acknowledge him.

There had been a reason for Mabul’s assertion. A reason he had not spoken of back then.

“It is by no means because he dismisses your will.”

If he had dismissed him, he would not be standing here face-to-face telling him such things.

“Then why are you so certain?”

The very reason he held Geom Mugeuk in the highest esteem yet still believed he could not win was precisely this.

“Because it is thanks to you that the Cult Leader is awakening.”

Mabul stared intently at Geom Mugeuk and asked.

“Can you handle the awakened Cult Leader?”

Geom Mugeuk understood. The awakening Mabul spoke of was not merely martial arts strengthened through closed-door training. It meant that Geom Ujin, the person himself, was becoming stronger.

“That is why there are nine of us, are there not?”

At the joke that the Eight Demon Lords were all on his side.

“From what I saw before, there were only two?”

On the day he promised five years later, the only Demon Lord who remained at Geom Mugeuk’s side to the end was Geukak Soma.

“Then I shall simply have to work harder.”

With an expression asking if mere effort would suffice, Mabul exited the shrine.

Geom Mugeuk quietly followed behind, walking the temple grounds with him. Passing monks pressed their palms together in greeting.

“Have you stopped making stone statues?”

“I have.”

The stone statues he had been crafting remained in the corner of the courtyard. Seeing the dust-covered tools, it seemed some time had passed since he set them down.

Geom Mugeuk had expected to tease him for his weak will.

“It was for the best that you stopped.”

“For the best?”

“To be honest, it did not suit you, Lord Mabul. Controlling your mind by carving stone statues? You are not that kind of person, are you?”

Mabul halted his steps and asked.

“Then what kind of person am I?”

“You are someone who moves among people, reads their hearts, persuades them with passion, and fights with all your might for your goals, are you not? Controlling the mind is something you do when catching your breath after fighting fiercely alongside people.”

“Is that an insult? Or praise?”

“If it offends you, it is an insult; if it pleases you, it is praise.”

Geom Mugeuk added with a smile.

“Right now, I am simply telling you the truth.”

Rendered speechless, Mabul wore such an expression as he resumed walking.

“What business brings you here? A busy man like you would not come merely to visit. Is there something you wish to order me to do as the Cult Leader?”

“No. I came to report something I was not ordered to do.”

Mabul looked at Geom Mugeuk with an expression asking what he meant.

Geom Mugeuk knew. That not giving an order was far more difficult than giving one. Just as asking someone to leave was far harder than asking them to come.

That was why this meeting with Mabul was more delicate than the one with the Poison King.

“When my brother left the cult, I secretly asked the Poison King to watch over him.”

As if betraying emotional turmoil, the golden light emanating from Mabul’s body flared more intensely.

“Why report that to me?”

“I felt I should inform you, Lord Mabul.”

After a brief pause, Mabul asked.

“Why the Poison King, of all people?”

“The forged token my brother is investigating this time was coated with a severe poison. Worried there might be a poison user involved, I asked the Poison King.”

“Why tell me this?”

“I was afraid you might misunderstand later. That you might think my brother took the Poison King because he asked.”

In other words, he was worried a misunderstanding might arise between Geom Muyang and Mabul. His brother was not the type to make excuses, nor was the Poison King the type to explain in detail.

“So what if there is a misunderstanding?”

“What do you mean? It would be upsetting. Were it me, I would be so hurt I might curse outright. ‘After how precious I thought you were, you take the Poison King instead of me?’”

“Only a narrow-minded man like you would curse; I would not.”

“You should. If you do not, my brother will be the one upset this time.”

In the end, Mabul burst into laughter.

“Do you really fuss over such trivial matters one by one?”

“I believe it is such trivial matters that cause people to grow distant and come to resent one another. Is not even the grand dream of unifying the world born from such small sentiments?”

Geom Mugeuk’s sincerity continued.

“So please do not step back. Rather, step forward more actively. Even outside of a succession struggle, are there not countless matters in the main cult that require your aid, Lord Mabul? Please act for the sake of the main cult.”

A strange golden gleam seemed to flash across Mabul’s eyes as he looked at Geom Mugeuk, and soon the intense golden radiance softened.

Lately, Mabul had been feeling a helplessness for which he knew no answer. That was why he had been carving stone statues.

But not retreating and advancing instead, facing things in his own manner—perhaps Geom Mugeuk’s words were the way to overcome this helplessness.

“No. I shall go back to carving stone statues.”

“Then even if you carve stone statues, sell them. Carve statues that can be sold. I shall be your first customer.”

A warning not to sink no matter what he did. In that moment, Mabul thought of what he wished to say to Geom Ujin.

‘If you wish to win, Young Master, you must properly awaken.’

Geom Mugeuk bowed respectfully and turned to leave.

“Whew, I am busy, so I do not know when I shall visit again.”

From behind the departing Geom Mugeuk, Mabul spoke.

“Your brother is a strong man.”

That was because Mabul was someone who believed in his brother more than anyone. He was also someone who had staked his life on him.

“So you need not worry too much. The same blood that flows in your father and in you flows in the body of the Eldest Young Master.”

Watching the brilliantly shining golden radiance, Geom Mugeuk bowed his head respectfully.

“Yes, I believe so as well.”

But Geom Mugeuk’s words and actions differed.

Believing was one thing. Worry was another.

‘It is precisely because we share the same blood that there are parts that worry me more.’

After leaving Mabul’s quarters, he spoke to Jeokyeon, who accompanied him.

“Contact Eunwol and have them concentrate all available intelligence networks on my brother and the Poison King. And one more thing.”

Geom Mugeuk added one order he had delayed giving to Eunwol.

* * *

Several days had passed since Geom Muyang left the cult.

During those days, Geom Mugeuk’s pace of work had grown faster and faster.

And today, his speed was even greater.

It was not simply because he had grown more skilled. Today, he finished his work unusually quickly, like a man with pressing business. Normally, Geom Mugeuk would crack a silly joke after finishing a task, but today he uttered not a single one.

“Is there some matter today?”

“Is it not good to finish early? It gives more time to rest.”

But today, there was a different reason.

Having finished his work faster than usual, Geom Mugeuk followed Sama Myeong out.

“Is there something you wish to discuss?”

“No, since I finished work early, I thought I would tour Tongcheongak for the first time in a while. Would that be alright?”

Who would refuse such a request?

“Yes, let us go.”

And so Geom Mugeuk followed Sama Myeong into Tongcheongak.

Passing through a place with even stricter security than the Cheonma Hall, they entered to see a vast space filled with countless officers of Tongcheongak.

The place was busy. Truly, if one wished to best express what it meant to be busy, one need only show this place.

Sshk, sshuk, sshk!

Cylinder tubes containing dispatches continuously arrived from holes installed in one wall.

The officers sorted the arriving dispatches according to their importance.

Depending on their contents, flags stuck in a map of the Central Plains were moved to different locations.

Sama Myeong could tell. That Geom Mugeuk had come here because he was curious about news of Geom Muyang. That despite his calm exterior, he was deeply worried about his brother.

“Since you are here, let us hear news of your brother before you go.”

Sama Myeong spoke to a subordinate officer.

“Bring the most recent information regarding the Eldest Young Master.”

The officer immediately brought documents and reported.

“The Eldest Young Master has arrived at his destination, Changsha. During that time, he stopped at only two inns, and excluding time spent eating and sleeping, he headed toward his destination without rest.”

How could he not worry? Hyung, I told you not to dwell on nearly killing me! Even if I had been struck, I would not have died!

“If we stay here, I suppose we could even find out what rice my brother ate.”

At that, the officer flipped through the documents.

“We have records of what he ate at the inn here. Shall I report it?”

“That will not be necessary.”

He could truly appreciate how thoroughly Tongcheongak was monitoring his brother. The role of Eunwol, which was cooperating with them, was likely significant as well.

“My brother might have a woman hidden in the Central Plains. I suppose this shall reveal her.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s silly remark, Sama Myeong subtly asked.

“Are you worried about your brother?”

Geom Mugeuk answered with a smile.

“You know how wicked my brother is. Rather, I worry for the martial world.”

But contrary to his words, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze was fixed on the flag marked “Muyang.” The flag was placed in the Changsha region, and beside his brother’s flag, a flag of the Changsha Biaoju had been placed together.

And slightly apart from the two flags, a single ornate green flag was planted.

* * *

Geom Muyang was striding boldly through the streets of Changsha.

He passed by all the taverns and sights he would have visited had he come with Geom Mugeuk. He had run straight toward only his goal.

Only by capturing the one who had made that forged token—which had nearly killed his younger brother and nearly led him to an irreversible mistake—could he enjoy the leisure afforded by this long-awaited departure from the cult.

Of course, he did not act recklessly.

Geom Muyang knew exactly how to handle such matters. He did not resolve this by recklessly revealing his identity as the Eldest Young Master of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult or by using force. He was not a man who rushed his work simply because his heart was impatient.

A middle-aged martial artist was waiting for him in front of the Changsha Biaoju. He was Ho Myeong, the lord of the Changsha Branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, who had come after receiving advance notice.

“It is an honor to meet you, Eldest Young Master.”

“Branch Lord Ho, thank you for coming out.”

“The branch’s elites are all waiting nearby.”

Ho Myeong had truly been surprised to receive the Eldest Young Master’s dispatch. No matter that he had lost the succession struggle, he was the eldest son of the Heavenly Demon. A single slip of the tongue could cost him his head.

“If you but give the order, I can have everyone at the Changsha Biaoju kneeling within half a shichen.”

“That will not be necessary. Let us meet the chief with just the two of us. Do not reveal my identity.”

“Yes! Please wait a moment.”

Ho Myeong said a few words to the martial artist guarding the entrance of the Changsha Biaoju. At that, the martial artist turned pale and ran inside, while another martial artist guided Geom Muyang and Ho Myeong in.

As they waited in the main hall, Ji Daehyeong, the chief of the escort agency, appeared with his escort leaders. One of the escort leaders recognized Ho Myeong’s face and sent a telepathic message to Ji Daehyeong confirming that he was indeed the branch lord.

Geom Muyang had used the Changsha Branch Lord to meet the chief of the escort agency as quickly as possible. It was not a fox borrowing the tiger’s might, but rather a tiger borrowing the fox’s might.

“To what does the Evil Cult’s branch lord owe a visit to our agency?”

Only Ho Myeong was visible in Ji Daehyeong’s eyes. A visit from the Changsha Branch Lord of the Evil Cult, with whom there was normally no interaction, was truly a serious matter.

“I have come to inquire about the escorted goods you previously delivered to the Heuksu Faction.”

At Ho Myeong’s words, Ji Daehyeong’s expression hardened instantly.

“We do not disclose information about escorted goods to outsiders.”

Then Branch Lord Ho Myeong unleashed a cold demonic energy.

The escort leaders were not easily intimidated either. They raised their internal energy, ready to draw their swords at any moment. This was what they trusted.

“No matter who you are, if you touch us, the Zhongyuan Biaoju Alliance will not sit idle.”

They were one of the largest interest groups in the martial world, so even the orthodox and demonic sides did not treat them carelessly.

Then Geom Muyang, who had been silent, stepped forward.

“I have not come to intimidate or trouble you.”

The moment Geom Muyang opened his mouth, Ji Daehyeong could feel it. He had naturally assumed this young man was merely an attendant warrior of the branch lord.

‘This man did not come serving the branch lord. The branch lord came serving this man.’

From the moment he spoke, the dignity and pressure emanating from Geom Muyang were extraordinary.

“The item you delivered was a counterfeit of our cult’s weapon.”

Ji Daehyeong was startled. From his expression, he seemed completely unaware of this fact.

“A large escort agency like the Changsha Biaoju would not have delivered goods to the dark path without checking the contents.”

Geom Muyang looked at the accompanying escort leaders with cold eyes and asked.

“Who deceived the chief?”

Chapter 614: Wrinkles All Over This Handsome Face
-------------------------------------------------
The gazes of Escort Bureau Chief Ji Daehyeong and the escort captains turned toward one man.

He was none other than Escort Captain Chungbaek, the man in charge of delivering the shipment to the Black Water Sect.

A look of bewilderment flashed across Chungbaek’s face. When he had followed the Bureau Chief here, he had never imagined he would be singled out.

“This is baseless slander.”

His shout trembled as much as his wavering eyes.

Jian Wuyang, on the other hand, was composed. He slowly walked toward Chungbaek. Not only Chungbaek, but Ji Daehyeong and the other escort captains tensed as well.

Jian Wuyang stopped a few paces away and stared at Chungbaek. Even without trying to glare, Jian Wuyang’s gaze was fundamentally cold.

“Do we look so idle to you?”

It was a rhetorical question—would they have come all this way just to frame a mere escort captain?

His calm words carried persuasion. After all, Huo Ming, the Changsha branch leader, had come in person, and the speaker was someone who appeared to stand even higher than a branch leader.

Before Chungbaek could offer an excuse, Jian Wuyang continued. His low voice carried strength; because he was not rude, his words held even greater authority.

“I will not press the issue of you deceiving the Bureau Chief. That is a matter for your own escort bureau to handle.”

Jian Wuyang glanced at Ji Daehyeong once, then turned his gaze back to Chungbaek.

“There is only one thing I wish to hear. Who entrusted you with the shipment?”

A brief silence fell.

Ji Daehyeong did not step forward to defend Chungbaek, claiming there was no need to answer. He was overwhelmed by Jian Wuyang, and he had also begun to suspect Chungbaek unconsciously.

“It wasn’t that kind of item! Bureau Chief! Please believe me! This is a frame-up. I am innocent.”

Chungbaek’s excuses were not specific, and that made him all the more suspicious.

But they could not suspect him blindly. Their opponent was the ruthless Demonic Cult, after all.

Ji Daehyeong stepped in and asked Chungbaek in a gentle tone. He was still one of his own men, after all.

“Captain Chung. Calm yourself and tell me calmly what happened.”

“The shipment was ordinary.”

“What was it?”

He had received several reports regarding recent escort missions, but he could not recall any item that required special attention. That meant either Chungbaek had filed a false report, or the Demonic Cult was pinning the blame on him.

“The shipment was ordinary military weapons.”

“Who entrusted them to you?”

“I cannot speak of it in a place where outsiders are present.”

“It is fine. I permit you to speak.”

Then came words that should never have been spoken in this situation.

“Do you suspect me?”

He was certainly suspicious.

“Not at all. If it is a conspiracy, as you say, should we not uncover the truth?”

“Very well. Then I shall tell only the Bureau Chief. The one who handed me the goods was—”

As he lowered his voice and seemed to approach the Bureau Chief—

Whoosh.

Chungbaek leaped toward the entrance.

But someone moved even faster.

Thud!

A flying kick sent Chungbaek tumbling across the floor before he could escape through the entrance. Just as he sprang back up—

the man who had kicked him pressed an acupoint to seal his pulse.

The one who had anticipated his escape and stopped him was none other than Huo Ming, the Changsha branch leader.

As the one in charge of leading the largest branch in the Hunan region, his martial prowess was formidable.

Huo Ming grabbed him by the collar, dragged him over, and forced him to his knees before Jian Wuyang. Not a single escort captain dared to intervene in the process. If he were not guilty, he would not have fled. The moment he tried to run, the situation was already over.

Jian Wuyang spoke to Ji Daehyeong.

“I trust you will believe us now.”

The words were polite, but Jian Wuyang’s gaze was cold. The meaning in his eyes was clear: We have shown you this courtesy, so you should show some in return.

“I will take this man to the Changsha branch.”

Ji Daehyeong did not dare say he could not allow it. Having already revealed his strength, Huo Ming was emanating a rough demonic aura.

Jian Wuyang looked down at Chungbaek and tilted his head slightly.

“Are those who merely forge counterfeit weapons more fearsome than we are?”

Chungbaek’s face was twisted in terror. The Demonic Cult before his eyes was frightening, and so were those he had dealings with.

Huo Ming released Chungbaek’s acupoint just enough for him to walk.

Just as they took him out of the great hall and were walking toward the main gate—

Schwing!

Together with an ear-splitting sound of wind, the flesh-rending sound created by that wind!

Thud, thud, thud, thud!

Everyone’s gaze turned toward one man.

With a blank face, Chungbaek looked down at his chest.

Fssssss.

His chest, struck squarely by the hidden weapon, was melting away with a gaping hole.

Chungbaek’s body crumpled forward with a hollow thud.

Before him stood a man aiming a cylindrical hidden weapon. He was an ordinary-looking young man.

He had disguised himself as an employee of the escort bureau and had been sweeping the courtyard until he suddenly launched a surprise attack.

Neither Huo Ming, who had been walking alongside Chungbaek, nor Jian Wuyang, who had been following behind, could stop the ambush.

“You bastard!”

Just as Huo Ming was about to charge in, Jian Wuyang shouted.

“Careful. I heard the Zhenji.”

Huo Ming froze for an instant. As a branch leader, he knew well what kind of hidden weapon the Zhenji was. One of the Ten Great Golden Dragon Hidden Weapons designated by the Martial Alliance.

The man aimed the Zhenji, which had been pointed forward, at Jian Wuyang.

But Jian Wuyang showed no fear, his expression challenging the man to fire if he dared.

At that moment, Huo Ming drew his sword and stood in front of Jian Wuyang. He was determined to block it with his own body if necessary.

The man looked at Jian Wuyang and grinned. It was the simpering laugh of a madman.

And he brought the Zhenji, which had been aimed at Jian Wuyang, under his own chin.

Schwick!

Thud, thud, thud!

There was no time to stop him. He committed suicide with the Zhenji. It seemed his purpose had been to kill Chungbaek and then take his own life from the very beginning.

Huo Ming rushed over and examined the body, but there was no way he could be alive.

Jian Wuyang went over, picked up the Zhenji in the man’s hand, and inspected it. It was a counterfeit Zhenji powerful enough to have killed even his younger brother.

Jian Wuyang’s gaze grew cold and heavy.

That he had been watched since coming here, and that the assassin had committed suicide after the ambush. Suicide sounded easy, but forcing someone to kill themselves after they accomplished their goal was no simple feat. It meant the opponent was no ordinary organization.

Intuiting that this matter would not be easily resolved, Jian Wuyang immediately gave Huo Ming an order.

“Report this situation as-is to the Tongtian Pavilion. And select only the elites from among the branch’s martial artists.”

“Yes, understood.”

Afterward, Jian Wuyang turned back to Escort Bureau Chief Ji Daehyeong.

Ji Daehyeong cried out with a startled and bewildered face.

“That man is not one of ours! We had nothing to do with this!”

Because the incident had occurred inside the escort bureau, he was terrified of being misunderstood as having been involved. If the Demonic Cult fabricated charges and pinned them on him, it would be hard to escape.

“I am aware.”

At Jian Wuyang’s calm words, Ji Daehyeong let out a sigh of relief.

He had harbored suspicions that the Demonic Cult might be scheming something, but this ambush cleanly dispelled those doubts.

“He would not have faced annihilation simply for delivering an item once.”

Ji Daehyeong thought so too. If it had been a single mistake, Chungbaek would have sought forgiveness rather than chosen to flee. In other words, it meant he had disguised goods as shipments and delivered them multiple times.

“Can you hand over the records of escort missions during his time as a captain?”

Though the current situation was such that Ji Daehyeong had to comply whether he wanted to or not, Jian Wuyang handled the matter politely and rationally.

“Do not worry that the list will be leaked. It is only to track down those who killed him. Since an escort captain is dead, you too must discover the truth, must you not?”

Ji Daehyeong agreed without hesitation.

“Understood. I will provide the records.”

“Send them to the Changsha branch of our main sect.”

“Understood.”

Jian Wuyang turned and left the place.

Huo Ming, following behind, asked him.

“Where are you going?”

Since they had come to the side that handed over the goods, now it was time to go to the side that received them.

“To the Black Water Sect.”

* * *

As Jian Wuji was leaving the Heavenly Demon Hall, someone appeared before him.

“Young Cult Leader!”

It was Seo Daeryong.

“Right Arm! How have you been?”

Jian Wuji greeted him gladly, and Seo Daeryong broke into a wide smile and ran over.

“I agonized a hundred times over whether I should come to the Heavenly Demon Hall to pay my respects or not.”

“Why didn’t you come?”

“You’ve become the temporary cult leader. Somehow, it felt like I shouldn’t come.”

Seo Daeryong looked up at the Heavenly Demon Hall. The majestic Heavenly Demon Hall and the giant stone statue of the Heavenly Demon Soul guarding it were enough to make one feel intimidated just by looking.

“How could I? It’s a frightening place just to look at.”

At that, Jian Wuji cast him a suspicious glance.

“That’s not the reason, is it?”

“What do you mean?”

“You didn’t forget me because you were playing with Miss Dana, did you?”

Seo Daeryong’s face flushed slightly. It wasn’t wrong. Whenever he had even a moment of free time, he spent it with Dana.

The Dan sisters were all busy. The second sister, Danbi, was zealous in her work as an enforcer of the Yellow Springs Pavilion, and the youngest, Danyeons, was doted upon as the little beloved of the Ghostshadow Squad.

The eldest, Dana, had sought out the Yihua Sword Venerable to receive swordsmanship instruction once, and was now devoting herself to training.

Then, when Seo Daeryong’s duties ended late at night, they would meet and nurture their love. Because they only met briefly amid their busy schedules, that time was all the more precious.

“Are you happy just thinking about it?”

Seo Daeryong replied with a broad grin instead of words.

It was wonderful to have a woman who was on his side.

“These are good times.”

“Such good times, yet the Young Cult Leader has never experienced them even once.”

Seo Daeryong shrugged.

“Finally, I have something I can beat the Young Cult Leader at!”

“Can you truly call that winning?”

“Don’t say such ominous things with that meaningful, scary expression!”

The two looked at each other and laughed. Seeing Seo Daeryong always lifted his spirits.

“Is something going on these days?”

“Why do you think so?”

“After all, I am the right arm attached to the Young Cult Leader’s body. I just know.”

How did he know? In the old days, they would have drunk together countless times at pleasure houses. As time passed, everything changed. People and situations alike.

But Seo Daeryong tried his best to delay that change. He had come to see Jian Wuji today to block the tide of change with his very body.

*‘No matter how much the world changes, I do not want my relationship with the Young Cult Leader to change! I want to continue living a life where there is someone I would gladly lay down my life for!’*

How could Jian Wuji not know his heart? That was why he could tease him like this.

“Since even my right arm has grown a heart, should I slowly cut it off now?”

“I’m sorry, but even if limbs were dangling from that right arm instead of a heart, I still wouldn’t fall off!”

Only after exchanging such banter did Jian Wuji speak honestly to Seo Daeryong.

“My older brother has left the cult.”

This was top secret; even the Yellow Springs Pavilion Master did not know yet.

“Has he taken on a dangerous mission?”

“I don’t know. That’s why I’m more worried.”

If it were definitely a dangerous mission, he could prepare accordingly. But it was the not knowing that weighed most heavily on a man’s heart.

“Shall I go out and assist the Great Young Master?”

“You have a hard time with my brother, don’t you?”

“It’s beyond difficult. The Great Young Master is terrifying.”

“And you’d still go?”

“Because the Young Cult Leader is worried, right? If quick-witted me is by his side, I will surely be of help.”

For the sake of the Young Cult Leader, he could even accompany the Great Young Master. What couldn’t he do?

“You have someone to protect now, so you should take care of yourself.”

Feeling that these were not empty words but held sincere meaning, Seo Daeryong flinched.

“I haven’t even properly spread my wings yet, and already you want me to be careful?”

“Where is happiness easily given? If you gain one thing, you must lose another. Look at me—since I obtained the temporary cult leader’s seat, I’ve been riddled with anxiety. Wrinkles are piling up on this handsome face of mine. Look, a new wrinkle is arriving over there.”

Right at that spot, a Tongtian Pavilion soldier came running.

“News has arrived. I believe you must come with me.”

As Jian Wuji walked following the soldier, he looked back at Seo Daeryong. He was watching him with worried eyes.

*When I returned, I told my father:*

*“I will be happy.”*

He recalled what his father had said then.

*“If you tread this path, do not even think of happiness.”*

“You are right, Father. Back then, I boasted that I would be happy, but I have come to realize that the stronger my martial arts grow and the deeper my relationships with people become, the harder the path becomes. I also realize that when one has nothing, one can boast all the louder.

Still, as I have done until now, I will find a way.”

Jian Wuji said loudly to Seo Daeryong.

“I’ll come to see the elder soon. Let’s have a drink then!”

* * *

“The escort captain who delivered the shipment to the Black Water Sect is dead. Fortunately, the Great Young Master is safe.”

Jian Wuji felt relief at Sima Ming’s report. Jian Wuji broke the frozen atmosphere with a joke.

“I told you my brother is no good, didn’t I? If he had caught the guy, he would have let him live. Only he made it out unharmed.”

Sima Ming did not laugh even though he knew it was a joke.

“This time, a counterfeit Zhenji was used as well. The assailant committed suicide on the spot.”

Both reports were grave. This counterfeit was a Zhenji as powerful as the genuine article.

If that Zhenji had been fired at the Great Young Master?

To make matters worse, the enemy was an organization that made its assassins commit suicide.

Sima Ming cautiously proposed.

“Will you send more support?”

Jian Wuji’s deepened gaze turned to the main camp on the topographical map.

Various colored Demon Lord Banners were planted there. Jian Wuji’s gaze settled on the golden flag symbolizing Mabul among them.

If he sent support now, his older brother would be furious. It was a matter of his pride. At least, Mabul was the one most likely to soften that anger.

“No, there is no need.”

Even if Mabul went out, it would be to gather poisonous herbs, not to help.

Jian Wuji’s gaze shifted back to the Changsha region.

Next to his brother’s flag was a green flag.

It was the Poison King’s Demon Lord Banner.

He could almost see the Poison King standing with twelve poison pouches at his waist.

The Poison King had stepped out from the Thousand Poison Forest, his own personal world, and was standing in the outside world. Jian Wuji knew better than anyone how strong he became at such times. Yes, he had to trust him.

“If the Zhenji had targeted my brother, the user would have melted before he could pull the trigger.”

Chapter 615: I've Come to Collect Payment
-----------------------------------------
Sama Myeong could tell.

That a green flag was fluttering in Geom Muggeuk’s heart right now. That he absolutely trusted the Poison King.

The special bond between them was something that could not be deciphered even by the brilliant strategists of Tongcheongak.

“Where is Hyung’s next destination?”

Sama Myeong looked at the topographic map and answered.

“The Eldest Young Master’s next destination is the Black Water Faction in Yueyang.”

A subordinate strategist pasted a slip of paper with the name Black Water Faction written on it over the location of Yueyang.

Even without Sama Myeong giving separate orders, the Tongcheongak strategists began gathering and sorting information on the Black Water Faction on their own. It truly demonstrated what the greatest information organization of the martial world was capable of.

And Sama Myeong was already familiar with the basic information regarding the Black Water Faction.

“The Black Water Faction is notorious even among the dark paths of Hunan. They have expanded their organization through all manner of evil deeds and now rank among the top ten dark path factions in Hunan. Their attempt to purchase the counterfeit Jin’gyeok this time appears to be aimed at crushing rival factions and taking control of the Hunan dark path.”

“What kind of person is their leader?”

“The leader of the Black Water Faction is a man called Huang Ba, one who is a child of the dark path to the bone. He is so crazy about money that one could say he’s mad for it. Having been born and raised on rough streets, he has never once retreated when facing an enemy. In the Hunan martial world, he is regarded as a madman. He is probably someone who wouldn’t even fear our cult.”

Then Geom Muggeuk gave an unexpected reply.

“That actually makes it easier for Hyung to deal with him. His greed will make for a good weakness to exploit. And if the other party acts out of turn, Hyung’s true nature will come out too. Everyone just doesn’t know. Hyung has the dirtiest temper in our cult! Everyone is being fooled right now!”

Geom Muggeuk’s voice rang loudly through the strategy room.

Others might frown thinking he was badmouthing his older brother behind his back, but at least Sama Myeong and the strategists here knew better than anyone.

They knew how hard Geom Muggeuk had worked to keep that foul-tempered brother of his from getting killed.

“Please send all information and data on the Black Water Faction to Hyung as quickly as possible.”

“Understood.”

Geom Muggeuk rose from his seat.

“I shall take my leave now.”

“Please do.”

Sama Myeong politely clasped his fists in salute.

“But it is late. Are you not going home, Strategist?”

“There is a place to sleep here. During emergencies, I sleep here.”

“You mean your office?”

“No. There is a quarters with a bed as well.”

It seemed there was a place to sleep within Tongcheongak. Since he had only ever met here in the strategy room and in his office, he hadn’t known such a place existed.

“May I take a brief look around?”

“Please do.”

Sama Myeong guided Geom Muggeuk to the strategists’ quarters.

There were rooms where ordinary strategists could sleep, and Sama Myeong’s own room was separately prepared. It was larger and more pleasant than expected.

“Shortly after I became Chief Strategist, the Cult Leader had it newly furnished for me.”

He thought that perhaps around that time, his father had begun to truly trust Sama Myeong.

“Do you often sleep here?”

“Originally, I only slept here during emergency situations. But these days, I often do.”

“You are of an age. You should sleep at home.”

Sama Myeong’s house had been prepared within the cult grounds, but when he went there, he was alone in that large house. He was more comfortable here, but he could not say such things.

“As I told you before, I have greatly benefited from the heart method taught by the Cult Leader.”

He had conversed with Sama Myeong many times and even shared drinks with him at a tavern.

But in truth, he did not know much about him. That was true even of his house. Though he had a house within the cult, Geom Muggeuk had never once been there.

Part of it was that Geom Muggeuk had deliberately kept his distance for Gowol’s sake, and Sama Myeong himself did not speak much of personal matters.

“Don’t you have any hidden liquor?”

Sama Myeong had said so once. That he too had been a heavy drinker in his youth.

Geom Muggeuk had spoken in jest, but Sama Myeong took out liquor from a cabinet.

“I occasionally have a drink when I cannot sleep.”

Sama Myeong poured a drink for Geom Muggeuk.

“You should have a drink too, Strategist.”

“I do not drink when an emergency is declared.”

Sama Myeong poured cold tea from a teapot and took a sip.

“Is an emergency declared like this even when I am away?”

“To be honest, a higher level of emergency is declared.”

Well, of course. An emergency is declared even when Hyung goes out, so when the successor is away from the cult...

Whenever he returned, Sama Myeong always treated him calmly, but unbeknownst to him, he had passed through such long and tense moments in places unseen.

One could tell just by looking at the documents piled high on the desk against that wall. How Sama Myeong’s peaceful smile was a smile forged.

“I am sorry.”

“Whatever for? It is only natural that I do this.”

“No, I am apologizing in advance. When Father emerges from his seclusion, he might go wandering around again.”

At Geom Muggeuk’s half-joking, half-serious words, Sama Myeong gave a faint smile. Geom Muggeuk felt a desire to do something for the Chief Strategist who suffered from having met the wrong young cult leader.

“Strategist, is there anything you wish to do? Ah, excluding unifying the martial world.”

At Geom Muggeuk’s addendum, Sama Myeong’s smile deepened.

“Not particularly.”

“Is there anywhere you wish to go?”

As if to say there was nothing, Sama Myeong quietly shook his head and drank his tea.

“Then what was your childhood dream? Surely you didn’t dream of becoming the Chief Strategist of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?”

Sama Myeong briefly recalled his childhood.

He seemed to have had many things he wanted to do. Surprisingly, he could not clearly remember what he had wanted to become or what he had wanted to do.

“I do not recall well, but perhaps it was to become the Chief Strategist of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?”

At Sama Myeong’s jest, this time it was Geom Muggeuk who laughed.

“If you can’t remember, it’s because you’ve used your brain too much on feeding our cult.”

This time, Sama Myeong asked about Geom Muggeuk’s dream.

“What was the Acting Cult Leader’s dream? Excluding becoming the successor.”

“I had none, excluding that.”

Surprised by Geom Muggeuk’s answer, Sama Myeong asked.

“None?”

“Yes. Why are you so surprised?”

In his life before the regression, he truly had no dreams. Due to the competition with his brother that began from a young age, he only wished to become the successor. Afterward, he lived only for the sake of regressing.

If one were to speak of dreams after regressing?

“I thought that since you are the Acting Cult Leader, you would somehow have many dreams.”

“You probably felt that way because I talk so much.”

Geom Muggeuk emptied his cup and rose from his seat.

“I shall stop disturbing you and take my leave for real now.”

“I will see you tomorrow.”

Sama Myeong followed him out to see him off.

Looking back from the entrance, he saw that Sama Myeong was not returning to his quarters with the bed, but walking back to the strategy room.

The emergency here would continue until the deployed flags returned to the cult grounds.

* * *

Huang Ba, the leader of the Black Water Faction, was furious.

He was always furious, but today he was particularly more so.

“So? You still haven’t found the goods?”

“Yes.”

The subordinate who answered curtly was Ming Gui, Huang Ba’s right-hand man.

Had the one reporting been anyone other than Ming Gui, a palm strike might have already flown and cracked his skull.

But while that might apply to others, it could not be done to Ming Gui.
