The one who had made the Black Water Faction what it was today was none other than Ming Gui. Ming Gui possessed martial arts far superior to the leader Huang Ba, yet the reason he devoted his loyalty to Huang Ba was the debt of gratitude for saving his son.

Five years ago, a group of vagabonds had ravaged and murdered Ming Gui’s wife and tried to kill his young son as well. Huang Ba, who happened to be passing by, killed the vagabonds and saved the son.

From that day forth, Ming Gui became Huang Ba’s right-hand man and devoted his loyalty to him.

“Could the sellers have staged this themselves?”

They had secretly agreed to pay a large sum to purchase one of the Ten Great Golden Dragon Hidden Weapons. However, on the day they received the goods, someone stole them.

Whoever it was, they had undoubtedly learned of the transaction beforehand and seized the opportunity.

“We hadn’t paid them yet; why would they do that?”

The sellers had said they would receive payment for the goods after delivery. If they had intended to steal the goods from the start, they would have taken the money in advance.

“So it was the Great Light Faction bastards after all?”

The Great Light Faction was another dark path faction competing for interests with the Black Water Faction in the Hunan region.

Just then, Ming Gui quietly raised his hand to draw Huang Ba’s attention, then spoke softly toward the door.

“Who is it?”

Then the door opened, and a man revealed himself.

“I’ve come to collect the money.”

The man wore his bamboo hat pulled low, hiding his face, but a rough and domineering air seeped through his low voice.

Ming Gui sent a telepathic message to Huang Ba.

—Be careful. He is no ordinary man.

One could tell simply by the fact that he had infiltrated this far while evading the eyes of the subordinates.

Huang Ba nodded slightly and asked the bamboo-hatted man.

“What money do you speak of?”

“If you received the goods, you must pay.”

The man walked over and sat facing Huang Ba.

“We never received the goods.”

“The Jangsa Escort Agency clearly stated that they delivered the goods to you.”

Huang Ba’s eyes turned savage. The fact that he had to pay without having received the goods made his anger surge.

Of course, had he been someone who obediently handed over money in such a situation, he would not have survived in this rough dark path underbelly until now.

“What if I cannot pay?”

Then the man spoke in a low voice.

“You failed to receive the goods, so you must not have been able to test the hidden weapon.”

He raised his hand and pointed it at Huang Ba. Before anyone knew it, he held the Jin’gyeok in his hand.

“Would you like to become the test subject yourself?”

Schwing.

Ming Gui’s sword was half-drawn. He too was ready to cut down the opponent in an instant.

“No matter how fast that sword is, it cannot be faster than this.”

Even with the Jin’gyeok aimed at him, Huang Ba did not cower.

“If this bastard kills me, cut off his arms and legs, peel all the skin from his torso, and put him in a salt barrel.”

Huang Ba’s bravado stemmed from the belief that the man could not fire without receiving payment.

Indeed, the man did not unleash the Jin’gyeok.

“Bring the one who first struck the deal with us.”

“Why him?”

“If there is no money, life must pay the price. I shall at least collect the life of the one who first called us.”

He spoke as if that were their law.

Huang Ba inwardly cheered. He had been thinking of bargaining down the price; if he could substitute a subordinate’s life instead, he would gladly accept.

It was precisely when Huang Ba was about to order a subordinate to be brought.

“You!”

Until then, Ming Gui, who had been silently watching the man, opened his mouth.

“You are not from that place, are you?”

Huang Ba looked at Ming Gui with a surprised face.

“This man is not someone who would serve under another and run around collecting money.”

The instinctive sense of crisis Ming Gui felt from his opponent was something he had never experienced in his life.

“Remove your bamboo hat!”

Then the man slowly removed his bamboo hat.

The one who revealed himself beneath the bamboo hat was none other than Geom Muyang.

He had disguised himself as them, taking advantage of the fact that the counterfeit weapon sellers had agreed to receive payment after delivery.

What Geom Muyang needed to find out from the Black Water Faction was how they had learned of the hidden weapon sellers and in what manner the transaction had been conducted. There was definitely someone within the Black Water Faction who handled the practical matters.

Thus, this plan had been devised based on the information that Huang Ba was a maniac for money. He would surely hand over a subordinate instead of money. Geom Muyang had thought this would be an easier and more certain method than beating up a madman to find out.

Ming Gui had seen through the fact that Geom Muyang was not someone who would run around collecting money. That he had seen through the disguise despite the bamboo hat and the fierce, rough demeanor showed that Ming Gui’s insight into people was extraordinary.

Geom Muyang turned the words he had heard back on Ming Gui.

“You too are not someone who should be serving under such a man.”

The moment his eyes met Geom Muyang’s gaze, Ming Gui knew.

‘This is not someone I can face.’

On the other hand, Huang Ba shouted in a fury, feeling slighted.

“You bastard, who are you?”

Geom Muyang placed the Jin’gyeok he held onto the table and asked.

“Who were you planning to kill with our goods?”

For a moment, Huang Ba was blank-faced, then he shot to his feet.

“The Demonic Cult!”

This reaction was a confession that he had known the item he tried to purchase was a hidden weapon of the Demonic Cult.

A heavy silence permeated the air.

A single icy remark slipped from Geom Muyang’s lips.

“Sit. Stop looking down on me.”

Now, Geom Muyang revealed an aura of an entirely different caliber than before. The icy chill dwelling in his pupils left Hwang Pae breathless.

*‘If he wanted to kill me, he wouldn’t have disguised himself in the first place.’*

With that in mind, he judged that acting boldly would be to his advantage.

“As if anyone would be afraid of the Demonic Cult—”

Smack!

Hwang Pae’s face whipped to the side. It was Geom Muyang’s fist, which had flown in from nowhere, smashing into his face before he could tell when or how.

Shring!

The very moment Myeonggwi drew his sword.

“Don’t.”

At that single word from Geom Muyang, Myeonggwi’s body froze. Powerful demonic qi bore down on him, rendering him immobile.

Hwang Pae spat out broken teeth and shouted.

“Kill this bastard!”

Demonic Cult or not, he was the type who simply had to act on his temper to feel satisfied.

In the next moment, Hwang Pae’s body shot into the air. Geom Muyang had grabbed him and flung him toward the door.

Crack.

Crashed through the door and tumbling across the courtyard, Hwang Pae sprang up and shouted. This time, he shouted at the subordinates filling the courtyard.

“Kill that bastard!”

But no one moved.

“What are you waiting for, you bastards!”

In that instant, Hwang Pae flinched in surprise. He had thought they were his subordinates, but unfamiliar martial artists were staring at him.

They were none other than Branch Hall Leader Homyeong and the elites of the Changsha Branch Hall.

Behind them, his own subordinates were kneeling. The few who had attacked without recognizing their opponents had been so thoroughly thrashed that their faces were mangled beyond recognition.

Though the Heuksu Faction was a prominent faction of the dark path in Honam, how could they possibly stand against a branch hall leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and his elite subordinates?

Seeing Hwang Pae, one subordinate instinctively tried to rise and rush out.

Thrust!

A branch hall martial artist standing behind him nonchalantly plunged a sword into his neck.

Watching him crumple and spew blood, the martial artists of the Heuksu Faction all bowed their heads in unison. They were men who had lived without knowing the meaning of fear. Men who, out on the streets, no one dared to even meet eyes with—today, they had truly met their match.

Even in this situation, Hwang Pae gritted his teeth.

“So what if you’re from the Demonic Cult? Go ahead, kill me too! Try killing Hwang Pae on this Honam soil! Do you think the Sado Alliance will just stand by if you touch us!”

If they had wanted to kill him, they would have done so already. With this sole belief, he held out to the end. Because his subordinates were watching, he pushed even harder.

Geom Muyang, who had been watching him thrash about in fury with an indifferent gaze, turned his head to look at Myeonggwi.

“You lost your family to wanderers, didn’t you?”

When he suddenly mentioned that matter, Myeonggwi was startled. In other words, it meant that he had come after investigating him.

“From what we found out…”

At that moment, Hwang Pae cut off Geom Muyang’s words and shouted.

“What do you want?”

Chapter 616: We Were Never Kind to the Murim
--------------------------------------------
The silence that descended over the hall was caused by Hwang Pae’s sudden change in attitude.

When the man who had been raging like a madman abruptly calmed down, everyone’s eyes turned toward him.

Only then did Hwang Pae realize that he had made a mistake. He had been hurling curses at them, only to ask what they wanted in the end. Still, he had truly been so startled moments ago that it felt as though his liver had leaped out of his mouth.

As if an unguarded remark had slipped out, Hwang Pae suddenly shouted and returned to his former demeanor.

“What do you want, you ask?”

Geom Muyang, on the other hand, remained as calm and indifferent as ever.

“The person who made the deal with them.”

Hwang Pae blinked for a moment before hurriedly calling for someone.

“Gu Yong! Where is Gu Yong?”

He was the man who had led this deal.

Gu Yong served a role akin to a chief steward in the Heuksupa. He had a keen nose for money and often brought in profitable ventures. This time, he had brought them an opportunity no one had expected: the chance to purchase the Demon Cult’s hidden weapon, which had been designated a Murim Forbidden Weapon.

The fact that it was a weapon of the Demon Cult concerned Hwang Pae, but that was precisely why he coveted it.

Hwang Pae’s hesitation did not last long. It would require a huge sum of money, money dear as blood, but since payment was deferred, there was no concern about being cheated.

Above all, with that hidden weapon, dealing with the masters of the Daegwang Sect would not be difficult. Once they began assassinating them one by one, it would only be a matter of time before they became the greatest black-robed sect in Honam.

He had undertaken this scheme while dreaming of such grand ambitions, and now everything had turned into a complete mess. All because of this bastard.

“Gu Yong, aren’t you coming out?”

Though Hwang Pae searched frantically, Gu Yong was nowhere among the kneeling martial artists.

Hwang Pae rushed over, hauled one of his subordinates to his feet by the collar, and shook him.

“Where is Gu Yong?”

“I don’t know.”

Hwang Pae grabbed and shook another man, but he did not know either.

Then another subordinate standing toward the back spoke up.

“He went to Hwayeong Pavilion to drink.”

Hwang Pae’s eyes rolled back.

“That lunatic!”

All this had happened because of that bastard, and he had gone off to drink at a pleasure house? The sun had not even set yet.

Hwang Pae slapped the cheek of the man whose collar he was still holding and shouted.

“Go find him right now! Hurry!”

As he continued yelling, Ho Myeong, the leader of the Ji Dan, raised a finger to his lips and gestured for silence.

Hwang Pae’s face twisted.

‘This bastard is shushing me with a finger instead of saying it?’

But the words never left his mouth.

Ho Myeong and the Demon Cult warriors standing around him were staring at him with the coldest, most indifferent eyes. It seemed they might suddenly rush forward and hack him to pieces.

‘Damn Demon Cult bastards!’

Hwang Pae was known for charging into battle like a madman, heedless of his own life. But at this moment, his madness was selective.

He had not been afraid when fighting black-robed thugs who hurled curses while bleeding profusely. But when he looked at these men who could calmly stab someone to death with those indifferent eyes, he felt fear.

“Get up.”

Ho Myeong helped up the man who had said Gu Yong had gone to Hwayeong Pavilion.

Then he personally took four subordinates and set out. The fact that he was moving himself showed how important this matter was.

Hwang Pae looked at Geom Muyang again. Compared to these slaughterer-like men, he felt that he might be able to communicate more easily with this man, who at least appeared to be their leader.

Hwang Pae’s momentum faltered.

“When Gu Yong arrives, take him with you. Then I will consider what happened today to have never happened and will not report it to the Sado Alliance.”

Hwang Pae was the kind of man who would sever another person’s ankle if they stepped on his foot while passing by. Yet even after having several teeth broken, he was saying he would let the matter pass as if nothing had happened.

“Very well.”

When Geom Muyang answered readily, Hwang Pae inwardly sighed with relief.

‘He made that promise in front of his subordinates, so he won’t go back on his word.’

He glanced at Myeonggwi. Myeonggwi had been standing there, seemingly lost in thought, but when he sensed Hwang Pae’s gaze, he looked over.

Hwang Pae nodded at him with an expression that said to endure it. He tried his best not to appear awkward.

A short while later, the men who had gone to fetch Gu Yong returned.

But Gu Yong was not with them.

Ho Myeong reported to Geom Muyang with a solemn expression.

“Gu Yong was found stabbed to death in a pleasure house.”

Geom Muyang’s eyes grew cold. They had left the escort agency and rushed here without pause, but the enemy had acted first.

“They’re really starting to irritate me.”

With those words, demonic energy flowed from Geom Muyang’s body, turning the surrounding air cold and heavy.

Ho Myeong could tell that Geom Muyang was truly angry. He and the Ji Dan warriors held their breath, uncertain whether this was a moment when they should intervene, but Hwang Pae, growing impatient, stepped forward.

“Gu Yong’s death was not our fault. So leave now. Keep your promise!”

When Hwang Pae emphasized the promise, Geom Muyang strode toward him.

Startled, Hwang Pae instinctively summoned his internal energy.

But his dantian had already been sealed. When Geom Muyang had thrown him into the courtyard earlier, he had suppressed Hwang Pae’s internal energy.

Half out of his mind, Hwang Pae had not even realized that his internal energy had been sealed.

Crack!

Geom Muyang’s fist slammed into Hwang Pae’s face again. Hwang Pae went flying and rolled across the floor. When he raised himself, three or four more broken teeth fell from his mouth. When he opened his lips, his front teeth were now plainly exposed.

“If you lay a hand on us…”

He had meant to say that the Sado Alliance would not let this pass, but Hwang Pae could not continue.

The eyes of Geom Muyang, who had walked over and was now looking down at him coldly, were terrifying beyond words.

Then words Hwang Pae could never have understood flowed from Geom Muyang’s lips.

“This is all because of my younger brother.”

Thud!

Geom Muyang kicked him in the face, and Hwang Pae collapsed again as though slammed into the floor.

This time, his nasal bone and cheekbone broke. Hwang Pae groaned in pain.

“You think you can shout at us just because you’re someone like this.”

Geom Muyang walked toward him again.

“We have never once been kind to this Murim.”

As Geom Muyang raised his foot as if to crush Hwang Pae’s skull, Hwang Pae screamed for his life.

Geom Muyang looked down at him as though he were a worm, then lowered his foot and turned his gaze toward the Heuksupa warriors kneeling on the ground.

“Is there anyone who knows about this deal?”

None of the Heuksupa warriors opened his mouth.

Geom Muyang spoke coldly.

“Kill them all.”

Ho Myeong and the Ji Dan warriors drew their swords without hesitation.

“Please spare me!”

Screams and pleas erupted, but the warriors raised their swords without mercy.

Just as they were about to wipe out the Heuksupa—

“I know!”

One man shouted. He was the very man who had earlier said that Gu Yong was at the pleasure house. His name was Seok Su. He had been Gu Yong’s friend.

“I heard about this deal.”

Geom Muyang raised a hand and stopped his subordinates.

The swords that had been about to pierce the Heuksupa warriors stopped in unison behind their necks.

Ho Myeong pulled Seok Su to the side, while the warriors surrounded him on all sides.

“What did he tell you?”

Just then, one of the kneeling warriors suddenly sprang to his feet. In his hand was a Jingeok.

“Stop him!”

Ho Myeong shouted and stepped in front of Seok Su.

He knew that the Jingeok could not be stopped at this distance, but Ho Myeong did not dodge.

At the very moment the Jingeok was about to be launched—

Ssshhhhhhk!

A streak of sword light flashed and cleaved through the air.

Slice.

The sound of flesh and bone being severed rang out.

Thud.

The arm of the man who had been about to launch the Jingeok was cut off and fell to the floor.

It was the fifth form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, the Azure Heavens Form.

Geom Muyang had unleashed the supreme quick-draw technique of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

Thwack, thwack!

Ho Myeong rushed over, sealed the blood points of the man whose arm had been severed and was gushing blood, then pressed the blood points around the stump to stop the bleeding.

“Heeheeheehee.”

Even with his arm severed, the man laughed like a madman. He looked exactly like the man who had committed suicide at the escort agency.

Thwack, thwack!

Ho Myeong quickly sealed the man’s speech and sleep points as well. When the man slumped to the ground, the Ji Dan warriors formed a circle around Seok Su and the fallen man, keeping watch in every direction.

Ho Myeong shouted at those on their knees.

“All of you, take off your clothes!”

Ho Myeong strode over and stabbed a hesitant man in the chest.

The remaining men stripped in an instant. Fortunately, none of the naked men carried hidden weapons.

Before leaving, Geom Muyang spoke to Myeonggwi one last time.

“What use is there in blaming the one who deceived you? You’re the fool who got deceived.”

The moment Myeonggwi heard those words, a chill ran down his spine and his entire body shuddered.

In truth, Myeonggwi had sensed something strange earlier, when Hwang Pae suddenly changed his attitude. Hwang Pae had been startled and cut himself off when he heard that the death of Myeonggwi’s wife had been investigated.

And now he understood. Hwang Pae was the one who had plotted and carried out that incident in the past. He had done it to obtain Myeonggwi.

Myeonggwi approached Hwang Pae. With several teeth broken and his nose crushed, Hwang Pae was a complete mess.

“Was it you?”

“What kind of crazy nonsense is that?”

Hwang Pae’s voice trembled with agitation. Today, this was the moment he had feared most—not the slaughterer-like Demon Cult warriors, nor the Demon Cult master who had beaten him senseless.

“I willingly became a bladesman for you.”

For five years, Myeonggwi had served him faithfully. He had not liked Hwang Pae’s actions, but he had wanted to repay the debt he owed for Hwang Pae saving his son.

Yet the more time passed and the more Myeonggwi learned about Hwang Pae, the stronger one doubt became.

This was not a man who would step forward for someone else.

He was a man who tormented, robbed, and killed others. So how could he have killed the wandering martial artists that day and saved Myeonggwi’s son?

Crack! Crack! Crack!

Myeonggwi’s fists slammed into Hwang Pae’s face in rapid succession.

“Tell me! Was it you?”

Hwang Pae grimaced as though in pain.

“It hurts. Stop.”

Blood streamed from his mouth and nose.

But Myeonggwi did not stop punching.

Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud!

The blows were filled with emotion, and Hwang Pae could not endure them any longer, even out of sheer pain. He instinctively realized that Myeonggwi would kill him no matter how much he denied it. If he was going to die anyway—

“How long have you known that bastard? How dare you make this mess just because you believe whatever he says? Then again, you believed my claim that I saved you like a fool.”

Myeonggwi’s face twisted at the words that amounted to a confession.

“What was so important about that wretched woman? After all I did for you. You ungrateful bastard.”

Myeonggwi asked in a cold voice,

“Then why did you leave my son alive?”

“You idiot. So I could keep you under my thumb!”

Hwang Pae grinned with his mangled face.

Stab! Stab! Stab! Stab! Stab!

Myeonggwi’s sword riddled his body.

The bastard’s words were true. That was why Myeonggwi had been unable to leave Hwang Pae despite the inexplicable discomfort and suspicion he had felt. He had been worried about his young son.

His fear that the boy might be endangered if he became caught up in a fight had suppressed his doubts.

Myeonggwi rose, holding his sword with blood dripping from its blade, and looked at Geom Muyang.

“You were right. I was the fool who got deceived. If you’re going to kill me, then kill me.”

He was worried about his son, but at least he was glad he had been able to take revenge before dying.

Geom Muyang looked at him, then silently turned away.

When he began to leave with his subordinates, Myeonggwi urgently called after him.

“You’re just leaving?”

“What if I don’t?”

If it had been his younger brother, he would have settled the matter somehow. He might even have punished those whose evil deeds had gone too far.

But Geom Muyang had no interest in it. Depending on the circumstances, he might kill them all, or he might leave them alive.

Interfering needlessly in the lives of these insignificant people was his meddlesome younger brother’s role.

“If you hadn’t revealed the truth, I would have been used by that filthy bastard for the rest of my life. Thank you.”

Myeonggwi bowed respectfully.

“It wasn’t me who revealed the truth. He revealed it himself.”

Myeonggwi looked puzzled, wondering what Geom Muyang meant.

Geom Muyang did not explain further and went outside.

No record of the incident from five years ago remained. He had merely brought it up to test Hwang Pae, but Hwang Pae had panicked, made a commotion, and destroyed himself. If he ever learned the truth, he would not be able to rest peacefully even in death.

In any case, since it was Myeonggwi who had killed Hwang Pae, this matter would not cause conflict between the Cheonma Divine Cult and the Sado Alliance.

Geom Muyang did not look back even once as he left. Until he had disappeared completely, Myeonggwi remained with his hands clasped respectfully, never raising his head.

* * *

Geom Muyang and the Ji Dan warriors left the Heuksupa and arrived at the nearest branch.

“Find out everything they know, even if you have to torture them. Use every means and method at your disposal.”

“Yes, understood.”

At Geom Muyang’s command, the warriors took the two men they had brought to the branch’s interrogation room.

Next, he gave Ho Myeong an order.

“Send urgent dispatches to every other Ji Dan and branch in Honam and summon their elite forces.”

Today’s events had made one thing certain. They were a far more meticulous and powerful organization than he had imagined.

Geom Muyang recalled the man who had laughed like a madman even after his arm had been severed. Hwang Pae had only pretended to be insane. That man was the true lunatic.

To deal with men like that, they needed to properly assemble their own forces as well.

“Have them equip every escort armor stored at the Ji Dan and branches before sending them out.”

“Yes, understood.”

Ho Myeong answered energetically, then cautiously added,

“But if we do that, the Murim Alliance and the Sado Alliance will become alarmed.”

This matter had nothing to do with his younger brother, regardless of how close his brother might be to the heirs of the Murim Alliance and the Sado Alliance.

Setting aside his personal anger at the fact that he had nearly killed his own younger brother with his own hands, this was an official mission to capture those who had forged the Divine Cult’s hidden weapons.

“There’s no need to explain everything to the Murim Alliance and the Sado Alliance. Don’t ask for their understanding, either. Convey only this.”

Geom Muyang added curtly,

“Cheonma Divine Cult operation in progress.”

Chapter 617: Leave This Murim to Big Brother for Now
----------------------------------------------------
“They were the ones who approached us first.”

Seoksu, a friend of Guyong—the man who had handled the counterfeit hidden weapon dealings for the Black Water Sect—confessed everything he knew without even needing to be asked.

Seoksu was terrified. He had truly never dreamed that a day would come in his life when he would be dragged into the Demonic Cult and interrogated.

Guyong had been stabbed to death at a pleasure house, and moreover, the man who had tried to launch a hidden weapon earlier had clearly been trying to kill him. He could not forget the sight of that man laughing like a madman even after his arm had been cut off.

“They said they had weapons from the Demonic Cult and asked if we were interested in buying them.”

The man seated in front of Seoksu was Branch Altar Master Ho Myeong. The matter was too important to leave to his subordinates, so he had personally stepped in to conduct the interrogation.

Seoksu, having seen earlier that Ho Myeong held a high position, was even more nervous.

“They even gave Guyong money to make the deal happen.”

Ho Myeong, who had only been listening to him, finally opened his mouth.

“How much?”

“He didn’t say the amount, but from the look of it, he seemed to have received several thousand nyang. The bastard was in such a good mood that he went to Hwayeong Pavilion and bought drinks lavishly.”

Ho Myeong did not believe all of it.

The most important thing right now was discerning whether this bastard before him was making up nonsense in order to survive.

He wouldn’t lie just because he was terrified? Nonsense.

Having lived his entire life as a demonic practitioner, he had seen many people.

Terrified people lied well, too.

He had even seen a man clutching a sword wound with his hand, tears streaming down his face, while lying as brazenly as if it were nothing.

One must never look down on a human’s instinct to survive.

“Tell me about the one who gave him the money.”

“It wasn’t a man.”

“What?”

“He said it was a woman.”

That was unexpected even to Ho Myeong. A woman had approached them?

“Tell me everything you know about that woman.”

“All I know is that she was a woman. That is everything I know.”

In that instant, Ho Myeong’s voice dropped coldly.

“Then why did you say you knew about this matter?”

Seoksu was flustered.

“I thought even that much would count as information.”

“A woman gave him a large sum of money? How are we supposed to find that woman with this information?”

A chill as cold as his voice seeped from Ho Myeong’s eyes.

“You thought you could deceive us by saying something vague and passing it off as enough.”

“No, no!”

Seoksu waved his hands frantically, his innocent-looking face seeming as if he might burst into tears at any moment.

But Ho Myeong knew very well what the bastards of the Black Water Sect were like. They were leeches who sucked the lifeblood from people.

Ho Myeong took out a small bottle from his robes and set it down before him.

“Drink it.”

Seoksu asked in terror.

“What is this?”

“A confession drug.”

Seoksu had heard of it before. A confession drug that, once drunk, clouded the mind and made one answer every question asked.

And he also knew that confession drugs had side effects. If one was unlucky, one might go mad, or sometimes even die.

“Please spare me!”

Seoksu’s face turned pale.

“If I have to force you to drink it, you’ll die even if you confess.”

Seoksu reached toward the medicine bottle with trembling hands.

‘I have to remember. Please.’

Seoksu desperately recalled what Guyong had said that day. They had met and exchanged some crude, pointless lewd jokes. Then they had gone to Hwayeong Pavilion together.

Ah, right. While drinking with the courtesans, Guyong had said a few more words about that matter. What had he said then?

That desperation finally created a miracle. He set down the confession drug he had been about to drink and shouted.

“Ah! That’s right! He said the woman had burn marks on the back of her hand.”

He had remembered Guyong saying that her face was pretty and her figure was good, but it was a shame there were burn marks on her hand. It was something the bastard had said while holding a courtesan’s hand.

“Burn marks?”

“Yes, he definitely said they were burn scars. I’m certain.”

Seoksu watched Ho Myeong’s reaction with eyes full of expectation. Since he had remembered something important, he thought he would not have to take the confession drug.

Ho Myeong softened only his gaze and his words as he spoke to him.

“I understand. Now drink.”

These damned Demonic Cult bastards!

* * *

Half a quarter later, Ho Myeong emerged from the interrogation room.

Behind him, Seoksu could be seen. He was still sitting in the chair in a dazed state.

Ho Myeong headed toward another investigation room a short distance from the one he had just left. There, the investigation of the assassin whose arm had been cut off was in full swing.

The one-armed assassin was truly out of his mind, so a normal interrogation could not be conducted. Torture did not work either, so rather than trying to hear from him who was behind it, they were in the midst of determining by what method he had been brainwashed.

Geom Muyang was watching the scene from behind with his arms crossed.

Ho Myeong approached carefully and reported.

“The intermediary was a woman, and they say she had burn marks on the back of her hand. Since the testimony obtained with the confession drug was the same, it seems to be true. I will relay everything we heard to Tongcheon Pavilion and have them search for that woman.”

With only that, it would be like searching for a needle on a sandy beach, but they had at least found a clue.

Just as he turned to leave, Geom Muyang abruptly spoke.

“That woman may be someone who works at an ironworks.”

Why would he say she worked at an ironworks? Ho Myeong wondered, then belatedly realized something.

“Ah! Because of the burns!”

At Ho Myeong’s words, Geom Muyang nodded. At the main cult, he had mostly worked with the ironworks. Because of that, he had encountered many people who worked there. For those who handled iron and fire, burns were common.

“I will tell them there is a possibility she works at an ironworks.”

Ho Myeong looked at Geom Muyang with a newly impressed expression. Once one heard it, it seemed obviously connected, but connecting the two upon simply hearing it was not an easy thing.

“The number of women working at ironworks is relatively small, and if there is also the characteristic of a scar on the back of her hand? Perhaps Tongcheon Pavilion really might be able to find her. If they do, it will be thanks to you, First Young Master.”

It was not flattery; he was genuinely impressed. Just what kind of person was that young master, to have defeated such a First Young Master and become the Young Cult Leader?

At that moment, the martial artist who had been examining the body of the man with the severed arm finished his investigation and turned back to them. Among the branch altar martial artists mobilized this time, he was someone well versed in such esoteric arts.

“It appears to be an esoteric art that plants commands into the subconscious, allowing the subject to live normally in ordinary times, but once a certain special condition is met, they carry out only the command.”

In other words, the reason this man, who had been kneeling earlier, had suddenly turned into a madman was because a special situation had occurred: Seoksu was about to reveal the secret.

“Moreover, once the esoteric art activates, they immediately lose their reason and are made to commit suicide.”

It meant that if they had not swiftly sealed his ma blood point after his arm was cut off, this man would also have killed himself.

“The one who implanted this esoteric art is no ordinary expert.”

They had made the counterfeit hidden weapons almost indistinguishable from the genuine articles, and even used such an esoteric art. These were not people merely trying to make money by selling fakes.

“To determine exactly what esoteric art was used, we must escort him to the main cult and investigate there.”

Geom Muyang looked at the man laughing in the interrogation room.

“Is there any chance he can return to normal?”

“With an esoteric art of this level, the person who cast it must undo it personally.”

“Then it can never be undone.”

There was no way someone who used an esoteric art to control people like puppets and make them kill themselves would obediently release it.

Shiiing.

A streak of sword light shot from Geom Muyang, and the man who had launched the surprise attack died as his heart was cut apart.

“We’ll be seeing plenty of those under esoteric arts from now on. And…”

Geom Muyang walked out of the place with cold eyes and said,

“I’ll hear what kind of esoteric art it is directly from him.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk opened the enormous doors and went inside.

A plaza spread out before his eyes.

Ninety-nine enormous bookcases filled the place.

On those shelves were countless peerless secret manuals capable of stirring the hearts of martial artists.

It was none other than the Heavenly Demon Library, where he had once obtained the Four Steps of the Wind God.

“Father! I came in here without your permission! I might make a mess in here. I might sit anywhere I want and even eat food. So hurry up and come out!”

Once he became the acting cult leader, he could now naturally enter places that had previously required the Heavenly Demon’s permission. Among those many places, the first one he entered was this Heavenly Demon Library.

Geom Mugeuk walked between the bookcases, pulling out books here and there.

He was not choosing carefully; he picked books that caught his eye wherever he saw them.

There were books ranging from discussions on the principles of martial studies to the advice of masters for real combat. There were histories of the martial world, and foundations covering martial arts as a whole.

There were manuals explaining qi and blood points, methods for balancing the body, ways for a martial artist to use the body, basic attacks and defenses. Methods for predicting an opponent’s movements and finding openings. Methods for circulating and applying true qi.

He chose some because he liked the title, others because he liked the calligraphy written on the cover, and others simply because he wanted to read them once.

In that way, he chose more than twenty books.

Snap!

Then, when he flicked his finger, his surroundings changed, becoming a green field.

Sssst.

A large desk and chair appeared in the middle of the field.

When he lightly gestured again, a cool breeze blew.

Geom Mugeuk activated the Temporal Heaven Secret Art.

Now, Geom Mugeuk’s Temporal Heaven Secret Art gave him seven times more time than the outside world. When he had faced the Sword King, it had been six times, but he had made progress in the meantime.

Now, one shijin outside was seven shijin here.

‘You’re the only one I can rely on!’

Geom Mugeuk piled the books on the desk and began to read.

They were of many different kinds, but one thing was certain. These were all carefully selected books worthy of being placed in the Heavenly Demon Library.

Geom Mugeuk read them one by one.

The reason for this bout of reading was this.

Geom Mugeuk had told Sama Myeong that he had anxiety, but the cause of an anxiety even greater than his brother’s lay in the Nine Flames Demonic Art.

The Nine Flames Demonic Art had reached the eighth star.

Even his current achievement was faster than that of any cult leader in history, incomparable to them, but Geom Mugeuk was not satisfied with it.

‘The crisis is going from the eighth star to the ninth.’

It felt as if he were standing before a massive wall he could not cross.

If he crossed this wall, there would be an even greater wall beyond it. For now, he had to cross this wall first.

Geom Mugeuk decided to read the books in the Heavenly Demon Library as his method for overcoming this wall.

Until now, he had overcome barriers through brutal training, repeating the Nine Flames Demonic Art again and again. At other moments, he had overcome them through real battles that transcended death. He had also overcome them through his father’s teachings.

And this time, he intended to overcome it by diligently reading martial arts manuals here.

Why this method, of all things?

It was because his instincts had led him to it.

If his father entering closed-door cultivation, and his becoming acting cult leader and thereby gaining free access to this place, could be called fate, then it was fate.

There was also one more reason.

After meeting the Sword King, he had begun thinking a great deal more about the fundamentals of martial arts. The process of creating martial arts by applying the Yellow Dragon Sword Art had probably had a great influence.

Geom Mugeuk trusted his instincts. Just as wanting to eat meat was a signal that the body needed the nutrients contained in meat, if he now wanted to read all sorts of books about martial arts, then this must be the time when he needed them.

Geom Mugeuk read with an easy heart. He did not read with the thought that he absolutely had to gain something. If a book became too boring while he was reading, he simply closed it and read the next one. At Geom Mugeuk’s level, what content could be difficult for him? He read any book smoothly, without getting stuck.

One shijin, two shijin, three shijin… seven shijin.

How long had he been immersed in reading like that? From outside came the voice of his guard captain, Jeok Yeon.

“A message has arrived from Tongcheon Pavilion.”

Then Geom Mugeuk’s surroundings began to change.

Sssssss.

The countless bookcases of the Heavenly Demon Library reappeared around him.

Geom Mugeuk took the books he had brought out of the Space-Time Transposition Art and began to walk.

Whirik. Whiririk, whik.

The secret manuals floated up into the air and flew off in different directions.

The books slipped into their original places. It looked like no great feat, but simultaneously inserting the books with telekinetic force was, in truth, a divine feat found in none of the martial arts manuals in this place.

Geom Mugeuk came out of the Heavenly Demon Library.

Jeok Yeon relayed the news sent from Tongcheon Pavilion.

“They say the First Young Master is gathering the elite martial artists of Hunan.”

It was a report that the situation there was serious, but Geom Mugeuk instead smiled.

“It seems those bastards have thoroughly pissed my brother off.”

The fact that his brother was on high alert was actually reassuring. At the very least, he would not be careless.

“And they say he sent only the words ‘the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult is conducting an operation’ to the Murim Alliance and the Evil Dao Alliance.”

At that report, Geom Mugeuk laughed in good spirits.

“If Father were here, he would have truly liked that.”

Father, your favorite eldest son is turning the martial world upside down.

If he teased his father about this, his father would certainly look at him with that characteristic sneer of his.

And he would have been very pleased to hear news of his older brother.

“My brother is my brother, and I’m so hungry I can’t bear it. Since I came out anyway, I should eat something before going back in.”

Jeok Yeon looked at Geom Mugeuk with a strange expression. It had only been one shijin since Geom Mugeuk had eaten and gone in, yet he was already that hungry again.

Another good thing about becoming acting cult leader.

He could eat meals prepared by the Heavenly Demon Hall’s chef.

“The thing I envy Father most for isn’t the Heavenly Demon Treasury or the Heavenly Demon Library—it’s you, Chef!”

And so, he was able to see the Heavenly Demon Hall chef’s bright smile for the first time in a long while.

Geom Mugeuk ate his fill, even packed up food the chef made for him, and then went back into the Heavenly Demon Library.

Geom Mugeuk chose books from the shelves. This time, too, he did not choose carefully. He chose according to his mood. It did not matter.

After all, he intended to read every book in the Heavenly Demon Library before coming out.

‘Father, I’ll leave this martial world to my brother for a while and enter closed-door training in the Heavenly Demon Library.’

Chapter 618: I’m Worried You’ll Handle It Too Well
--------------------------------------------------
The Yueyang Branch of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was in a constant state of tension.

When the First Young Master arrived leading the elites of the Changsha Sub-Branch, everyone had thought it was merely a passing wind.

If there was urgent business, he might stop by. In a life lived as a demonic practitioner, how could the wind not blow once in a while? So long as you were not swept away by it, that was enough.

But the wind kept blowing.

The next day, the martial artists of the Pingjiang Branch arrived.

It was nearby, so they knew its circumstances well. The strongest martial artists of the Pingjiang Branch had arrived. And they had even opened the armory and donned protective armor.

Beginning with them, all the elites of Hunan started gathering.

The elites of the Yiyang Branch arrived, followed by the martial artists of the Hengshan Sub-Branch. Someone said to be rising spectacularly in the Hengshan Sub-Branch these days came as well, and they could even see the face of the monstrous new recruit who had joined the Yongxing Branch.

Elites from other sub-branches and branches arrived one after another.

Only then did they understand. This was no passing wind, but a typhoon. Their own Yueyang Branch had entered the eye of the storm.

And today, at last, a piece of news was delivered into the heart of that typhoon.

Ho Myeong entered Geom Muyang’s room at a brisk pace.

“Word has come from Tongtian Pavilion. They say they have found the woman from the ironworks with a burn on the back of her hand.”

Even if the number of women working in ironworks was small, surely there were more than one or two such women in the Central Plains. And yet Tongtian Pavilion had produced results.

Geom Muyang did not doubt those results. If they said they had found her, then they had found her.

Geom Muyang sprang to his feet.

“Let’s go.”

At Geom Muyang’s reaction, as if he intended to set out and seize her at once, Ho Myeong wore a troubled expression.

“The thing is, the place that woman belongs to is……”

Ho Myeong added cautiously.

“She is attached to the ironworks of the Martial Alliance headquarters.”

“!”

Geom Muyang’s face hardened. The most troublesome name to deal with had come up.

Ho Myeong continued relaying the information sent by Tongtian Pavilion.

“The woman’s name is Guo Yong, age twenty-seven. She is one of the three master artisans of the Martial Alliance headquarters’ ironworks.”

“A master artisan at twenty-seven?”

To become a master artisan of the Martial Alliance ironworks at such a young age—and as a woman at that—meant not only talent, but effort pushed to the extreme.

‘And yet she made a counterfeit?’

She had even met Gu Yong personally to sell the item. There were parts of this he could not understand.

“It has also been confirmed that she left the ironworks around the time she met Gu Yong. The problem is that they lost track of her movements after she returned to Wuhan. They suspect word of the Black Hand Society matter here may have reached her.”

Judging by her movements, she seemed certain to be the woman they were looking for.

“In the end, we’ll have to go to Wuhan.”

At Geom Muyang’s words, spoken almost to himself, Ho Myeong could not answer.

The place where the Martial Alliance headquarters was located.

To the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, Wuhan was truly forbidden ground.

Moreover, even if they sent word in advance, entering Wuhan with the elites of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was impossible. The Martial Alliance would never permit such an operation.

“I will enter Wuhan alone.”

There was a reason for that decision.

“With all the elites gathered here, they won’t expect me to enter Wuhan by myself.”

Ho Myeong spoke firmly.

“Absolutely not.”

“Then is there another way?”

Of course, there was no other way. Even so, sending Geom Muyang there alone was unthinkable.

“Then at least allow me and only my subordinates to accompany you.”

Ho Myeong stared at Geom Muyang with eyes that said he would never back down.

“If you intend to go alone, cut us down first.”

Geom Muyang looked out the window for a moment, lost in thought. Before long, he nodded and gave permission.

“Very well. Then let’s inform only the branch leader here and slip out quietly.”

It meant they would not tell any of the other martial artists here.

But they had to inform the branch leader. If people started searching because the First Young Master had disappeared, rumors would spread in no time.

“Understood. I will find a discreet way for us to leave.”

At that, Geom Muyang gestured for him to come and look out the window.

Ho Myeong walked over and looked outside beside him.

Carriages and carts delivering goods were arriving in a line.

As the number of people staying here increased, naturally, so did the number of people coming and going. To feed this many people, they had to buy a tremendous amount of ingredients and food, and they needed many more people to wash dishes and clean.

Thus, several times a day, carriages and carts carried in food and people.

Ho Myeong understood Geom Muyang’s intention.

“That’s true. People don’t pay much attention to carts going out.”

Even if incoming carriages were searched thoroughly, outgoing empty carriages and carts were not inspected nearly as strictly.

* * *

A few days later, a large carriage was nearing Wuhan.

“We will arrive in Wuhan soon.”

The people packed into the cargo compartment in the back were Geom Muyang, Ho Myeong, and the elites of the Changsha Sub-Branch.

They had taken off their martial uniforms and dressed like ordinary laborers. Some were bare-chested, and others had disguised themselves as old men with wrinkles and beards. The carriage was disguised as one transporting workers to a construction site in Wuhan.

They were more tense than ever. Though they were elites of their sub-branch, none of them had experience carrying out an operation in front of the Martial Alliance headquarters.

Rather than reassuring his subordinates, Ho Myeong emphasized the danger.

“As you know, this operation is extremely dangerous.”

It was, quite literally, an operation that risked their lives.

“We will have to face two kinds of enemies. Those who made the counterfeits, and the righteous sects as well.”

In some ways, the masters of the Martial Alliance were more dangerous than the bastards making the counterfeits. How many Martial Alliance masters bore grudges against the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult?

Crucially, because they had not informed the Martial Alliance of this matter, they would have no excuse even if they were killed by their hands. On top of that, this was an operation undertaken with none other than the First Young Master.

“Our mission is one. Follow the First Young Master’s orders and protect him.”

Ho Myeong looked at Geom Muyang and spoke with force.

“We will serve you well so that we may all escape safely.”

He left out the rest. Even if all of us have to die. Ho Myeong’s loyalty was genuine.

Geom Muyang gazed at Wuhan as it drew gradually closer and answered indifferently.

“I will not be returning down this road alone.”

* * *

When the Blood Heaven Blade Demon lifted his head and looked out the window, something was floating through the air toward him.

What came drifting through the air to hover before the startled Blood Heaven Blade Demon was a book.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon received the book that had flown to him. A strange light passed through his eyes when he confirmed the title, and he immediately opened it and began to read.

“No, what if the pages had been coated with poison? How can you just grab it and start reading like that? You even lick your finger sometimes when you turn the pages.”

The person who emerged from the darkness in the distance, slandering him with the accusation that he licked his fingers, was Geom Mugeuk.

Without even looking at Geom Mugeuk, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon turned the pages and said,

“Dying while reading a book wouldn’t be so bad.”

Geom Mugeuk shouted joyfully and ran into the room.

“Elder! I missed you!”

As Geom Mugeuk rushed in to embrace him, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon stood up and respectfully cupped his fist in salute.

“I greet the Acting Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk, who had been charging forward, stopped awkwardly.

“Why are you being like this? Between us?”

“Do you have any orders to give, Acting Cult Leader?”

“It’s me. Me. The handsome Young Cult Leader who shook off the other seven Demon Venerates begging me to visit them the moment I returned from leaving the cult, and came running to see you first. This is really too much!”

Only then did the Blood Heaven Blade Demon relax his expression and sit back down.

“Too much? You’re the one who’s too much, only coming now to greet me.”

“That’s why I brought a gift, isn’t it? It’s a book you wanted to read, right? And there it was, right in the Heavenly Demon Library.”

The book he had brought was a collection of essays left behind by an old master. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon had read several books that master had written, but this was the only one he had been unable to obtain.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon kept his gaze on the book as he spoke.

“Don’t you know you’re not allowed to take books out of there?”

“And yet you’re reading it so diligently.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon quickly turned another page.

“Because this is my only chance to read it.”

“No, please read it slowly. I’m giving it to you as a gift.”

“You’re giving this to me?”

“When you say something like that, you should look at me while you say it!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s gaze was still fixed on the book.

“This is not a book you can give away without the Cult Leader’s permission.”

“Aren’t I the Cult Leader?”

“The Acting Cult Leader.”

“Then I’ll change the cult rules. Aren’t temporary cult rules still cult rules? And this is rude to Father as well. ‘There is a book I wish to read, so please allow me to check whether it is in the Heavenly Demon Library.’ What was so difficult about saying that?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon silently read the book. His special relationship with his father was probably not something easily explained in words.

“I heard you’ve been holed up in the Heavenly Demon Library lately?”

“I’m reading with the thought that if I don’t read now, when will I?”

“A good attitude.”

A satisfied smile formed on the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s lips.

“My decision to read books this time was entirely because of you, Elder.”

“Because of me?”

“After meeting you, I learned the pleasure of reading.”

“You didn’t even read that much. You always fell asleep after a few pages.”

Geom Mugeuk pretended not to hear and continued.

“Our elder must be this strong because he has read so many books. That must be why he is so impressive. If I read many books too, might I be able to cross the wall of martial learning that blocks me now?”

Because he truly believed that, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon even nodded as he said,

“No matter how high the wall, if you stack books into stairs, one day you will be able to climb to the top.”

He meant that Geom Mugeuk should step on those books and cross over.

“If you cannot climb it, it is because you have not read enough books.”

“I’m probably the first Young Cult Leader to try to break through to the next realm of the Ninefire Demonic Art through books.”

After a brief pause, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon said,

“That is why your Ninefire Demonic Art will be the strongest.”

When Geom Mugeuk looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, he was still looking at the book.

This time, when the Blood Heaven Blade Demon glanced up at him, Geom Mugeuk was looking at the bookshelf.

“There are many books here I haven’t seen before.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon might have taken his words as flattery or a joke, but in truth, his influence had played a large part in Geom Mugeuk’s decision to enter the Heavenly Demon Library.

Geom Mugeuk pulled one book after another from the shelf and examined them.

How much time had passed like that?

“Take this book back.”

“Why? I told you it’s a gift.”

“No need. I’ll ask the Cult Leader for it later.”

Geom Mugeuk gave him a suspicious look.

“You’ve already finished reading it, haven’t you?”

When the Blood Heaven Blade Demon flinched, Geom Mugeuk pressed the attack.

“Of course you must have finished it. Just now, you were only half listening to me because you were reading, weren’t you? Right? You didn’t even look at my face once, did you? Did that book happen to have a subtitle like this? My Young Cult Leader, Traded for a Book!”

Only then did the Blood Heaven Blade Demon smile broadly. It had been so long since he had read a good book, and exchanging jokes with Geom Mugeuk like this was truly enjoyable.

Just then, Jeok Yeon’s report came from outside.

“Strategist Sima requests to see you at Tongtian Pavilion.”

Until now, reports had been delivered directly, telling him what had happened. But asking to meet at Tongtian Pavilion meant that something had occurred.

“Elder, I’ll be going. I’ll see you later.”

“Take the book!”

Geom Mugeuk received the book the Blood Heaven Blade Demon carefully sent over with telekinetic force, tucked it into his robes, and vanished from sight.

After he left, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was about to sit back down when he noticed a book he had not seen before lying where Geom Mugeuk had been.

As if he had anticipated that it would not be accepted, he had even prepared a note.

—The cult won’t collapse just because one poetry collection goes missing from the Heavenly Demon Library.

That book, too, was one the Blood Heaven Blade Demon had been unable to obtain and had never read. In fact, it was a book he had wanted even more than the one from earlier.

Geom Mugeuk had remembered even this book, which he had once mentioned in passing.

“You are still the same, even after becoming Acting Cult Leader.”

Most likely, he would not change even after becoming Cult Leader.

With a deep smile, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon sat down and began to read. This time, he began turning the pages very slowly. Even licking his finger once as he did.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk had already heard the news that his elder brother had headed to Wuhan.

The reason Sima Myeong had called him today was because someone else had headed to Wuhan as well.

A single flag rose on the terrain map. The character written on it was han (寒), cold. For Tongcheon Pavilion to represent an expert with a flag meant that the expert’s skill was truly exceptional.

“Hanwolgaek is headed to Wuhan.”

Hanwolgaek was an expert of the orthodox faction who had lost his family to a demonic practitioner in the past. For that reason, his grudge against the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult ran deep.

“Coincidentally, after the Eldest Young Master departed for Wuhan, he too set out for Wuhan.”

It could have been a coincidence, or it could have been anything but.

If that had been all, they would not have summoned Geom Mugeuk.

“Hanwolgaek is a person of concern to our cult, so we have always kept track of his movements. However……”

A new flag was placed on the terrain map. This time, it was the character byeok (碧), azure.

“While investigating another matter by chance, we discovered that Byeoksan Sword is also heading to Wuhan.”

He, too, was an expert worthy of having his own distinctive flag. The problem was the timing. Of all times, just as the Eldest Young Master was headed for Wuhan, two renowned experts of the orthodox faction were making their way there as well.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze deepened.

“If they’re going because of my brother, then there’s someone behind this who can move at least two flags.”

He did not know how those two experts were connected to the mastermind behind this affair, but in any case, they were figures who represented the orthodox faction. Even if they attacked first, they were not people who could simply be killed without consequence. Especially not in Wuhan.

Sama Myeong spoke carefully.

“At least two.”

What if other experts, ones Tongcheon Pavilion had not identified, joined them as well?

“Please order the Eldest Young Master to return.”

He was saying it because his instincts as a strategist told him something was wrong, but—

“My brother won’t come back.”

Geom Mugeuk stared at Sama Myeong. Even in this situation, Sama Myeong was not trying to send him out. Then again, the more dangerous the situation became, the more he would refuse to do so.

Though Geom Mugeuk had not even asked to be sent, Sama Myeong spoke first, firmly.

“No.”

Geom Mugeuk obediently nodded. He knew well that Sama Myeong considered protecting the successor of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult more important than any other duty.

In truth, even if Sama Myeong had asked him to go, it would have been something he needed to think over. To go himself when the exact nature of the danger had not yet been revealed? That was a poor strategy. It would mean ignoring his brother’s pride and his brother’s request, choosing only to save his life.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the green Demon Venerable flag on the terrain map. Sama Myeong’s gaze naturally followed.

“Are you worried that the Poison King may not be able to handle it?”

“No. I’m worried he’ll handle it far too well.”

Sama Myeong understood what he meant and silently nodded.

What if a situation unfolded in which the Poison King carried out a massacre in Wuhan?

The strongest deterrent against war in the current martial world was, in effect, standing on the front line.

Both the Poison King and his brother were far too hardline. People who would never bend. They needed someone who could stand in their place.

“Please inform one person of the current situation.”

And so the news was conveyed to that one person.

A short while later, the strategist who had gone to deliver the message at Geom Mugeuk’s command returned.

Upon his return, he placed a new flag on the terrain map. It was the flag of the person his brother trusted most, and the person who understood his brother best. It was a golden flag.

“Lord Mabul departed the cult for Wuhan just moments ago.”

Chapter 619: Why Did You Make It Without Our Permission?
--------------------------------------------------------
Mabul was heading toward Muhan.

When he heard the information sent from Tongcheongak, he judged that he had to go.

Han Wolgaek and Byeoksan Geom.

They were opponents not easily handled by the Great Young Master. Mabul knew all too well that Han Wolgaek’s grudge against the main cult was as deep as the sea.

Just then, someone shouted from far behind.

“Wait a momeeeeeent.”

At that familiar voice riding the wind, Mabul halted his lightness skill.

Whooooosh.

The one who rushed up from behind fast as light and landed was Geom Mugeuk.

“For someone with such short legs, you certainly are fast.”

“So now that you’re the acting cult leader, you openly resort to personal attacks.”

“Sitting on the throne naturally makes one arrogant. Everyone, kneel!”

Though their conversation was filled with negative words, the expressions of the two looking at each other were bright.

“What is it?”

“You gave me work, so I should at least come greet you.”

“You didn’t chase after me because I left without greeting the acting cult leader, did you?”

“As expected! You know the nature of power all too well! No matter how much you carve a stone statue, Lord Mabul, you are the very embodiment of lust for power.”

Mabul snorted. He could tell Geom Mugeuk was deliberately provoking him. Like the saying that one carves a stone statue to sell it, he was telling Mabul to return to those old days when he had tried to seize power again. Vividly, without settling down.

“I’ve come because I have a request.”

Geom Mugeuk unwound the Supreme Heavenly Silkworm Silk wrapped around the hilt of the Black Demon Sword. That which had been shared with Geukak the Lesser Demon, and shared with Chwima as well, was now being passed to a new person.

“Please wrap this around my brother’s heart.”

Mabul received the Supreme Heavenly Silkworm Silk. At first, he had not believed it—the words of Geom Mugeuk saying he would fight for succession while saving his brother. But now he believed.

Yes, perhaps even then, something like this had been wrapped around the Great Young Master’s heart. Though invisible to the eye, something that had protected his heart, something even more powerful than what he now held in his hand.

Mabul’s gaze turned to the Black Demon Sword. Some of the Supreme Heavenly Silkworm Silk still remained on the hilt.

Looking at it, Mabul abruptly spoke.

“What about me?”

As if he had heard a joke, Geom Mugeuk laughed aloud.

But Mabul did not laugh.

“Is only the Great Young Master’s heart a heart?”

“But Lord Mabul, someone like you doesn’t need something like this.”

“I’m a person who bleeds when cut by a blade too. Did you think gold powder would pour out of me instead of blood?”

“It wouldn’t look cool if a Demon Lord wrapped something like this around himself.”

“Have you never given this to another Demon Lord?”

“…….”

“As I thought. You just didn’t want to give it to me.”

In the end, Geom Mugeuk pretended he couldn’t win and unwound the rest, handing it all over.

“Why take it when you won’t even use it?”

“I will use it.”

“You absolutely won’t, Lord Mabul!”

“We’ll see.”

If Geom Mugeuk had given it all from the start, saying half was for his brother and half for Lord Mabul, Mabul would surely have said he didn’t need it.

That was why he had deliberately employed a contrarian ploy. Whether Mabul had guessed that intention and accepted it while pretending not to know, he could not tell.

Mabul looked at the leather bag hanging on Geom Mugeuk’s back and spoke.

“I suppose you brought that to give to me as well. Hand it over.”

How quick-witted he was. Inside the leather bag were three sets of protective armor taken from the Heavenly Demon Armory. Among them was a small-sized set of protective armor for Mabul.

“Why three sets?”

“If you happen to meet him, please give one to the Poison King as well.”

Mabul stared at Geom Mugeuk. With a look that said, ‘You must already know.’

“That man won’t wear it.”

“Yes, he would refuse.”

“Then why?”

“Anyone would think I sent Lord Mabul because you’re close to my brother, but that is not why I asked you.”

“Then what is the reason?”

The true value of Mabul in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes.

“To make the impossible possible. It’s what you do better than anyone else.”

Mabul, who had been looking at Geom Mugeuk for a moment, began slowly winding the Supreme Heavenly Silkworm Silk around both wrists.

“You aren’t sending me because of your brother, are you? And it wouldn’t be to make the Poison King wear protective armor either.”

He had accurately grasped Geom Mugeuk’s intention.

“You want me to stop that man, the Poison King?”

Instead of answering, Geom Mugeuk smiled a smile of affirmation.

If Han Wolgaek, who held a deep grudge against the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, went mad and ran amok, the Poison King would eventually have to step in.

The moment one let their guard down at his boyishly fresh appearance and started spouting nonsense, the principles everyone had to uphold would shatter.

The principle of not angering the Poison King.

Because if things unfortunately went awry, a war could break out.

“Because only Lord Mabul can stop the Poison King.”

“Why me?”

He had expected a grand reason, but.

“Isn’t Lord Mabul the King of Poisonous Herb Gathering?”

At the unexpected reason, Mabul laughed.

The playfulness vanished from Geom Mugeuk’s face. He bowed his head respectfully.

“I’m sorry for asking you to do something so difficult.”

Having wound all the Supreme Heavenly Silkworm Silk, Mabul strapped the leather bag onto his back. Because Mabul was short, strapping it on obscured almost his entire body.

“So now that you’re the cult leader, you start by handing me luggage.”

“It’s a burden that only a little giant can shoulder.”

“If you couldn’t even talk.”

Mabul turned and began running again.

Geom Mugeuk stood in that spot, watching until he completely disappeared. Honestly, watching was harder than going out to fight himself.

But even if anxiety made sleep impossible, there were times when one simply had to watch. Like a parent’s heart toward their child, because it was when we stood alone that we grew. The opponent, and even the one watching.

Geom Mugeuk turned in the opposite direction from where he had disappeared.

“Then shall I go read that book I left unfinished?”

In a flash, Geom Mugeuk’s figure shrank to a dot and vanished.

* * *

The carriage carrying Geom Muyang and her party slowly passed through the central streets of Muhan.

Disguised as construction workers, they sat clinging to the luggage compartment of the old carriage, looking around the streets.

A middle-aged martial artist walking with long strides, a long sword on his back and robe hem fluttering; an old man sitting in a tavern, stroking his white beard as he shared drinks; those young martial artists standing around laughing and chatting with such youthful faces; and the Murim Alliance martial artists in uniforms passing by with sharp gazes—in their eyes, everyone they saw was of the orthodox faction.

In the distance, the magnificent buildings of the Murim Alliance’s main compound and the giant martial artist statue symbolizing the chivalrous warrior could be seen.

Homyeong did not show it outwardly, but inside he was nervous.

He had only heard of it; it was his first time actually coming before the Murim Alliance’s main compound. And how could it be only him? The subordinates who had come with him were the same.

It was not just the people.

The countless sects, large and small, visible along the way were all orthodox sects that followed the Murim Alliance.

If a fight were to break out, orthodox martial artists would pour in from all directions.

Truly, they were passing through a Dragon Pool and Tiger’s Den.

The place they arrived at was a large manor.

It was not a secluded place far away, but slightly removed from the main street. Other houses were crammed right next to it, and a short walk away was the main street they had just passed.

A place where the magnificent buildings of the Murim Alliance’s main compound looked even larger. Truly, it was right at the throat of the Murim Alliance.

Everyone got down from the carriage and entered the manor. As they went inside, parts of the manor buildings were collapsed here and there, as if workers had been called for repair work.

“Welcome, come in quickly!”

The one who greeted them was the proprietor of the place.

“I intend to splendidly renovate this place.”

At the same time, he was a martial artist from Tongcheongak.

“Come, come. Rest inside today and we’ll start work tomorrow.”

Tongcheongak had purchased this manor and disguised it as if they were doing expansion work. It was so that Geom Muyang and the branch martial artists who had arrived in Muhan disguised as workers would not arouse any suspicion.

Truly, Tongcheongak had spared no support for Geom Muyang.

As soon as the manor gate closed, the branch martial artists surveyed their surroundings. Over the walls and up to the roofs. After confirming there were no watching eyes, they took out the swords and protective armor hidden beneath the carriage floor and went inside the building.

Once inside the building, a middle-aged man finally introduced himself.

“I am Hua Yin, from Tongcheongak. It is an honor to serve a precious guest.”

Normally, martial artists belonging to Tongcheongak revealed themselves by their operation names, but only this time did he reveal his real name. Although he had been pushed back in the succession struggle, his opponent was the flesh and blood of the Heavenly Demon. That was why he himself, the one in charge of Muhan, had stepped forward personally.

“I leave it in your hands.”

After Geom Muyang gave a brief greeting, Branch Leader Homyeong stepped forward.

“Did you say you lost track of Gwak Yeong?”

Then an unexpected answer came out.

“No.”

At the word no, Homyeong and the other martial artists were all surprised.

“Gwak Yeong returned to Cheolbang this morning.”

Geom Muyang’s expression stiffened slightly as she listened. It was a situation where she should be glad to have found her, but.

It bothered her that Gwak Yeong had returned so conspicuously on the very day she herself had entered Muhan.

Homyeong felt similarly.

“It could be a trap.”

At his words, Geom Muyang nodded.

“Perhaps.”

But even if it were a trap, it was not something she could retreat from.

That was not the only news being awaited.

“There is something you should know. Han Wolgaek is currently in Muhan.”

Geom Muyang flinched at the name Han Wolgaek. Before she could even react, more bad news followed.

“And Byeoksan Geom will arrive soon.”

Homyeong quickly asked Hua Yin.

“Did they come because of our operation this time?”

“We are currently checking.”

Gwak Yeong had returned of her own accord, and two famous experts of the orthodox faction were coming here?

Truly, the Dragon Pool was bubbling and boiling, and from the Tiger’s Den, the roar of a hungry tiger could be heard.

Homyeong looked at Geom Muyang. Even if they had come because of them, he knew Geom Muyang was not someone who would get scared and turn back.

As expected, Geom Muyang calmly asked Hua Yin.

“Where is Gwak Yeong’s house?”

“She has lodgings in the Inner Court of the Murim Alliance.”

As she is the artisan of Cheolbang, it seems they specially provided her with lodgings in the Inner Court.

If it was the Inner Court rather than the Outer Court? Entering was almost impossible.

“There is a teahouse that Gwak Yeong frequents. After finishing work, she would stop by to drink tea.”

“Where is it?”

“It is a teahouse called Xiang Xiang on the main street.”

Hua Yin had prepared more than just information. He opened the closet. Inside, it was full of various types of martial uniforms.

“In those clothes you cannot carry weapons, so please wear these. They are the martial uniforms most commonly seen here in Muhan.”

Along with that, he gave them new identities.

“If a situation arises where you must reveal your identity, say that you are disciples of Chongjianmen, an orthodox sect in Honam.”

Chongjianmen was also a disguised sect of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“The disciples of Chongjianmen are currently out sightseeing at the Murim Alliance’s main compound with their eldest senior brother.”

Hua Yin brought a paper with names written on it and told each of them their names. If anyone checked with Chongjianmen, they would hear that these people had gone to tour the Murim Alliance’s main compound.

“Thank you for your consideration.”

Everyone began changing into martial uniforms.

“Don’t forget to wear the protective armor under your clothes, everyone.”

Homyeong, who was looking after his subordinates, was worried that only Geom Muyang lacked protective armor.

But he could not give her the one he was wearing either. He could not give the Great Young Master protective armor that was supplied to ordinary martial artists. Though he thought she would be fine since her martial arts were so profound, he still wished he could put something better on her if possible.

Shortly after, they quietly slipped out through the manor’s back gate.

* * *

Geom Muyang was drinking tea alone at Xiang Xiang.

The remaining martial artists were scattered here and there—across the tavern on the opposite side, the roadside, the alleyways, the building next door—keeping watch over the surroundings. One could only imagine how carefully they had taken their positions one by one.

In truth, today was closer to reconnaissance. She had wondered if she would really run into her from the very first day.

—It’s her!

At Homyeong’s telepathic message, she raised her head to see a woman entering the teahouse.

There was a large burn scar on the back of the woman’s hand. It could not be missed. She had no intention of hiding the scar, having even rolled up her sleeves.

Boldly displaying her wound, she was as beautiful of face and graceful of figure as rumored.

Having entered the teahouse like this, Gwak Yeong looked around inside to see where she should sit.

At that moment, her eyes met Geom Muyang’s.

Then Gwak Yeong smiled with her eyes and slightly bowed her head.

Geom Muyang could not tell. Whether she had smiled knowing who she was, or whether it was simply an eye greeting upon making accidental eye contact.

In any case, one thing was unexpected. Her atmosphere was brighter than Geom Muyang had imagined.

Then Homyeong sent a telepathic message to Geom Muyang from outside.

—There doesn’t appear to be any tailing or ambush.

Geom Muyang also raised her qi and surveyed, but neither the customers inside nor anyone else showed any particularly noticeable danger.

‘Did she really come alone to drink tea?’

If so, all that remained was to subdue her and take her out. Outside, a carriage to transport her was already prepared.

But Geom Muyang was cautious. Because things were unfolding too smoothly, she was even more careful.

Gwak Yeong was drinking tea, watching the pedestrians coming and going outside. Her gaze was deep, as if contemplating something.

Then a group of passing men spotted her and bowed politely.

She returned the greeting in a bright voice and waved her hand at them. Seeing that they were all muscular, they seemed to be men who worked at Cheolbang.

As she, who had been looking outside, turned her head again to drink her tea, Geom Muyang was already sitting across from her.

It was a situation that should have startled her, but she calmly asked.

“Why are you sitting down without asking permission?”

On the other hand, Geom Muyang went on the offensive.

“Then why did you make it without our permission?”

From the very first words, it was a declaration revealing her identity and a statement pressing Gwak Yeong.

If she tried to scream or flee, her acupoints would be sealed before she could even attempt it.

But Gwak Yeong showed an unexpected reaction.

She leaned her upper body forward, bringing her face close to Geom Muyang’s, and spoke with utter boldness.

“Are you stupid? Who asks permission to make a counterfeit?”

Chapter 620: Why Look at the Sky on the Day You Die?
----------------------------------------------------
‘Who asks permission to make counterfeits?’

Geom Muyang had not expected Gwak Yeong to ask such an impudent question. It flustered him all the more because she was right.

“If the materials had been the same, you wouldn’t even have known they were counterfeits.”

What is this? This shamelessness?

She was far too different from what he had expected. This was a situation where she should have been prostrating herself, begging to be spared.

Is it a trap after all?

Geom Muyang calmly surveyed the surroundings again. Still, he could find nothing suspicious inside the teahouse.

Gwak Yeong picked up the teacup in front of her and took a sip. Her palms were covered in calluses, and her hands were rough. Not only were there burn marks, there were so many scars that one hesitated to call them a woman’s hands.

Her hands laid bare the life she had lived.

Sensing Geom Muyang’s gaze, Gwak Yeong raised her hand so the back of it faced him.

“Scars of glory!”

She was proud of those scars.

“Thanks to these hands, several people survived that day.”

Gwak Yeong asked Geom Muyang,

“Do you have scars like these too?”

When he remained silent, she found the reason for that silence in a strange place.

“Are you angry because I called you a fool?”

Only then did Geom Muyang open his mouth.

“If I were angry, it would be because of the counterfeits you made.”

“It’s a world overflowing with fakes!”

Of course, it did not work on Geom Muyang. Rather, when she sensed his gaze growing colder, she avoided his eyes and lifted her teacup.

She sipped her tea, then looked out the window.

It was the same scenery as always, yet today it felt especially lively.

As she watched the people passing by, she bit her lip slightly. Her lips were trembling. She was acting as if she were bold in front of him, but in proportion to that boldness, she was afraid.

She sighed as if talking to herself.

“They said there was absolutely no way I’d be caught, so what is this?”

From the feel of it, she had not known she would meet him here today.

If so, there was something that did not make sense.

They were meticulous enough to eliminate even the witnesses. They were people capable of drawing in Han Wolgaek and the Byeoksan Sword.

So why had they left her so defenseless? She should have been the most important person of all.

Still looking outside, she asked,

“I’m in a lot of trouble, aren’t I?”

The crime of making counterfeits was not so light that she could afford to act this relaxed. If there had been no reason to uncover the people behind her through her, she would already have been dead. Even after they extracted information from her, her life would still be hanging by a thread.

Geom Muyang rose from his seat.

“Follow me quietly.”

She did not get up.

“I haven’t finished my tea yet.”

Did she think she would die if she were dragged away? The hand holding her teacup was trembling.

It was right then. Someone passing in front of the teahouse stopped and called out to her.

“Craftsman Gwak!”

He was a middle-aged martial artist dressed in the uniform of the Murim Alliance. Behind him, more than ten martial artists followed in attendance, each with nimble steps and sharp gazes.

The middle-aged man was none other than Do Insu, the leader of the White Dragon Squad, an elite organization of the Murim Alliance alongside the Blue Dragon Squad.

“Squad Leader Do!”

Gwak Yeong rose from her seat, then said quietly to Geom Muyang,

“What should I do? If I don’t go, that man will come over here.”

Without waiting for Geom Muyang’s answer, she went outside.

“How was the sword I sent you last time?”

“It was excellent. Since Craftsman Gwak made it, how could it not be?”

Everyone acknowledged Gwak Yeong’s skill, and all were certain she would become the youngest person ever to be in charge of the Iron Workshop.
