There was a reason the White Dragon Squad Leader treated her so warmly. Every organization within the Murim Alliance tried to maintain good relations with the Iron Workshop. Only then would they be sent weapons that were even slightly better than those of other organizations.

The White Dragon Squad Leader’s gaze turned toward Geom Muyang. For some reason, he was a man who drew the eye.

“And who is that great hero?”

Gwak Yeong looked at Geom Muyang. Her eyes seemed to say,

What should I do? Should I just reveal that you’re from the Demonic Cult?

But for whatever reason, unexpected words flowed from Gwak Yeong’s mouth.

“He’s my friend. A friend who’s helped me a lot with my work for a long time.”

Geom Muyang rose from his seat and greeted him politely with a cupped-fist salute.

“I am Jang Hyeong of the Sunggeom Sect.”

“Oh, so you are Great Hero Jang.”

At the word that he was Gwak Yeong’s friend, the wariness in his eyes eased. That alone showed how much he wanted to stay in Gwak Yeong’s good graces.

“I still have something to discuss with the Squad Leader. Friend, let’s meet again next time.”

At her words, Geom Muyang nodded.

He had no choice but to let her go. With the White Dragon Squad Leader and the White Dragon Squad martial artists watching, he could not forcefully take her away.

And so Gwak Yeong left the place with the White Dragon Squad Leader. As she walked off into the distance, Gwak Yeong turned back. After looking at Geom Muyang for a moment, she quickened her steps and disappeared.

When Geom Muyang left Hyanghyang and began walking, Homyeong quietly came up beside him. He, too, had watched Gwak Yeong use the Murim Alliance martial artists to slip away.

“I will inform Tongcheon Pavilion that Gwak Yeong is likely to go into hiding again. I’ll tell them to cast a tight net this time so we don’t lose her.”

Then Geom Muyang gave an unexpected answer.

“Gwak Yeong will not run.”

“Pardon?”

“She will probably come again tomorrow.”

She had fled like that, and yet she would come again tomorrow? Homyeong could not understand Geom Muyang for thinking such a thing.

“May I ask why?”

It was Geom Muyang’s instinct. For some reason, he had a feeling she would not simply leave like this.

If he had to give Homyeong a reason,

“If the ones behind her intended to hide her, they would not have let her return.”

And yet they had done the opposite. She, who had vanished, had returned precisely on the day they arrived.

* * *

The next day, Homyeong kept an even sharper watch around Hyanghyang.

Through Tongcheon Pavilion’s marked agents, he tried to grasp the movements of the Murim Alliance’s masters and elites as much as possible, and the martial artists of the Earth Corps strained every nerve. The peace in which nothing happened only made them more uneasy.

Geom Muyang entered Hyanghyang alone.

Then he sat in the seat he had occupied yesterday and ordered tea.

“You’ve come again.”

The owner recognized Geom Muyang and greeted him warmly. According to Tongcheon Pavilion’s investigation, he was not suspicious. He was an ordinary man who had run this teahouse here for a long time.

Tongcheon Pavilion had been thorough enough to investigate even all the regular customers. But they could find no guests they needed to be wary of.

Geom Muyang sat alone and drank his tea.

Even as time passed, she did not appear.

‘Was my intuition wrong?’

Perhaps it was. He might have misjudged her, confused by her overly confident and bright demeanor.

Just as a full double-hour passed and Geom Muyang was about to rise from his seat—

—She has arrived.

Homyeong’s voice transmission reached him, and shortly afterward, Gwak Yeong entered the teahouse.

She had fled yesterday as if running for her life.

This time, she came first to the seat where Geom Muyang sat.

“The quality of the iron that came in today was the best since I entered the Iron Workshop. Truly, we’ve never had such good iron before.”

She behaved as if yesterday’s flight had never happened, as if she were dealing with a real friend. She even smiled brightly.

“You don’t know, do you? On days when good iron comes in, good things happen. It’s an old belief of the Iron Workshop.”

“A superstition, then.”

With that cold reply, Geom Muyang stared icily at her and asked,

“Why did you not run and instead come back?”

He had expected it, but he was curious about the reason. Moreover, she had come alone again today.

He had examined the surroundings even more thoroughly than yesterday, but still there were no hidden masters in ambush.

“Where could I run when people like you have marked me?”

If that reason was sincere, then she had made a clever choice. Just because she had left like that yesterday did not mean they would have lost her if she tried to run.

“Unless I hid deep in the mountains somewhere.”

“Then you might save your life.”

After ordering her usual tea from the teahouse owner, she said to Geom Muyang,

“My life isn’t so grand that I’d have to hide in the mountains just to protect it.”

Her words were bold, yet tinged with sorrow.

“And what would I do alone in the mountains? A life where I can’t work at the Iron Workshop would be…”

She did not finish, but he could tell she meant death would be better.

“If you like your work so much, why did you do such a thing?”

She only toyed with her teacup and did not answer. He could clearly sense that there were circumstances behind it.

In any case, she would be interrogated using a confession drug, so it did not matter if she refused to speak now.

“Let’s get up.”

“I’ve only taken one sip!”

But Geom Muyang rose first and left the teahouse.

After sipping down the tea left in her cup, she followed Geom Muyang outside.

A carriage was waiting in an alley near the teahouse.

“Let’s walk.”

“Who are you planning to meet today to slip away?”

When Geom Muyang climbed into the carriage first, she had no choice but to climb in with him. It was a carriage whose windows were covered so she could not see where they were going.

“I’d like to look outside as we go.”

The carriage set off, but Geom Muyang did not open the closed window for her.

And then came a blunt sentence.

“You’ll have a chance to see the sky once before you die.”

After a brief pause, she answered,

“Why would I look at the sky on the day I die? I should look at people.”

Geom Muyang gave no reply.

After a moment of silence, Gwak Yeong cautiously asked,

“Who are you?”

From the way she looked at him, Geom Muyang could tell. The woman did not know who he was. The people behind her had told her nothing.

Geom Muyang did not answer.

Before long, the carriage arrived at the manor.

They had circled the outskirts several times to check whether they were being followed, but there was no one pursuing them and no one trying to protect her. She was utterly alone.

Just as Geom Muyang opened the carriage door and was about to step out—

Srrk.

Gwak Yeong aimed something at Geom Muyang.

Amazingly, what she held in her hand was a Jingyeok. But it was not the counterfeit Jingyeok Geom Muyang had seen earlier.

It was far smaller. So small it could be perfectly hidden in the palm of one’s hand.

“It’s a Jingyeok that fires only one shot. I made this too.”

Because they were sitting face to face inside the carriage, the Jingyeok was aimed right before his face.

But Geom Muyang did not look frightened at all.

“I asked who you are.”

From the moment she first saw him, she had sensed that he was not just an ordinary demon cultist. He did not feel like a butcher who hunted down and killed those who made counterfeits. He spoke very little, but there was a certain authority about him.

Even now, with the Jingyeok aimed before his eyes, he did not so much as blink.

“You are going to kill me without even knowing who I am?”

A heavy silence flowed.

“Why don’t you shoot? All you have to do is move your finger.”

Gwak Yeong’s eyes trembled, while Geom Muyang’s eyes remained calm.

“You’ve never killed a person, have you?”

“Is that something to boast about? Having killed people?”

Though she trembled, she tried to remain bold.

“It would be my first time, but I can kill you.”

“Then kill me.”

But she did not fire the Jingyeok.

“I didn’t take it out to kill you. I took it out to live.”

Gwak Yeong slowly lowered the Jingyeok she had been aiming.

“I will stake my life and fate on you.”

Her eyes were more solemn than ever.

“You said you don’t even know who I am. What do you believe in, to stake your fate on me?”

Gwak Yeong stared at Geom Muyang and said,

“I don’t believe in you. I believe in myself. I believe in my own choice.”

Gwak Yeong gazed intently at Geom Muyang. When they first met in the teahouse, she had bowed her head slightly toward him and smiled without realizing it.

It had not been a smile offered simply because their eyes met. The instant she saw him, there had been some resonance rising from deep within her heart. She could not explain what it was.

“I spared your life, so grant me one request. After that, you may kill me if you like. If you are a person with honor, promise me here and now.”

The look in her eyes, resolved for death, spoke the truth.

It was right then.

Whip.

The Jingyeok she had lowered was once again aimed at Geom Muyang.

The one more surprised was Gwak Yeong, who was holding it. Her finger moved on its own and tried to pull the mechanism that would launch the hidden projectile.

“No!”

She tried to turn it aside, but her arm would not move.

“Dodge!”

She shouted, but the Jingyeok fired.

Shaaaak!

Pak!

In that instant, Gwak Yeong squeezed her eyes shut. She knew better than anyone that at this distance, it could never be dodged. She had made it herself.

‘Ah! What on earth just happened?’

She had absolutely not meant to kill him. And yet her hand had moved on its own and fired the Jingyeok.

Chiiiiik.

She heard the sound of something melting away.

She squeezed her eyes even more tightly shut.

Then she heard a single sentence.

“Open your eyes.”

At Geom Muyang’s voice, she snapped her eyes open.

Geom Muyang was sitting there. There was a hole right beside his head. It seemed that at the final moment, her hand had moved and shot beside him. She had been so shocked and flustered that she had not realized her hand had moved.

Greatly relieved, she asked,

“What just happened?”

Geom Muyang had moved her fingers and arm according to his will, as if manipulating an object through the air. They were extremely close, and she did not know martial arts, so it had been an easy matter.

He had shown her directly that even if she had tried to kill him, she could not have done so.

Geom Muyang opened the carriage door and stepped out first.

“Enough nonsense about fate. Get down.”

Geom Muyang gave an order to his subordinate.

“Take her to the interrogation room.”

Gwak Yeong stepped down from the carriage with an awkward sigh.

“But today was definitely a day when good iron came in.”

Chapter 621: Though It’s the Heartless Demonic Cult
---------------------------------------------------
The air was heavy and foul.

They called it an interrogation room, but in truth it was a torture chamber. Shackles and chains hung from the walls, and on one side, torture implements were laid out across a long table as though on display.

Just where were they planning to stab with iron needles of so many different thicknesses?

Sharp saws and daggers, and all manner of other unpleasant tools whose purposes she could not guess.

And the brazier and branding irons set beside the table were certainly not there for roasting sweet potatoes.

The moment Gwak Yeong saw them, her whole body trembled of its own accord. It was not a fear she could suppress by will.

She forced her gaze elsewhere, only to see red stains on the floor this time. Looking closely, she realized it was blood.

No matter how strong one’s temperament, it was difficult to remain composed in such an atmosphere. She could not shake the fear that she might be tortured, die miserably in the end, and be buried in the back yard.

But soon she slapped her own cheeks, hard.

“Get a hold of yourself, Gwak Yeong! This is the path you chose.”

On the first day she had met that man, when she had been able to get away through the White Dragon Unit Leader, she had not fled then and there. Nor had she informed them.

Going back to the tea house to meet that man had been entirely her own choice. It was the conclusion she had reached after staying up all night.

Why had she done it?

When she had looked back while walking after the White Dragon Unit Leader, he had been staring at her.

With an indifferent gaze that revealed no emotion.

Perhaps it had been because of that indifferent expression he had shown in a situation where it would have been understandable for him to display irritation or anger. In that situation, whether he had gotten angry or laughed, either would have made her feel awful.

Right then—

Creeeeak.

She flinched at the sound of the iron door opening. Her heart pounded as though it would burst.

The thick iron door opened, and two people stepped inside. Disappointment brushed across her heart. She had hoped he would come in, but the ones who entered were Branch Leader Ho Myeong and a subordinate.

“If you do as instructed, it won’t take long.”

At Ho Myeong’s words, Gwak Yeong pleaded.

“Please call the man from earlier.”

“He is an honored person.”

“Then call that honored person. I’ll tell him everything.”

Gwak Yeong felt it instinctively. She must not speak to these people.

What would happen if she revealed everything?

They would obtain all the information and then kill her. Yes, they were demons; they were more than capable of doing that.

That was why she had to stake her fate not on them, but on that man from earlier. She had to receive a promise from him.

But Ho Myeong did not listen to her.

He took a small bottle from his robe and placed it on the table before her.

“Drink it.”

“What is this?”

“A confession drug.”

Though she had not learned martial arts, she worked at the Murim Alliance. She had heard rumors about this confession drug.

“I’ve heard that those who try to resist confessing to the end can die from the side effects, or be left crippled.”

Ho Myeong nodded. There were many kinds of side effects. Someone might simply be unlucky and die even after spilling everything smoothly, and as she said, the side effects appeared more readily in those who refused to reveal the truth.

“There aren’t many people with that level of will.”

“There’s one right in front of you.”

Gwak Yeong was not bluffing. She truly had no intention of telling these people, and she trusted her own will. That was why she was genuinely worried she might die from the side effects.

“You don’t want me to die either, do you?”

Gwak Yeong forced herself to shake off her fear and tried to stand firm.

“So please call him.”

Of course, it did not work on Ho Myeong. He could have sealed her paralysis acupoints and forced her to drink it, but Ho Myeong did not do so.

At his glance, the subordinate who had entered with him lit the brazier and set a branding iron atop it.

Sssssss.

A moment later, the branding iron glowed red-hot.

“Don’t think you can scare me with fire. I’ve helped with work in the forge since I was young, so I’m not afraid of fire. It’s practically a friend of mine.”

“It will hurt all the more if you suffer at a friend’s hands.”

When Ho Myeong gave the order with his eyes, the subordinate picked up the branding iron and approached her.

He brought the branding iron toward her face. The part of the body that could most certainly terrify a person was the face. Especially if that person was a woman.

But Gwak Yeong did not close her eyes. Nor did she scream. She stared at the approaching branding iron, eyes wide open, teeth clenched.

Just before the branding iron touched her face—

A voice came from behind.

“Wait.”

Fortunately, the branding iron stopped before it seared her face.

The person standing before the open iron door was Geom Muyang.

When he stepped inside, the subordinate holding the branding iron left, and only Ho Myeong remained.

Geom Muyang walked over and stood before her.

“Why did you ask them to call me?”

Geom Muyang had heard her words from outside the door. He could tell by instinct. No torture would make her say what they wanted to hear.

With resolve like this, she would truly die from the confession drug’s side effects, just as she had said.

“I told you in the carriage. That I staked my life and fate on you.”

Geom Muyang’s reaction remained cold.

“What do you know about me?”

“I told you I trust my instincts.”

Looking at the confession drug in front of her, Geom Muyang said,

“Drink it.”

Gwak Yeong looked down at the confession drug with trembling eyes, then picked it up.

Then she opened the stopper and, staring into Geom Muyang’s eyes, drank it down in one go.

After a short while, her face flushed red as the drug began to take effect.

Now the questioning began in earnest.

“Why did you approach me?”

It was not something he could rule out—the possibility that she had deliberately approached him.

“You were the one who approached me. You came to my seat, didn’t you?”

Her voice was languid, as though she had just woken from sleep.

“Were you not ordered to approach me?”

“I wasn’t.”

“Then why did you come to the tea house again the next day?”

Gwak Yeong’s eyes were unfocused and hazy.

“I thought you might be able to help me.”

“Why?”

“It was a feeling. I thought, thank goodness this is the person who came to catch me.”

Gwak Yeong frowned slightly and said,

“Is this what it feels like to take a confession drug? I’m dizzy, and my stomach feels so sick.”

Ordinarily, one answered under the influence of the confession drug while unconscious.

The fact that she recognized she had taken the drug meant her mental strength was extraordinary. At the very least, in the current situation, that was not a good sign.

Geom Muyang looked down at her in silence for a moment.

Gwak Yeong smiled faintly and said something unexpected.

“You’re handsome.”

Geom Muyang snorted with an expression of disbelief.

“Don’t just ask me questions. I want to ask one too. This scar—is it very ugly?”

She was not in her right mind. She had begun saying whatever came to her unconsciously.

“Don’t laugh at me. This is my scar of honor. Do you know how scared I was back then? Do you?”

Drip.

Blood flowed from her nose.

“Huh? I’m bleeding. I didn’t lie, but I’m bleeding.”

Ho Myeong spoke quickly.

“The side effects are starting to appear.”

“Give her the antidote.”

“Yes.”

Ho Myeong fed Gwak Yeong the antidote. It was medicine that dispelled the effects of the confession drug.

After some time passed, Gwak Yeong regained her senses.

“What happened?”

“The side effects appeared, so we gave you the antidote.”

At the thought that she had narrowly survived, she felt relieved.

“Thank you.”

“It is not over yet.”

“No, it’s all over. When I went to find you the next day, it was already all over. The only thing left is whether you will make a promise with me or not.”

“What promise?”

Earlier, in the carriage, she had said that since she had not fired the Jingyeok, she wanted him to make her one promise.

“Please save them.”

Naturally, he had thought she meant herself.

“Please save my younger sibling.”

Only now did Geom Muyang understand why she had made the counterfeit hidden weapon. Why she had come to him and struggled to obtain such an absurd promise. She had not been trying to save herself.

“My younger sibling is being held hostage by them.”

She clenched both fists tightly.

“After my parents died, that child was the only family I had left. There’s no one kinder in the world.”

One question naturally arose.

“Why didn’t you tell the Murim Alliance?”

If she was an artisan of the Murim Alliance’s forge, the Alliance would have moved to save her sibling.

“They told me there was someone on their side in a high position within the Murim Alliance. They said no matter how secretly I reported it to the Alliance, they would find out first in the end.”

“And you believed that?”

She seemed clever enough not to believe it easily.

Then she said something astonishing.

“I had no choice but to believe it. Because the place where I heard those words was my residence in the inner court of the Murim Alliance.”

If outsiders could freely enter and leave the inner court of the Murim Alliance, it meant that, just as they had said, someone high-ranking was involved.

“They said if I made it for them just once, they would release my sibling and never come looking for me again.”

She let out a sigh of regret.

“They broke their promise. Stupid me. It was a promise they never intended to keep in the first place.”

The fact that they had not released her sibling meant they intended to force her to make other hidden weapons.

“You asked why I staked my fate on you? Because I couldn’t keep getting dragged around by them. Even if I did, in the end, they would kill my sibling. I became certain of that when I saw them break their promise this time.”

With an expression that said she had judged correctly, Geom Muyang gave a slight nod.

“You can use me, or kill me. You can sear me with that branding iron, or make me drink ten bottles of confession drug. I’ll tell you everything I know. In return, please save my sibling.”

She did not hide her feelings. She thought that with this man, it would be better to be honest than to resort to shallow tricks.

“I should be negotiating with you using the information I know, but you people are too frightening. I can’t do it.”

Geom Muyang did not answer.

Gwak Yeong pleaded again.

“Even if the Demon Cult is bloodless and tearless, you must have siblings too, don’t you? If you do, you can understand how I feel, can’t you?”

Geom Muyang, who had remained silent all along, spoke to her.

“I am someone who once wished my younger sibling would die.”

For a moment, Gwak Yeong flinched.

“Please pretend you didn’t hear what I just said.”

She pleaded in another way.

“Then for the rest of my life, I’ll work under your orders and make anything you tell me to make. I don’t know how high your position is, but if you take me with you, you’ll even get promoted.”

Ho Myeong, who had been watching, laughed silently.

“My career is ruined anyway. I’ve sullied the honor of the Murim Alliance’s forge, so how could I live on as an artisan? I’ll make things for you. You saw it, didn’t you? That little Jingyeok? I made that myself too. I’ll make anything you want.”

Still, Geom Muyang said nothing.

In the end, Gwak Yeong burst out,

“You heartless man! If someone begs this much, you could at least promise, even if it’s an empty one!”

Geom Muyang said bluntly,

“I do not make promises I will not keep.”

Yes. Because she had sensed he was that kind of person, she had made this gamble.

“If you don’t help me, I’ll die, become a ghost, and appear in your dreams every night to torment you.”

She tried threatening him like this and appealing to his goodness too. Appealing to the goodness of a demon.

“If you’re not the type to help others, then help someone just once in your life. Who knows? When you fall into hell, King Yama might forgive you because of that good deed.”

But Geom Muyang did not budge. She realized that this impregnable fortress would not collapse from her pleading or persuasion.

‘Right, I must be insane. A demon is just a demon. Some instinct that was.’

She silently looked down at the back of her hand for a moment.

“Do you know what really makes me angry? It’s that all of this happened because of my efforts. The effort of coming out alone at night and hammering away without even sleeping—those efforts ended up becoming efforts to kill my sibling. I’m so angry and wronged.”

She had endured even when the heated branding iron was about to sear her face, but at last, for the first time, a single tear dropped from her eye. The fallen tear ran hotly over the burn scar on the back of her hand.

‘I’m sorry. This older sister is sorry. Still, even if you’re born again, please be born as my sibling again. Then I will never become an artisan or anything like it. I’m sorry, truly.’

It was truly a misfortune that had come upon her life like a natural disaster.

But as if to say that the world did not hold only misfortune like natural disasters, a quiet sentence reached her ears.

“I will save your sibling.”

Gwak Yeong jerked her head up. Geom Muyang had spoken only that one sentence and added nothing more. That was why Gwak Yeong could understand. This man truly kept his promises.

Geom Muyang gave Ho Myeong an order.

“Take the rest of her statement concerning those people in detail. Do not leave out a single thing.”

“Yes, understood.”

Then Geom Muyang turned and walked toward the iron door.

Just before he opened the door and left, Gwak Yeong shouted after him.

“Why?”

It might not have been wise to ask the reason when he had agreed to save them, but she was too curious.

Without looking back, Geom Muyang left behind words she had never expected.

“Let’s just say it’s because good iron came in today.”

Chapter 622: What Are You Doing Squatting There?
------------------------------------------------
Geom Muyang looked out the window from his quarters.

The Jidan martial artists were visible in the courtyard.

As if taking a break from work, they were sprawled here and there in loose disarray, but in their own way, they had spread an ironclad net of vigilance.

Suddenly, Geom Muyang recalled what Gwak Yeong had said earlier.

—You must have siblings too, don’t you? If you do, then you should understand how I feel, shouldn’t you?”

He had answered that he was someone who had once wished his younger brother would die, but that was, after all, an emotion of the past.

If his younger brother were being held hostage?

The first thought that came to mind was this.

Please, someone take him away.

And then another thought naturally followed.

Not that anyone could take him away just because they wanted to, but even if they did, they would die of sheer rage. After listening to all the things that fellow said, they would end up confused as to who the hostage really was. They would surely find themselves saying, We’ll release you, so please just leave.

As he thought that, a faint smile naturally formed at the corners of Geom Muyang’s mouth.

It was no longer surprising that he could think such thoughts while recalling his younger brother. What was surprising was that the feeling was no longer surprising.

Just then, Homyeong’s voice came from outside.

“The investigation is complete.”

The door opened, and Homyeong and Gwak Yeong entered.

Homyeong handed Geom Muyang a paper on which the results of the investigation were written.

Geom Muyang read through it carefully.

It detailed when and how the men had approached her, and what they had said. Their appearances, manner of speech, and even physical characteristics were all recorded as well.

She had also remembered how far they had traveled when she was moved by carriage with her eyes covered in order to make the counterfeit hidden weapons, and what sounds she had heard along the way.

As Geom Muyang read, he lifted his head and looked at Gwak Yeong. As if she knew what he was asking without him having to say it, she answered.

“I memorized it desperately. I thought perhaps a day like this might come someday. Though I didn’t expect it to be you people rather than the Murim Alliance.”

Geom Muyang’s gaze returned to the paper.

It recorded what people she had met at the place where they made the counterfeit goods, and through what process they had made the hidden weapons. It even described what kind of iron had been used there, and what tools were used to make them.

She was certainly intelligent. Not because she had a good memory, but because she had memorized only the parts that were truly necessary.

Geom Muyang handed the paper back to Homyeong.

“Send it to Tongcheongak at once.”

With information this detailed, Tongcheongak would surely be able to uncover clues about those men.

Homyeong left, and only Geom Muyang and Gwak Yeong remained there.

First, she thanked Geom Muyang for promising to save her younger brother.

“I was too overwhelmed earlier and couldn’t properly thank you. Thank you, truly.”

Geom Muyang gave no answer. Instead, he turned his head back toward the window.

Now that the investigation was over, she was even more tense.

What would become of her now?

She still bore the crime of having counterfeited the Demonic Cult’s hidden weapons.

He had promised to save her younger brother, not to forgive her.

“I’m of no use now.”

She had told them everything she knew.

“Even if you kill me and bury me, I won’t resent you. I won’t appear as a ghost either.”

Of course, she did not mean it sincerely.

She did not want to die. Saving her younger brother was more important than her life, but if this man did manage to save him?

She wanted to go on living together with her brother. She wanted to see him marry, have children, and live his life. In place of their parents who had passed away early, she wanted to watch over him until he could live on his own.

If even she died, he would become a lone orphan under heaven.

Then Geom Muyang abruptly said,

“You said you’d get me promoted, didn’t you?”

For an instant, her eyes widened. She had said it earlier in the torture chamber when she had shouted at him. She had said she would spend her life doing whatever he ordered and making whatever he told her to make. That if he took her with him, he would even be promoted.

Of course, they had merely been words she had blurted out in agitation. She had never once imagined a life making weapons for the Demonic Cult.

“That… Are you really saying you’ll take me with you? You’re joking, aren’t you?”

Just as she stood flustered, not knowing what to say,

Homyeong hurriedly returned. From his expression alone, it was clear just how urgent the matter was.

“I think you must evacuate.”

Geom Muyang sprang to his feet.

“An urgent message came from Tongcheongak. An emergency operation has been assigned to the Baengnyongdan, and their target is this place.”

Already, outside the window, the Jidan martial artists were moving. They pulled out weapons hidden among lumber and piles of dirt, climbed onto the walls and rooftops, and entered true vigilance.

It was surprising enough that the Murim Alliance had learned the location of this place, but their objective was even more surprising.

“They say it is an operation to rescue this woman.”

Because it was not a covert operation but an official operation of the Alliance, Tongcheongak had been able to learn of it quickly and contact them.

Even in such an urgent situation, Geom Muyang did not hurry.

“How much time do we have?”

“We must get out within one gak.”

Geom Muyang understood why those men had kept Gwak Yeong alive.

It was clear that they intended to use her to make them clash with the Murim Alliance. Without even stepping forward themselves, they might be able to force them to withdraw from Wuhan.

And at this point, the most important question was how they had discovered this place. On the way here, he had checked several times to make sure they were not being followed or watched.

Then one thought flashed through his mind.

With an expression that seemed to say, Surely not, Geom Muyang asked Gwak Yeong,

“Did you perhaps receive anything from them?”

“No, nothing.”

Then she flinched in surprise.

“There is one thing.”

She took a letter from her bosom.

“My younger brother sent it to me.”

Geom Muyang did not take the letter she held out with his hand.

When he made it float up through the air by remote object manipulation, Homyeong understood what Geom Muyang was thinking.

Homyeong took a leather pouch from his bosom. When he unfolded it, more than ten large and small bottles were fitted inside.

Homyeong took out one of the bottles, removed the stopper, and dropped a single drop onto the letter.

Then the color of the letter turned blue.

“It’s Tracking Scent.”

At those words, Gwak Yeong was startled.

“They must have applied Tracking Scent in anticipation of us finding this woman. Or else they may have applied it in preparation for the possibility that she would flee.”

At Homyeong’s words, Geom Muyang nodded. Whoever it was, it was clear there was a very clever one among them.

Since it was a letter sent by her younger brother, she would always carry it close to her heart.

Gwak Yeong was afraid.

More frightening than the fear that this man might rage at her like fire was the terror that he might cancel his promise.

Then the letter floated over to her.

She caught it reflexively and asked Geom Muyang in confusion,

“Why give this to me?”

Geom Muyang said in an impassive tone,

“It’s the last time.”

The moment she heard those words, her heart dropped. She had taken it to mean that he intended to kill her.

But that was not it.

Belatedly, she understood. He meant for her to read it one last time before burning it. If he failed to save her younger brother, this letter would become her brother’s last words to her.

Emotion surged in her heart. She was truly grateful to Geom Muyang for thinking of her even in this urgent situation.

“I’ve read it dozens of times, so I’ve already memorized everything inside.”

With trembling hands, she held the letter out again.

“Thank you.”

Whoosh.

With Geom Muyang’s scorching yang energy, the letter burst into flames in midair.

In the meantime, Homyeong took out another medicine bottle from the pouch and poured it all over her body.

Since she had carried the letter in her bosom, the Tracking Scent would remain on her body as well. He covered that scent with another smell.

Geom Muyang led the way out of the room, and Gwak Yeong and Homyeong followed behind him.

The martial artists moved divided into a scouting group, a main force, and a rear guard, while two martial artists guarded Gwak Yeong on either side, prepared for any hidden weapons that might come flying in.

Thus, before the Baengnyongdan stormed in, they first escaped the manor.

* * *

At Honghwaru (紅花樓), the finest pleasure house in Wuhan, a man was drinking.

White hair, an angular face, and eyes like those of a wounded beast. In truth, he was a man with deep wounds in his heart.

The man sitting alone drinking without inviting any courtesans in was none other than Han Wolgaek.

Having lost his family to demonic practitioners, he was a man who would spring awake from sleep at the mere mention of the Demonic Cult. If his opponent was a demonic practitioner, he killed them without asking the reason. His hatred for the Demonic Cult was that immense.

The reason such a man had come to Wuhan was because of one person’s request. A request from someone who seemed as though he would never ask anything of anyone in his life. That was why the request weighed all the more heavily.

And he knew better than anyone how deep his hatred for the Demonic Cult ran.

—I will lay out the stage for you, so dance with your blade once. A demonic practitioner whom no one but you can stop is coming to Wuhan.

Han Wolgaek did not refuse.

‘Yes, if my opponent is a demonic practitioner, I’ll dance as much as needed.’

At that moment, a martial artist carrying a message came and reported after respectfully offering courtesy.

“It is time.”

Then a powerful murderous intent flowed from Han Wolgaek’s two eyes.

After emptying his final cup, he said coldly,

“Go and tell them. Not a single Demonic Cult bastard who set foot in Wuhan today will return alive.”

* * *

After leaving Honghwaru, Han Wolgaek moved quickly.

Then one person caught his eye.

By the wall of the pleasure house, a young man was squatting and looking at a small blade of grass that had sprouted through stone.

Beside him were a bamboo collecting tube and a small herb pouch containing a few roots of medicinal herbs. Inside the collecting tube were insects whose names were unknown.

Han Wolgaek’s expression twisted. By nature, he was not one to tolerate such sights.

As he walked past, he said to the young man,

“What is a grown man doing squatting there?”

Then the young man lifted his head and looked at him. He was a very young-looking and handsome man.

“You look perfectly fine! At a time like this, swing your sword one more time instead. If you laze around, the Demonic Cult bastards will cut off your head.”

Even ordinarily, he was not one to hold back when he saw the unsatisfactory behavior of his juniors.

Especially since young people these days were said to dislike their seniors’ nagging, he did it all the more. If these fellows collapsed, the righteous martial world would be finished.

After letting out his sincere nagging, Han Wolgaek strode past the place.

The young man glanced at his back once, then looked back at the grass.

Once Han Wolgaek disappeared from sight, an old man approached the young man.

“What grass are you looking at so intently?”

The young man answered,

“Look at this. Bisa Grass (秘莎草) is growing in a place like this.”

The young man was none other than the Poison King, and the old man was Sangseon, who assisted him.

The Poison King was wearing a long robe over his own clothes, so the poison pouches at his waist could not be seen.

For that reason, this ordinary long robe could be considered the most powerful weapon in the martial world.

The moment the Poison King threw off the robe, if his opponent saw the Twelve Zodiac signs drawn on the twelve poison pouches, they would flee in terror.

“It really is Bisa Grass.”

Sangseon sat beside the Poison King with a surprised expression. They had already seen several poisonous plants he had never seen before. Until now, there had never been any reason for the Poison King to gather poisonous herbs in Wuhan.

It was right then.

From far away, someone came running toward them. It was none other than Han Wolgaek, who had passed by a short while ago.

Running in haste, he looked as frantic as though he had left something important behind.

His dignified face was urgent and pale.

“Move!”

Han Wolgaek passed by the Poison King and Sangseon like the wind. This time, he had no leisure to nag.

He ran straight into Honghwaru and headed for the privy. Han Wolgaek was a master capable of controlling even his bodily functions, but today, things did not go as he wished. It was truly the first time he had ever been in such urgent need. Face and demonic practitioners alike were matters for later.

Watching him enter Honghwaru, Sangseon smiled and said,

“It seems he’ll be stuck sitting in the privy all day today.”

At Sangseon’s words, the Poison King nodded. The very moment Han Wolgaek had spoken to him and passed by earlier, he had administered a poison that caused stomach trouble. He had not lifted a finger, merely looked at him, and in that instant he had poisoned him.

In a certain sense, the most frightening thing about the Poison King might not be his poison, but his youthful appearance that made one lower their guard the moment they saw him.

In any case, Han Wolgaek ought to consider himself fortunate that it ended at this level. If the Poison King had truly set his mind to it, he would no longer be of this world.

“I brought out several amusing poisons this time.”

Even without the Poison King saying so, Sangseon knew that the poisons used on this trip to Wuhan would be unusual.

Unless Geom Muyang’s life was at stake, no one would die from the Poison King’s poison. The Poison King himself knew that better than anyone.

He knew what role he played in this martial world. He knew what the consequences would be if he killed righteous faction people with poison in front of the Murim Alliance headquarters.

The two walked side by side. With an herb pouch at his side and an insect collecting tube in his hand, who would ever think him the Poison King?

“Please rest at an inn. Once the Eldest Young Master’s new hideout is decided, I will inform you.”

From the moment he arrived, the Poison King had been watching Geom Muyang’s back without missing a single instant.

“When is that person coming?”

“They say Lord Mabul is coming as quickly as possible, so he should arrive in Wuhan before long.”

A strange sense of anticipation filled the Poison King’s face. Sangseon naturally assumed he was pleased because he would be able to share the task of protecting Geom Muyang.

But what the Poison King was looking forward to was something Sangseon had not imagined at all.

“Make me a list of rare poisonous herbs found only around Wuhan.”

Chapter 623: As Long as We Don't Get Caught
-------------------------------------------
“The Eldest Young Master has safely arrived at his second residence.”

At the strategist’s report, Geom Mugeuk and Sama Myeong let out breaths of relief.

Beside the existing map of the Central Plains, a detailed topographical map of Wuhan had been made, centered on the main headquarters of the Martial Alliance.

Three flags stood upon it.

Two flags that had nearly drawn close to one another had moved apart.

They were Geom Muyang’s flag and Han Wolgaek’s flag.

Between those two flags stood one more. The thing that had forced apart the two flags that must never meet was—

The Poison King’s Demon Venerable Banner.

Yes. If not for that green flag, who else could have kept the enraged Han Wolgaek alive and sent him back?

Thank you, Poison King. When you return, I shall offer up this body of mine and let you conduct as many poison experiments as you wish.

Sama Myeong said to Geom Mugeuk,

“The urgent situation has passed, so please go and rest now. I will handle the remaining matters at the Heavenly Demon Hall.”

The two of them had been working at the Heavenly Demon Hall as usual when they received the emergency message and came to Tongcheon Pavilion.

It would have been nice to read books at the Heavenly Demon Library, but he had to read while doing what needed to be done. Taking his father’s place was far more important than reading.

“No. I will handle the remaining matters at the Heavenly Demon Hall. You are the one who should rest, Strategist.”

“I am fine.”

“I am not fine with it.”

Because Tongcheon Pavilion had been placed on emergency alert, Sama Myeong had not slept properly of late. The accumulated fatigue was written plainly across his face.

“Come with me.”

Geom Mugeuk took Sama Myeong by the hand and led him away. The place they went together was his room in Tongcheon Pavilion, the one with the bed he had shown him before.

“Close your eyes, even if only for a short while. I will not leave until you lie down.”

When Geom Mugeuk stubbornly insisted that he would not back down, Sama Myeong had no choice but to lie on the bed.

“Then I shall commit discourtesy against the Acting Cult Leader.”

Thinking that Geom Mugeuk would leave if he at least pretended to sleep quickly, Sama Myeong closed his eyes.

At that very moment, Geom Mugeuk’s hand moved lightly.

The gentlest finger wind Geom Mugeuk had ever unleashed flew out and pressed Sama Myeong’s sleeping point. It was a truly exquisite technique, one that left him unaware that his acupoint had even been pressed.

Softly, without even realizing he was falling asleep, Sama Myeong drifted off.

Geom Mugeuk left the room and quietly spoke only to the attendant strategist who assisted Sama Myeong.

“He will wake on his own in two shichen. Until then, do not wake him even if the Martial Alliance attacks.”

“Yes! I shall obey your command.”

Knowing well how much Sama Myeong overworked his aging body, the attendant strategist bowed respectfully with gratitude on his face.

After leaving Tongcheon Pavilion, Geom Mugeuk returned to the Heavenly Demon Hall and handled the work that had to be done that day.

Fortunately, he could now deal with the affairs within the cult with a fair degree of skill. Whenever he had to make a choice, he always thought the same thing.

What decision would Father make?

Then the choice became easier. Rather than focusing on his own thoughts or opinions, he placed emphasis on what his father would have done. After all, he was merely standing in for his father’s work.

Once he had finished everything he needed to do, Geom Mugeuk headed to the Heavenly Demon Library. His day was busy.

Geom Mugeuk entered the Heavenly Demon Library and immediately chose books.

Few things are as subject to personal taste as reading. That is why people mostly end up reading only the kinds of books they like.

But today as well, Geom Mugeuk picked the books he would read at random.

The unexpected lessons that came from reading without choosing.

The unfamiliar knowledge and impressions gained from books one would never have read because they were outside one’s field of interest were an experience no one who had not gone through it could ever understand.

Geom Mugeuk vaguely sensed it.

It was not that he would gain something by reading many books here in the Heavenly Demon Library, but that he would gain something from the choice to read without discrimination.

After choosing all the books, Geom Mugeuk snapped his fingers.

Snap!

In this field where a cool breeze blew, there stood only one desk and one chair.

Until now, he had tried many methods. He had set up a hammock between trees and read comfortably while lying down, read while leaning against a bed, and read sprawled on a warm heated floor.

But in the end, what he chose was simply sitting at a desk to read. This was the most comfortable.

Today as well, Geom Mugeuk read and read again.

He read secret martial manuals, history books, poetry collections, adventure tales, and the autobiographies of peerless masters. He simply read whatever came to hand.

The more he read, the more he experienced the strange sensation that he was not learning more, but discovering how much more he did not know. Each time he read, his eyes were opened to a new field.

In the past, unless there had been some special reason, he had not read secret martial manuals at all.

Needless to say, with the Nine-Flame Demonic Art, and even the Flying Heaven Sword Art, he possessed supreme martial arts that left him no need to covet others.

But this time, he tried reading other martial arts texts as well. They were more than worth reading. At the very least, they were martial arts good enough to be placed in the Heavenly Demon Library.

Geom Mugeuk set aside the book he had finished and opened the next one.

Principles for the Use of the Iron Weight.

It was a title that truly made one not want to turn the page.

A weight referred to the counterweight of a scale, but in the martial world, there were warriors who used weights as weapons, though they were extremely rare. In fact, a martial artist who used weights was something one might see only once or twice in an entire lifetime.

For that reason, an ordinary martial artist would not even have taken it from the shelf, but Geom Mugeuk was different. He had resolved to read every book without discrimination.

As he turned the pages, Geom Mugeuk tilted his head.

The title of the manual and its contents were different. The contents of the book were martial arts for using a dagger.

Geom Mugeuk examined the cover of the book. Looking closely, he realized that someone had pasted an extra cover over it.

Riiip.

When he tore off the cover, a new title was written underneath. It was a different secret martial manual, disguised with the title of a martial art no one would be drawn to.

The moment he saw the newly revealed title, Geom Mugeuk was startled. It was a secret martial manual he knew. It was also a martial art that someone he knew had desperately desired.

“So it was here!”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze deepened at the depth of the fate arranged by heaven.

He immediately released the Space-Time Transposition Art.

After taking the hidden manual, he also took one more book from the shelf. Both of these books were for him.

At that moment, outside the Heavenly Demon Library, Jeogyeon, the head of his guards, was setting down food.

He could not enter, nor could he remain there, so when he placed food at the entrance like this, empty bowls would later be put out.

Geom Mugeuk usually came out after three or four shichen, but considering that time, he always emerged after eating an astonishing amount of food.

Had he suddenly developed gluttony?

The amount of food Geom Mugeuk requested was so great that it made one wonder whether several people were inside. But without fail, all the dishes came out empty.

Jeogyeon worried aloud to himself.

“Is it truly all right for you to eat this much, Young Cult Leader?”

He did not know. From outside, it was one meal, but because the Secret Art of Time Heaven had been activated, several meals were needed. One shichen outside was seven shichen inside.

At least the fortunate thing was that he did not gain weight. Did reading consume that much mental strength?

“What worries you so much that you are eating like this?”

Just then, the door of the Heavenly Demon Library opened and Geom Mugeuk came out.

“Who else would I be worried about? My elder brother.”

Jeogyeon was startled.

“Young Cult Leader! No, Acting Cult Leader!”

Geom Mugeuk dashed off somewhere in a hurry.

“Don’t ever have an elder brother in your life!”

“That is not something I can decide for myself… But where are you going?”

“To the kitchen.”

“I brought the dishes here!”

“I’m not going to eat. I’m going to prepare a meal.”

“Pardon?”

Leaving the bewildered Jeogyeon behind, Geom Mugeuk vanished from sight in an instant.

* * *

A meal was laid in the place where the Heavenly Demon dined.

It was a meal Geom Mugeuk had personally prepared for one person.

One person entered that place.

It was none other than Geukak Soma, dressed in a pure white martial uniform and a white mask.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been standing before the dishes, greeted him.

“It has been a long time, Lord Soma.”

This was his first reunion with Soma since returning to the cult. He had wanted to see him so badly, but it had been a moment he had treasured and held back.

“I greet the Cult Leader.”

Geukak Soma paid his respects with solemn courtesy. He did not call him Acting Cult Leader, but Cult Leader.

How could Geom Mugeuk not know? The heart behind Geukak Soma’s deliberate omission of the word “acting.”

It was not that he looked down on his father, but that he respected Geom Mugeuk, who had taken the seat of Acting Cult Leader for the first time.

“Being called Cult Leader feels awkward.”

“You will hear it until you grow sick of it someday.”

“Do not even speak of that position. I never knew there was so much work. If I were not so troubled, would someone like me, who hates books, be reading them all in the Heavenly Demon Library?”

“That is not why, is it?”

Geukak Soma knew how Geom Mugeuk felt.

“You must be holding back your desire to run to the Eldest Young Master by reading books.”

“How did you know?”

It was true that he felt that way too. No matter how much he trusted the Poison King and the Demon Buddha, he was still worried about his brother.

The two eyes behind the mask seemed to say:

How could I not know your heart, Young Cult Leader?

Geom Mugeuk asked with a smile,

“Have you ever been here before?”

“This is my first time.”

A place for eating and Geukak Soma were the least compatible of combinations.

That was why this moment today would be all the more meaningful.

“Now, please sit.”

Geom Mugeuk and Geukak Soma sat facing one another.

“I made every dish myself.”

Surprise flickered through Geukak Soma’s eyes.

He knew well the cooking skills Geom Mugeuk had shown while camping, but to prepare such a fine table?

“On the day you removed your mask, did I not say I would serve you food and wine? That day is today.”

The two eyes behind the mask smiled brightly. Geom Mugeuk had not forgotten something he had said in passing.

And there was also a reason Geom Mugeuk had chosen this place.

“This is probably the quietest place in our cult—no, in this entire martial world—to have a meal.”

It meant that he could remove his mask and eat comfortably here. A place where no one could steal a glance at his face.

It was Geom Mugeuk’s consideration for Geukak Soma.

No matter how close they became, no matter how deeply Geukak Soma showed his loyalty, Geom Mugeuk never took this relationship lightly. The more that was so, the more he tried to be courteous and do well by him.

Geukak Soma silently gazed at Geom Mugeuk, then slowly began to remove his mask. The moment he removed his mask now was different from when he had removed it after the previous battle ended.

Geukak Soma placed the pure white smiling mask on the table.

Beneath the mask, Geukak Soma’s face was revealed.

Features perfectly harmonized with kind eyes. As expected, even seeing him again, he was a handsome Geukak Soma.

“I have long looked forward to this day, when I could drink comfortably with you, Young Cult Leader.”

Geukak Soma first courteously poured wine into Geom Mugeuk’s cup, and Geom Mugeuk filled his cup as well.

Their cups met in the air with a clear sound.

After drinking the wine refreshingly, Geom Mugeuk picked up his chopsticks and said,

“Please try the food.”

After tasting the dishes, Geukak Soma was sincerely impressed.

“You made this yourself?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk took out one of the two books he had brought. This book was the second one he had taken from the shelf.

“Such a book was in the Heavenly Demon Library.”

Taesuk Jinmirok (泰熟珍味錄).

That book was a cookbook written by Tae-jung, who had once been the most famous chef in the Central Plains. Among chefs, it was truly a legendary book of secret methods.

“I cooked according to the secrets written here. Of course, the chefs helped me a little. Even if I am driven out from my position as Young Cult Leader now, I will not starve to death. I will be reborn as the chef of the finest delicacies under heaven.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Geukak Soma laughed. Seeing that smile put Geom Mugeuk in a good mood.

Geukak Soma was cooler and more mysterious when he wore the mask. But there was an unconfined joy in that handsome smile.

“Ah, this time I gained a fortuitous encounter in the Heavenly Demon Library.”

“Congratulations. Truly, congratulations.”

Geukak Soma rejoiced as if it were his own good fortune. Yes, Soma was someone who could sincerely share joy, which was said to be ten times harder to share than sorrow.

That was why he, too, could bring this out with such a pleasant heart.

“It is not a fortuitous encounter meant for me.”

To the puzzled Geukak Soma, Geom Mugeuk took a book from inside his robe. It was the secret martial manual that had been hidden beneath another cover.

Laughing Fragrance Dagger Art (笑香匕術).

Geukak Soma was startled when he saw the title of the manual. It was the first time Geom Mugeuk had seen him so surprised.

“I brought it as a gift for you, Lord Soma.”

At that, Geukak Soma asked in a trembling voice.

“How did you know I was searching for this martial art?”

Because you told me.

The Geukak Soma from the life before my regression. The man who had wanted to become my friend.

Mageuk Gwangpokjang, Hyeorangji, and Sohyang Bisul.

He had said that if these three martial arts were brought into harmony, they would display truly invincible divine might.

But at some point, Sohyang Bisul had been lost, and only Mageuk Gwangpokjang and Hyeorangji were passed down to later generations of Geukak Soma.

Whatever story lay in the past, that Sohyang Bisul had been hidden in the Heavenly Demon Library.

Geom Mugeuk asked, feigning ignorance.

“You were looking for this martial art?”

“I was.”

“When we fought before, did you not use daggers against your enemies, Lord Soma? At the time, I thought it would be good if there were at least one fine martial art for using daggers. Then, when I happened to discover this martial art and saw that it contained the character so (笑), meaning ‘to laugh,’ I thought it might suit you, Lord Soma, and brought it out.”

Geukak Soma’s eyes trembled. Now that he could see Geukak Soma’s entire expression, he could feel his emotion in full.

Geukak Soma was once again astonished that he and Geom Mugeuk were bound by such a deep fate. And he was deeply grateful to Geom Mugeuk. He felt that this gratitude was something he would have to repay not only in this life, but even in the next.

“This is a secret manual I have desperately wanted.”

Even his voice trembled.

‘This is not a secret manual I am giving him.’

It was a gift from the man he had been in the past, from the aged him of the distant future, to himself.

If he had not told him about it, Geom Mugeuk would never have thought to give this martial art to Geukak Soma.

Geom Mugeuk thought of the Geukak Soma from before his regression.

‘Don’t feel too sorrowful anymore. You and I have become friends like this.’

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly and held out the secret manual.

“What a relief. Since it’s something you need. I really do have good luck.”

But Geukak Soma could not readily accept the secret manual. He spoke worriedly.

“As you know, the martial arts secret manuals of the Heavenly Demon Library cannot be taken out without the Cult Leader’s permission.”

“Am I not the Cult Leader? You called me Cult Leader earlier, didn’t you?”

“No, that was…”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, Geukak Soma looked troubled. No matter how much he cared for Geom Mugeuk, his respect and loyalty toward Geom Ujin remained unchanged. With Geom Ujin absent, accepting this martial art weighed on his mind.

He could have made a show of it if he wanted, but Geom Mugeuk wanted Soma to accept it comfortably.

Geom Mugeuk made a gesture of covering his mouth and whispered softly.

“I’ve already given Elder Doma a book from the Heavenly Demon Library as well. And this cookbook will go to the cook of the Heavenly Demon Hall. So you may accept it with an easy heart.”

Geom Mugeuk added in an even smaller voice. Though he did not know whether such a thing was possible in the main cult, where there were no secrets.

“We just have to make sure Father doesn’t find out.”

Chapter 624: If You're Giving Gifts, Give Three
-----------------------------------------------
“Understood. I gratefully accept the secret manual.”

Geugak Soma respectfully received the secret manual.

No matter how desperately he had desired this secret manual, if the one giving it had not been Geom Mugeuk, he would never have accepted it. Yes, if it had not been the Young Cult Leader, never.

He was the one who had retrieved exactly this secret manual from amidst all those countless books. The Young Cult Leader was someone bound to him by such a deep fate.

Therefore, Geugak Soma gladly accepted the Sohyang Bisul and made no effort to hide his joy.

Having removed his mask today, he smiled more brightly than ever.

Geugak Soma poured wine for Geom Mugeuk.

“What will you do when the Cult Leader finds out later?”

“What is there to do? It is already too late. By then, Lord Soma, you will have fully mastered the martial arts. If Father demands it back, I shall return it.”

Soma laughed at Geom Mugeuk’s shameless strategy.

Though he spoke that way, Geom Mugeuk was certain.

Father would not ask for it back. Instead, as punishment, he would make him do something else.

This time, Geom Mugeuk filled Soma’s cup.

“My congratulations, Lord Soma.”

It was not congratulations from Geom Mugeuk to Soma, but from one martial artist to another. For obtaining the martial arts that would complete oneself was a moment every martial artist dreamed of, even in their dreams.

The two emptied their cups once more.

And Geom Mugeuk’s gifts were not limited to that alone.

“I suppose it is time for this to display its true worth.”

Geom Mugeuk drew a dagger from within his robes.

It was a Myriad Year Cold Iron dagger forged by melting the shackles of Gowol.

“I wish to present this to Lord Soma.”

Soma knew what kind of dagger this was.

“Is this not a dagger that holds meaning for the Young Cult Leader?”

“That is correct.”

He had divided the metal and had daggers made, sharing them with those who had been present when the shackles were disposed of.

“Yet you would give this to me?”

“It is an item with meaning. I could not give Lord Soma something meaningless.”

It meant that he gave it precisely because it held meaning, not that its meaning prevented him from giving it.

Those who had shared it with him would surely understand.

“I am fine without it.”

Geugak Soma adamantly refused, but Geom Mugeuk had already made up his mind to give it.

“I wield a sword, and when I do not wield a sword, I use fist techniques. In truth, the only time I use this dagger is to peel fruit. A dagger born of Myriad Year Cold Iron—does it not deserve better than such a pitiful existence?”

Geom Mugeuk extended the dagger.

“Please save this dagger.”

Still, Geugak Soma did not accept it. The Sohyang Bisul alone was already too great a gift to receive today.

Then Geom Mugeuk grasped the blade and showed him the hilt.

“It is already too late, Lord Soma.”

A character was engraved on the hilt.

So (笑).

A sigh escaped Geugak Soma. Young Cult Leader, how can you show me such profound affection?

In the end, he could refuse no longer.

“Thank you, Cult Leader.”

He respectfully accepted the dagger Geom Mugeuk offered.

And so the Myriad Year Cold Iron dagger found its rightful master.

Geom Mugeuk looked forward to the day. The day when the Sohyang Bisul would be unleashed from that dagger held in Soma’s hand. The moment when his three martial arts would harmonize and he would be reborn as a perfect martial artist.

“By the way, did you know? The cult rules state that if you give a gift, you must give three.”

“There is such a rule?”

“When I became the acting Cult Leader, I looked it up and found that there is. So I had no choice but to prepare one more.”

Geom Mugeuk opened a large box he had prepared in advance beneath the table.

Inside the box, divided into several compartments, were spirit medicines.

They were the very spirit medicines he had collected from the underground vault he had previously descended to with the Sword King.

At that time, the Sword King had said this:

“Feeding them to others is a waste of effort. Just eat them yourself while you can.”

But the mouth he was feeding it to now was neither his own, nor was it a stranger’s.

He could have distributed them, one root each, to the demonic lords, but Geom Mugeuk did not.

If it was his fate to have discovered the Sohyang Bisul, then giving these spirit medicines to Geugak Soma would also be fate.

Geugak Soma silently met Geom Mugeuk’s gaze.

Geom Mugeuk said nothing else. He spoke only one sentence. The most powerful sentence to persuade Soma.

“Become stronger.”

In an instant, fierce emotion swept across Geugak Soma’s face.

He was one who had crossed life-and-death moments with Geom Mugeuk countless times.

He knew better than anyone what enemies they faced, and he well knew that absurdly powerful beings existed. That was why he knew Geom Mugeuk was striving even harder.

Yes, the Young Cult Leader was telling him this now.

Grow stronger and fight alongside me.

Geugak Soma likewise said nothing more. He no longer refused the gifts.

He slowly donned the white mask resting on the table once more. He too spoke only a single sentence.

“I will become stronger.”

* * *

Geom Muyang had still not left Wuhan.

The second hideout was not far from the first. Rather, it was closer to the main compound of the Martial Alliance.

It defied expectations that he would flee far away.

Geom Muyang stood by the window, gazing outside as it began to grow dark.

Gwak Yeong silently watched his back.

The most taciturn back in the world.

Could the back of someone she had met only a few days ago hold such great meaning?

She wanted to say something to him, but did not know what words to speak.

She had always been bright and confident.

The reason words did not come easily was guilt. She had never dreamed that her brother might have applied Pursuit Incense to his letter. Because of her, these people had nearly faced great danger.

Was he angry?

No. There was nothing more infuriating than asking an angry person if they were angry.

What will you do now?

What good is knowing that! Yes, that was not a question for her to ask.

In the end, the words she found were these:

“Use me to catch them.”

Had her words been too obvious? The taciturn back merely continued to stare out the window.

Geom Muyang did not place the blame on her.

He had anticipated there would be a reason she was kept alive. It was his own failure that he had not identified that reason in advance.

Then Branch Leader Homyeong entered and reported:

“The White Dragon Unit has begun to comb through Wuhan.”

Since this hideout was not a thoroughly concealed inner sanctuary of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, it would eventually be discovered by their search.

“How long until this place is found?”

“A day at the earliest, two days at the most.”

It was far too little time to resolve the problem.

Homyeong looked at Gwak Yeong.

“If that woman returns to the Alliance, the search will be halted.”

Because the purpose of their search was to find Gwak Yeong.

At that, Gwak Yeong quickly stepped back.

“Don’t tell me to go out! I won’t go out. Call me selfish if you must, but I cannot.”

Her instincts told her: if she went out, she would die.

“I will hide in a storehouse if you tell me to, and I will dig a hole in the ground and breathe through a reed if that is what it takes. Just don’t tell me to go back.”

When Geom Muyang and Homyeong said nothing, she finally let out a sigh.

Insisting like this was a selfish decision, just as she had said. Because the one who could save her brother was not her, but that man.

“I will go out. But please, keep your promise.”

Then Geom Muyang spoke in a low voice.

“If you go out, you will die.”

“I am not afraid of dying.”

She was prepared to gladly die if it meant saving her brother.

“Not you. Your brother will die.”

“!”

“As long as you are with us, you have value as leverage.”

Hearing those words, Gwak Yeong realized. Even now, she had value as leverage. Because she was the reason these people were being chased by the White Dragon Unit.

They could make other demands using her brother’s life as collateral. Because they did not know what role she might play, they would keep her brother alive.

Gwak Yeong looked at Geom Muyang with renewed eyes.

“You’re from the demonic path, yet so clever!”

The words had slipped out, and she flinched in surprise, covering her mouth. When a hint of bewilderment flashed in Geom Muyang’s eyes, she flushed red and lowered her head.

“I’m sorry. The words I was thinking in my head just popped out without thinking.”

Fortunately, a subordinate entered the room just then, delivering news from the Tongcheongak.

“The Tongcheongak has identified the location where the counterfeit weapons were made.”

Based on Gwak Yeong’s testimony, the Tongcheongak had succeeded in analyzing the location.

“They say they were manufactured in a warehouse building located in a remote area about twenty li away.”

Going there would be meaningless. It would already be empty.

“Who is the owner of the warehouse?”

“A man named Sogong.”

Hearing the name, Gwak Yeong was startled.

“I know him! He is a merchant who supplies materials to the Martial Alliance’s Iron Workshop.”

He was someone she was acquainted with. He had traded with the Martial Alliance’s Iron Workshop for a long time. That man was in league with them? She could not believe it.

Homyeong spoke to Geom Muyang.

“I will go and capture him.”

“Let us go together.”

Because of who their opponent was, they had to move carefully.

As Geom Muyang was about to leave, Gwak Yeong called out loudly. She was not going to be intimidated by her earlier mistake; she was going to see them off spiritedly.

“Safe travels!”

Geom Muyang glanced at her, then left without a word.

Once the two left, she muttered to herself in a low voice.

“Couldn’t you at least respond when someone speaks to you?”

* * *

Sogong woke from sleep.

A masked man was looking down at him. It was Homyeong.

Sogong startled in fright, trying to scream, but no voice came out. His vital point had already been suppressed.

“If you make a sound, not only you but everyone in this house dies.”

There was no more terrifying threat than that, so Sogong nodded.

Only then did Homyeong release his vital point.

Sogong sat up. There was another masked man in the room. Geom Muyang was sitting in a chair in the corner, watching him.

“What do you want?”

He had been a merchant his whole life. The fact that they had not killed him yet meant they wanted something from him.

It was Homyeong who asked. He cut straight to the chase and asked what he wanted to know.

“The warehouse—who did you lend it to?”

“I don’t know what you’re talking about.”

He denied it flatly, but his eyes were already shaking.

Homyeong immediately pressed down firmly on his shoulder. It was the pressure point used for torture.

As terrible pain surged through him, just as Sogong was about to scream, terrifying words flowed from Homyeong that made the scream retreat back into his throat.

“Anyone who wakes up and runs here because of your voice dies.”

Sogong gritted his teeth. Amidst the surging agony, he let out a silent scream.

After a moment, when Homyeong removed his hand from the shoulder, Sogong was drenched in sweat.

“……I cannot tell you.”

His words had changed from “I don’t know” to “I cannot tell you.”

This time, Homyeong pressed the pressure point even harder.

A mere merchant, he had endured once, but he could not endure a second time.

“Leader Do!”

What came from his mouth was not a name, but a title.

“Leader Do? Which Leader Do?”

To Homyeong’s question, Sogong answered with a trembling voice.

“The White Dragon Unit’s Leader Do.”

Geom Muyang was shocked, and Homyeong was shocked.

The White Dragon Unit’s leader, Doinsu.

They had never expected his name to come up.

Geom Muyang rose from his seat and approached Sogong. Intimidated by his momentum, Sogong quickly added. There was a reason he had been unable to answer easily.

“Leader Do said he was borrowing it for Alliance business. He said he was carrying out a secret operation and I must never speak of it.”

Geom Muyang, now close, stared intently into Sogong’s eyes.

“I am not lying. Please, spare me.”

Geom Muyang could tell he was not lying. The White Dragon Leader was not a name that could come up as a lie.

Geom Muyang spoke to him with cold eyes.

“If you reveal that we came here, things will become complicated. As a merchant, you can calculate as much. Just think of it as a bad dream.”

Tap.

Sogong, his sleep point suppressed, fell asleep.

Geom Muyang and Homyeong left Sogong’s house as stealthily as they had entered.

Only after reaching a secluded forest far from there did they remove their masks.

Homyeong spoke in a low tone.

“I never expected the White Dragon Leader to be involved in this matter.”

He could have been a villain from the start.

Or, as he had done with Gwak Yeong earlier, they could be blackmailing the White Dragon Leader by exploiting his weakness.

Whichever the case, he was not someone they could easily touch. If things went wrong, it could lead to a major conflict between the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Martial Alliance.

Homyeong inwardly tensed at what decision Geom Muyang would make. The moment he suggested going after the White Dragon Leader, this fight would enter a new phase.

And as Homyeong expected, Geom Muyang chose a frontal breakthrough.

“The clues lead to the White Dragon Leader, so we must meet him.”

Homyeong respectfully answered Geom Muyang’s words.

“Yes! I will request all information on him from the Tongcheongak!”

The two flew back toward their hideout.

After they disappeared, a short while later a man flew in swiftly and landed silently in that spot.

One could guess how great his skill was from his movement art alone.

Landing with his white changsam fluttering, he was extremely tall. His face was large, his eyes were large, and his arms and legs were long, and at his waist hung a brilliant blue sword.

He was none other than Byeoksan, the absolute master who had come to Wuhan this time together with Hanwolgaek.

He was the blue flag that had been pinned in the Tongcheongak operations room.

“You wretched things! Today will be your last day.”

Just as Byeoksan was about to secretly pursue the two again, he suddenly halted and looked to one side. Someone was here.

“Come out.”

True to his words, a voice came from behind a tree some distance away.

“The Buddha always taught us to show mercy.”

From behind the tree, a golden light began to flow forth faintly.

Chapter 625: Don’t Bring Out My Old Temper
------------------------------------------
At the end of the golden radiance, the Demon Buddha revealed himself.

The moment he saw him, Azure Mountain Sword’s face creased.

“Has that Buddha of yours ever shown mercy even once?”

Rolling the prayer beads in his hand, the Demon Buddha replied.

“A misunderstanding. Our Buddha treats this pitiful sentient being with compassion. Though there are very rare times when he cannot restrain his temper.”

With a leisurely expression, the Demon Buddha slowly walked toward him.

The two stood facing each other with only a few steps between them.

Azure Mountain Sword was so tall that one had to crane one’s neck to look up at him. The sight of the two of them was like the tallest man in the world standing face-to-face with the smallest.

“It seems you’ve grown even taller since last I saw you, my friend.”

“Who decided we were friends?”

The two already knew each other.

Since his youth, the Demon Buddha had wandered all over the Central Plains and associated with many masters.

Why else would he have been friends with the Cult Leader Pungcheon beyond the frontier?

“How many times have we met?”

Azure Mountain Sword remembered precisely.

“Three.”

“If we have met three times alive in this perilous murim, then we are friends. Are we not?”

Azure Mountain Sword’s response was cold.

“I say this only now, but you are far too rash.”

The Demon Buddha had always been skilled at saying things that touched people’s nerves. Because of that, he often turned his opponents’ insides upside down.

“In all three times we met, not once did you fail to offend me.”

At that, the Demon Buddha asked calmly.

“Was anything I said wrong?”

Azure Mountain Sword’s expression twitched. It was precisely because the words had not been wrong that they had offended him. They had struck at the heart of the matter, one after another.

“My neck hurts from looking up.”

The Demon Buddha turned around and perched himself on a rock. After staring at the ground for a moment, he said something unexpected.

“It was probably because of your height. I felt as if you had taken what I did not possess. That was why I said even more hateful things.”

Because these were words he would never have spoken in the past, Azure Mountain Sword instead grew wary of him.

“Do not play tricks.”

As if he understood, the Demon Buddha nodded. Saying such a thing was also due to Geom Mugeuk’s influence. Bringing up his own height himself? In the past, that would have been unimaginable.

Azure Mountain Sword stirred his majestic internal force and shouted.

“How dare you set foot here! You are now treading upon land you absolutely must not tread!”

The reason he had come here in the first place was to root out the Demon Cult bastards who had entered Wuhan.

Sitting on the rock, the Demon Buddha looked down at his own feet and said,

“I’m not touching the ground.”

Because the Demon Buddha’s legs were short, his feet did not reach the ground.

Of course, Azure Mountain Sword did not take it as a joke, but as mockery. Killing intent rose from his body.

“Yes, you always did enjoy mocking people.”

Staring straight at him, the Demon Buddha asked,

“Do you know who the person you tried to kill today was?”

Azure Mountain Sword gave no answer, but it could be known from his eyes and expression.

“So you knew he was the First Young Master.”

The Demon Buddha’s eyes shone coldly, tinged with gold. It was a phenomenon that appeared only in those who had achieved great success in his unique martial art, the Golden Great Lama Art.

Azure Mountain Sword drew up his internal energy, ready to draw his sword at any moment. Even before the Demon Buddha, he was not intimidated.

“If he entered Wuhan without the Martial Alliance’s permission, he deserves to die. The same goes for you.”

He released his aura. Grass and leaves all shook at once, and in an instant the surrounding air sank coldly.

Because he had never once fought the Demon Buddha in a life-and-death duel, he did not know what the outcome of their battle would be. But he was confident he would win.

Azure Mountain Sword’s hand slowly moved toward the hilt of his sword.

He was a master of the orthodox faction to the bone. Though not as much as Hanwol Sword, there was no way his feelings toward the Demon Cult were favorable. Moreover, the Demon Buddha was an opponent he had long intended to discipline properly one day.

“You know that if you draw that blade, there will be no turning back, don’t you?”

At the Demon Buddha’s question, Azure Mountain Sword answered coldly.

“Eradicating evil heretics is the path I have walked all my life. I will have no regrets.”

Amid the taut tension, the Demon Buddha spoke quietly.

“I respect that. You are a fine man of the orthodox faction.”

Because it was the first time he had ever heard such words from the Demon Buddha, Azure Mountain Sword flinched.

He was certainly different today from the past.

With eyes that asked what scheme he was trying to pull, he glared at the Demon Buddha.

The Demon Buddha looked up at the sky. Between the trees, the stars of the night sky could be seen.

“These days, my life has become more unhurried. I am no longer the man I was.”

Azure Mountain Sword mocked him.

“That must be because you have become a kite with its string cut.”

He knew well that the Demon Buddha had supported the First Young Master.

“Are you and the First Young Master not both useless now? If I kill you, I suspect your Demon Cult would thank me instead.”

At the mention of Geom Muyang, the Demon Buddha’s expression turned cold.

“Azure Mountain Sword.”

After the Demon Buddha called him coldly,

“I am speaking civilly. Do not make my old temper come out.”

Azure Mountain Sword knew well that those words, at least, were no bluff. He also knew that the Demon Buddha had the skill to say such a thing. And that he had a foul temper.

But if he had feared him for that, he would not have revealed in the first place that he had pursued the First Young Master. Today, this was a place where one of the two had to die.

“Changed, you say. As if.”

Azure Mountain Sword slowly drew his sword.

“Demon Cult bastards are not things one can fix and use again.”

At that moment, the Demon Buddha roared.

“Shut your mouth! If I speak nicely, then listen nicely. Where do you get off spouting this goddamn nonsense?”

After pouring that out, the Demon Buddha sighed.

“I apologize for cursing. Lately I have been too subdued and withdrawn. Seeing you after so long, I find it difficult to control my emotions.”

Perhaps that was why Geom Mugeuk had kept telling him not to sink down. To live a life that suited him.

“Madman.”

Azure Mountain Sword aimed his sword.
