So Jeongnak, who had been explaining to the physicians, turned his head to look at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“It is an extremely deadly poison against which no antidote in the world has any effect.”

Geom Mugeuk threw himself flat on the ground, clutched his leg, and pleaded.

“Please save my brother! He is my only brother. Please tell me you are one of those three people!”

Then So Jeongnak pulled Geom Mugeuk to his feet.

“That is right. I am one of those three.”

Geom Mugeuk rejoiced and grabbed his hand.

“What a relief! Physician! Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

Even when Geom Mugeuk touched him, he did not reject it at all. Rather, he gripped Geom Mugeuk’s hand tightly and said,

“In that sense, your brother is truly a fortunate man. There are exactly three people in the Central Plains who can detoxify this poison, and he was poisoned near the place where one of them is.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk released the hand he had been holding and took a step back.

“Then my brother was poisoned because of you, Physician?”

“What? What do you mean by that all of a sudden?”

Not only So Jeongnak, but also the physicians behind him stiffened. Their expressions said, How dare you say such a thing to So Jeongnak?

Geom Mugeuk, however, raised his voice instead.

“No matter how foul my brother’s temper is and how many enemies he has, would he just happen to be struck by such a rare poison in the teahouse right in front of Myeongsim Clinic? This is someone challenging you, Physician. They’re asking, Can you detoxify this poison or not! Isn’t that right?”

When Geom Mugeuk instead pressed him like that, So Jeongnak could not refute him.

“Take responsibility. Save my brother! If my brother dies, I’ll let everyone in the world know! I’ll tell them you let my brother die!”

Even in pain, Geom Muyang, who was lying down, shook his head. They really were on the same side, but how could he be that shameless?

“Save my brother!”

So Jeongnak was flustered. Even though it was a situation where he would have had every right to burst into anger and drive them out.

“Very well. I will detoxify him.”

So Jeongnak had the physicians who had come with him bring medicines, medicinal herbs, and the tools needed for treatment.

If one only looked at him now, he seemed like an innocent and pure man who had spent his whole life studying medicine.

Even while forcing his way and throwing a fit, Geom Mugeuk did not miss a single look in So Jeongnak’s eyes or a single word he spoke. He watched how he reacted and how he spoke.

Where did that confidence come from, that he did not mind at all even when his leg was grabbed and his hand was seized? Are you truly an ordinary physician?

A little while later, the physicians brought the medicines, herbs, and medicine bottles he had told them to bring.

“Great fortune must accompany this treatment. It is such a difficult detoxification that it will fail if the herbs are combined even slightly incorrectly.”

So Jeongnak closed his eyes for a moment and organized his thoughts.

Geom Mugeuk had naturally assumed that he would send everyone away or enter some room and make the antidote alone before coming out.

“Now, listen carefully to what I say from this point on.”

So Jeongnak began making the antidote while explaining it to the physicians.

At that sight, Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang looked at each other. Even amid his pain, his older brother seemed truly surprised by this scene, his expression full of astonishment.

“You said this is a poison that only three people in the world can detoxify, yet you are teaching everyone how to make it?”

Not only the physicians, but even he and his brother were listening, and so were the other patients.

The reason So Jeongnak gave was even more unexpected.

“It is not a secret method for making poison, but a secret method for making an antidote. What would I gain by hoarding it? As a physician, what pride is there in being the only one who knows how to detoxify something?”

Then he said to the physicians,

“When it comes to saving people, do not keep secret methods of your own. Spread them widely, and then spread them again, so that even one more life that need not die may be saved.”

“We will keep it in mind!”

The physicians’ faces were full of respect.

He combined the ingredients carefully. Of course, there were critical moments in the process. When the combination did not go as he intended, he would sink into thought for a long while.

Geom Mugeuk truly had never imagined that he would come here and watch him make the antidote. And that while even passing it down to the physicians.

If the quantity was even slightly wrong, he made it again. If the ingredients were not fresh, he had new ones brought and made it again. If the color he had in mind did not appear, he mixed it again.

He concentrated so intensely that his whole body was soon drenched in sweat. Perhaps because he was large, he sweated a great deal as well.

After a full two double-hours of effort, the antidote was completed.

So Jeongnak took a deep breath and administered the antidote to Geom Muyang.

Everyone watched tensely.

As the pain subsided, Geom Muyang’s face became somewhat more at ease.

“It succeeded.”

At that, all the other patients there cheered and applauded.

“The antidote must be taken three times over three days. I will come again tomorrow morning.”

So Jeongnak, completely drained, staggered as he walked. The physicians supported him by both arms and walked out with him.

Watching his retreating back, Geom Mugeuk looked down at Geom Muyang and said,

“Somehow, I feel like we’ve become the villains.”

With a face that now seemed a little more alive, Geom Muyang said in a weak voice,

“We are the villains.”

* * *

The next day as well, So Jeongnak combined the medicine with utmost care.

If anything, it took even longer than on the first day.

He asked the physicians questions and, in the middle, even had them do the mixing themselves. He truly was sincere about educating the junior physicians.

“You should make it yourself! What do those people know!”

Even when Geom Mugeuk shouted, it was no use.

“It is fine because they are making it as I instruct.”

“What do you mean, fine? Can’t you see his hand trembling?”

Despite Geom Mugeuk’s protests, he did not give up on making the junior physicians do it.

“Who are the other two people you said can make the antidote?”

At that, So Jeongnak had them stop mixing for a moment and looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“Of course, the Divine Physician of the Martial Alliance must be included, right?”

Though he knew that was not the case, when he casually mentioned him, So Jeongnak shook his head.

“If we speak only of medical arts, there is no one who can surpass Senior Divine Physician of the Martial Alliance or the Demon Physician of the Demonic Cult. But just because one’s medical arts are the best does not mean one can detoxify poison the best as well. That is a separate matter.”

Then a name that was extremely welcome to Geom Mugeuk’s ears flowed out.

“There is someone called Chungui. She treats illnesses using poisonous insects. If it is her, she should be fully capable of treating this poison.”

Yes, how could she be left out, when she had made him immune to all poisons?

If his brother had truly been in an urgent situation after being struck by a deadly poison, he would have carried him on his back and run to her with Swift Steps.

‘Lady Chungui! You are doing well, aren’t you?’

He wondered whether Pyo Gwang, who had become her disciple, was massaging her shoulders properly.

“Who is the other one?”

After a brief pause, he spoke. He was far more cautious than when he had mentioned Chungui earlier.

“The Poison King of the Demonic Cult.”

Geom Mugeuk carefully observed his expression and words when he mentioned the Poison King.

“Do you know the Poison King?”

“I have never seen him. But I have heard of his reputation.”

“What sort of person is the Poison King?”

As if he did not even want to think about it, he once again instructed the physicians on how to combine the medicine.

“If you keep doing this, I’ll carry my brother and go to Lady Chungui. Where does she live? Do you think I can’t find the Demonic Cult?”

Still, it was no use.

And so, the antidote for the second day, which So Jeongnak made together with the junior physicians, was completed.

He confidently administered the completed antidote to Geom Muyang.

Then his complexion returned even more than yesterday.

“It is a success.”

Geom Mugeuk was relieved.

“I am sorry for shouting for no reason earlier.”

“It is all right.”

“Tomorrow, I will make it. Together with that patient over there with the broken arm.”

At that joke, So Jeongnak smiled brightly. With even a smile added, he truly looked like a good person. So much so that Geom Mugeuk felt sorry for doubting him.

“I will say it again, but your brother truly is a fortunate man.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward his brother.

“I had always thought my brother was a very unlucky man.”

He was someone who had been pushed out of the position of successor to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult because of his younger brother, so how could he be fortunate?

Then Geom Muyang said quietly,

“I think I am fortunate too.”

Others would think he said it because he had met So Jeongnak.

But Geom Muyang’s answer held another meaning.

‘Having a younger brother like you is also good fortune.’

Because even after losing that succession struggle, he had not died and was still alive like this.

Given his personality, even if he had lived, it would not have felt like living. But because he had lost to such an extraordinary opponent, he had been made to feel not particularly wronged.

“Then I will see you tomorrow for the final treatment.”

That day as well, Geom Mugeuk went around Myeongsim Clinic and examined the place.

The patients were out of their minds from pain, and the physicians were out of their minds from busyness. So Jeongnak was busy, and the other physicians were busy too. Patients flooded in endlessly. Amid the crossroads of life and death, there was no gap for suspicion to seep in.

He looked here and there, but could find nothing suspicious. The only suspicious person in this Myeongsim Clinic was himself.

* * *

“How is your body?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Geom Muyang nodded. The pain that had felt as if his whole body were burning had vanished without a trace. After taking the final, third antidote, he was completely recovered.

Over the three days, So Jeongnak had not made a single mistake.

“You are truly a divine physician!”

When Geom Mugeuk exclaimed in admiration, So Jeongnak smiled.

Geom Muyang also expressed his gratitude to him.

“Thank you truly. How should I repay this grace?”

Then Geom Mugeuk said from beside him,

“Why ask? We have to repay him with money. How much is the treatment fee?”

Considering the poison, he thought the price would be whatever the man named.

“Just go. If anything, I’m the one who should be grateful for the chance to detoxify such a precious poison.”

So Jeong-rak patted Geom Mugeuk on the shoulder.

“I was truly moved by how much you care for your older brother. Do continue to get along well.”

After smiling so brightly, So Jeong-rak turned away.

On the first day, it had been a large-built physician walking toward them, but now, it was an immortal walking away.

Geom Mugeuk and Geom Muyang left Myeongsim Clinic and set off toward their lodging.

“At least with So Jeong-rak, we misjudged him.”

He was, in the truest sense, a divine physician sent by the heavens.

If it had been any poison other than the Omang Divine Poison, then they really would have spent three entire days suffering in vain.

But thanks to the effects of the Omang Divine Poison, he had gained a powerful resistance to poison.

Just then, unexpected words flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“That man is definitely someone who uses poison arts.”

Sensing that he was not joking, Geom Muyang stopped walking.

On the first day, he had been in too much pain to think straight, but even through that, he had watched and listened to So Jeong-rak carefully.

As far as he could judge, there had been nothing suspicious about him. If anything, that was what made him suspicious.

But he was definitely someone who used poison arts? Why on earth?

Geom Mugeuk calmly explained his reasoning.

“When he confirmed that the poison hyung had been struck with was the Omang Divine Poison, he was genuinely delighted. That joy was clearly not a physician’s joy, but the joy of a master of poison arts.”

Geom Muyang nodded, but he thought that could not serve as proof that So Jeong-rak was a poison practitioner.

“He’s famous for detoxification, so couldn’t he have been happy because it was a rare poison?”

When Geom Muyang spoke cautiously, Geom Mugeuk offered another piece of evidence.

“Do you remember what he said on the first day? He said he thought the Omang Divine Poison had been lost because it hadn’t appeared in the present age. Saying that a poison has been ‘lost’ is the sort of thing said by those who use poison arts.”

Geom Muyang tilted his head.

“He’s someone with great interest in poisons. He could have said it that way.”

That was not the only point Geom Mugeuk found suspicious.

“He explained the poison in such detail, so why didn’t he mention that?”

“Mention what?”

“The person capable of administering this poison. If he could say there were only three people capable of detoxifying it, it would have been natural for him to say there was only so-and-so who could administer it.”

This time, Geom Muyang nodded as well.

“He didn’t say it because he already knew who it was?”

That guess was plausible. But all three points were only circumstantial; there was no definite physical proof.

“Aren’t you finding him more suspicious because you decided from the start that he was the one behind it?”

“No. It’s definitely him.”

For the first time, Geom Muyang felt as though he was seeing a flaw in his younger brother. It felt as if Geom Mugeuk was forcing everything to fit just to push through his own argument.

‘So you had this side to you too.’

He did not dislike it. Until now, Geom Mugeuk had only shown an unbearably perfect side. Yes, he needed to have gaps like this for me to help him.

As they walked on like that, Geom Mugeuk suddenly spoke as if something had occurred to him.

“Ah, there was one more thing.”

“What is it?”

You don’t have to pretend to be so perfect with me.

“When he patted my shoulder earlier as we parted…”

Geom Muyang assumed it would be another circumstantial suspicion.

But shocking words flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“He poisoned me.”

“!”

After staring blankly for a moment, Geom Muyang shouted.

“You should have said that first!”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 641

Chapter 641: Or a Way Not to Die
--------------------------------
“What poison is it?”

Geom Muyang’s heart dropped. He had truly never imagined that So Jeongrak would have poisoned his younger brother.

The man had neutralized the Omang Divine Poison administered by the Poison King. How could a poison administered by someone like that be ordinary?

“Answer me! What poison was it?”

Geom Mugeuk had never seen his elder brother this agitated before. Even when he had lost the position of successor, his brother had not been this shaken.

“To think seeing you worked up could be this moving.”

“You brat! Don’t joke around!”

Geom Muyang was genuinely angry. Staring straight at his startled, agitated, furious brother’s face, Geom Mugeuk spoke calmly.

“Brother, I’m fine.”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes were calm. The way he stood and the aura he gave off did not make him seem ill in the slightest.

“How are you fine when you were poisoned?”

“To me, poison liquid is water, and poison smoke is smoke from a campfire. No, it’s less than that. It doesn’t even sting my eyes.”

“Stop talking nonsense!”

“I’m immune to all poisons.”

“Fine, you’re immune to all pois—what? What did you just say?”

Geom Muyang’s eyes widened as he stared at Geom Mugeuk. What did I just hear? Looking at his brother’s expression, Geom Mugeuk said it again.

“I said I’m immune to all poisons.”

For a moment, Geom Muyang could not say anything. He knew better than anyone how strong his younger brother was, how gifted a martial talent he had been born with, and how extraordinary a person he was.

But immune to all poisons?

Immunity to all poisons was an entirely different matter from all of that.

Even in the history of the martial world, there were only a handful of martial artists who had achieved such a state. That was how difficult it was.

It was a realm that could only be reached not through an ordinary fortuitous encounter, but through one bestowed by the heavens themselves. That was what immunity to all poisons meant to Geom Muyang.

That was why he suspected until the very end that his younger brother might be joking. But from Geom Mugeuk’s utterly serious eyes, he could tell. It was true. He really was immune to all poisons. The words that followed proved it.

“If I weren’t immune to all poisons, I would have taken charge of this matter somehow. I wouldn’t have let you handle something that dangerous.”

No wonder he had been so stubborn about making him play the patient.

Geom Mugeuk smiled and added a joke.

“Even if you want to kill me, poison won’t work.”

Geom Muyang did not feel jealous. On the contrary, he felt good. After all, he had lost the fight for succession to a younger brother with such an extraordinary destiny.

“How did it happen?”

“I was lucky enough to gain a fortuitous encounter while saving Pavilion Master Seo of Hwangcheon Pavilion.”

Perhaps this was the reason the heavens were helping his younger brother.

The Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult was living a life of saving, not killing. How could the heavens fail to take notice?

Every time he stopped a tavern table from being smashed, every time he ran to save someone, perhaps fragments of fortuity for his younger brother were gathering one by one.

“What does it mean that So Jeongrak poisoned you?”

“It means he’s someone who uses poison arts.”

At the same time, it also meant that the place backing Cheonae Hermit was definitely Myeongsim Clinic.

Geom Muyang thought of So Jeongrak. He remembered the devoted way the man had cared for patients. He remembered how passionately he had taught the younger physicians.

“Was all of that an act?”

At Geom Muyang’s doubtful question, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze deepened. It would be better if it had all simply been hypocrisy and acting.

“Or perhaps, after acting for so long, it became his real life.”

He could feel that dealing with this So Jeongrak would be no easier than dealing with Cheonae Hermit Hwa Yulcheong. After all, they had to deal with a man who lived such a respected life right in front of the Martial Alliance’s main headquarters.

“Let’s head back for now. We need to discuss this with the Poison King and Demon Buddha.”

* * *

When the two entered the estate, Geom Mugeuk burst into laughter at the sight spread out before him.

The courtyard was filled with medicinal herbs laid out to dry. He could tell without looking. During the three days they had been at the clinic, their Demon Buddha must have been subjected to terrible labor exploitation.

“We’re back.”

When Geom Mugeuk spoke loudly, Demon Buddha came out of the building first.

“You’ve returned?”

Demon Buddha looked relieved to see that Geom Muyang was safe.

There was no way Geom Mugeuk would let that pass.

“I’m here too! The temporary Cult Leader and patient’s guardian who diligently carried out his duties is also here.”

Demon Buddha was about to turn around and go back inside regardless.

“A temporary Cult Leader who even got poisoned.”

Demon Buddha flinched in surprise and turned back. Instead of looking at Geom Mugeuk, he looked at Geom Muyang.

When Geom Muyang merely nodded lightly instead of reacting as if to tell his brother not to joke around like that, golden radiance burst from Demon Buddha’s body.

“Poison King, come out for a moment.”

He called for the Poison King first.

“That is too much. You judge by my brother’s reaction rather than by my words!”

Ignoring Geom Mugeuk entirely, Demon Buddha shouted once more.

“Hurry and come out!”

At that, the Poison King came outside.

“The temporary Cult Leader has been poisoned.”

Despite the urgency in those words, the Poison King remained calm. Instead of examining Geom Mugeuk, he squatted down and looked over the poisonous herbs spread out in the courtyard as he said,

“He must have done something to deserve it.”

Just as Demon Buddha found that reaction strange, Geom Mugeuk spoke from behind him.

“Demon Buddha, I am immune to all poisons.”

Demon Buddha turned toward Geom Mugeuk. Though Geom Mugeuk looked at him with serious eyes, Demon Buddha once again tried to determine the truth through Geom Muyang.

Geom Muyang nodded. There could be no answer more certain than that reaction.

‘Truly astonishing and remarkable.’

Only then did Demon Buddha understand the Poison King’s reaction.

“You already knew, Poison King.”

The Poison King nodded. Back when Geom Mugeuk had taken the Heavenly Secret Pill, the Poison King had revealed it then. He had said he already knew that Geom Mugeuk was immune to all poisons.

Geom Mugeuk did not bother hiding the secret from these two.

“Now two more people know that secret. Ah, Pavilion Master Seo knows as well. He was with me when I obtained the fortuity of immunity to all poisons.”

He had no intention of deliberately boasting about it, but in this situation, where they had to face poison arts, he had no intention of deceiving his brother and Demon Buddha.

Demon Buddha did not rejoice at being told the secret. Instead, he spoke to Geom Mugeuk with a grave expression.

“Do not tell anyone else about this from now on. Since you told me, you may think you should tell the other Demon Venerables as well out of fairness. Do not think that way. As of today, this matter becomes an eternal secret. Promise me.”

How could Geom Mugeuk not know what he meant? He was making sure the secret would not leak any further, if only because of the promise made to him. The fewer people who knew, the safer it would be.

“I promise you, Demon Buddha.”

Geom Mugeuk bowed politely. This was precisely why Geom Mugeuk liked Demon Buddha and held him in high regard.

Because of him, Demon Buddha’s dream had been crushed, so it would not have been strange for him to resent him. And yet he sincerely worried about him like this. Though short in stature, Demon Buddha possessed a heart greater than anyone else’s.

Geom Muyang felt grateful to Demon Buddha as well. When he had heard his younger brother say he was immune to all poisons, he had not thought this far. There was still much he had to learn from Demon Buddha.

Geom Mugeuk reported to the two Demon Venerables.

“The fact that he poisoned me means he knows my brother’s and my identities.”

Geom Muyang tilted his head.

“Then why did he only poison you? If he meant to kill, he would have tried to kill me too.”

So Jeongrak had even detoxified his poison, had he not? He detoxified him, then poisoned his younger brother. There was something inconsistent about it.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the person who could answer that question. It was the Poison King. He was still moving here and there, examining the poisonous herbs spread around the courtyard.

“Normally, when poison enters my body, my body eliminates the toxicity on its own. But this time, I left the poison inside and brought it back.”

At the words that he had brought the poison back, the Poison King rose from his spot and approached Geom Mugeuk. He made Demon Buddha and Geom Muyang step back.

“Let’s see what poison it is.”

Geom Mugeuk extended his hand forward.

Sssssst.

A tiny droplet formed at the tip of Geom Mugeuk’s finger. He had expelled the poison he had left inside his body.

Geom Mugeuk was not merely a simple body immune to myriad poisons, one through which poison did not work. Having brought his martial arts to the extreme, Geom Mugeuk could even display such a level.

The Poison King fanned the scent toward himself with his hand, and immediately identified the poison.

“It is Three-Day Life.”

Three-Day Life was a poison Geom Mugeuk also knew.

Geom Mugeuk had once helped the Poison King in the Thousand Poison Forest. He had learned a great deal about poisons at that time, and Three-Day Life was one of the poisons he had learned about then.

Fwoosh.

The Three-Day Life at his fingertip burned away under his scorching yang qi.

“He wasn’t trying to kill me.”

Three-Day Life was a terrifying lethal poison. If one failed to take the antidote within three days after being poisoned, one would die on the fourth day. That was why it was called Three-Day Life, because one could live only three days.

“If he had simply meant to kill me, he would have administered a poison that killed me instantly on the spot. No, since he had to avoid notice, he would have administered a poison that made me die tonight or tomorrow morning. But to administer a poison that kills only after three days?”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to the Poison King.

“It seems So Jeongrak knows that the one who poisoned my brother was you, Poison King.”

The gaze that had been on the Poison King now turned to his brother.

“You asked why he poisoned only me instead of you, didn’t you? He wanted to say this to the Poison King.”

His gaze returned to the Poison King as he conveyed what he believed to be So Jeongrak’s intention.

“I neutralized your poison, so now try neutralizing mine.”

At that moment, the Poison King laughed as if it were absurd.

“So he tested me.”

Three-Day Life was one of the poisons whose presence was truly difficult to detect after one was poisoned. It was the most suitable poison to use as a test.

“If you had failed to discover within three days that I had been poisoned? You would have borne responsibility for failing to prevent the successor of our cult from being poisoned to death right before your eyes.”

Even if his father forgave him, it would be something the Poison King himself could not endure.

For an instant, a cold chill gathered in the Poison King’s eyes, then vanished.

“Of course, you naturally would have noticed.”

Geom Mugeuk believed it. No matter where the Poison King was looking, no matter how distracted he was by something else, he would have noticed.

He might fail to notice if Geom Mugeuk’s severed arm were brought to him because he was too busy looking at poisonous plants and insects, but there was no way he would fail to notice Geom Mugeuk being poisoned. It was a matter related to poison, after all.

“Have you perhaps met So Jeongrak before?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the Poison King shook his head. Regarding him, he had only heard rumors that there was a physician in Wuhan highly skilled at detoxification.

“I do not know what he is scheming, but he has challenged you, Poison King.”

Geom Mugeuk thought the provocative word “challenge” would make the Poison King say he would go find him at once, but surprisingly, he was not shaken at all.

He squatted down again and began wrapping the well-dried poisonous herbs in paper.

That is how you should be, Poison King. That’s right. Reacting means losing.

Demon Buddha squatted down beside him and began helping. The Poison King taught him how to wrap the poisonous herbs.

Demon Buddha began wrapping the poisonous herbs exactly as he had learned. His hands moved so skillfully and neatly that it was hard to believe he had only been taught once.

“I intend to visit So Jeongrak again in three days. If I go there unharmed, he will think you detoxified me. I plan to see what poison he administers next and confirm their intentions through that.”

Geom Mugeuk said to the Poison King,

“Please handle the conclusion later, Poison King.”

That was surely what the Poison King wanted. Since the man had challenged him with poison like this, the Poison King had to see it through to the end.

Seeing no reaction from the Poison King, Demon Buddha instead grew tense. Had he not experienced it several times in his life? People like this were truly frightening when angered.

Just then, a martial artist from Tongcheon Pavilion, who always delivered messages, arrived there.

“An urgent letter.”

He handed the urgent letter to Geom Muyang and withdrew.

After checking the contents, Geom Muyang relayed the news with a startled expression.

“The Golden Demon Command has been broken.”

At the words that the Golden Demon Command had been broken, everyone was shocked. Even the Poison King, who had shown little reaction despite the other side challenging him with poison, sprang to his feet. Demon Buddha had risen before him.

“Who broke it?”

At Demon Buddha’s question, Geom Muyang answered,

“It was not one person. It was the Demon Flame Lord and the White Bone Soul Demon.”

They were demonic figures from the previous and even earlier generations, men whose names alone could plunge the martial world into terror.

And the most important part was the final sentence of the message.

“They are coming here to Wuhan.”

A heavy silence settled for a moment.

In a situation where they had to deal with Hwayulcheong and So Jeongrak, people bearing infamy that was the complete opposite of theirs were on their way.

“They say they cannot tell which route they are taking or when they will arrive.”

Their martial arts were so formidable that tracking them properly must have been impossible.

Geom Muyang asked Mabul,

“I understand that those upon whom the Geumma Order has been placed are bound by a restriction.”

Mabul nodded and replied,

“That is correct. If they undo their internal energy on their own, they will die within a few days. Seven days at most.”

Anger flashed across Mabul’s face.

“It seems these old bastards are prepared to throw their lives away.”

Breaking the Geumma Order was not merely a matter of violating the cult’s rules. It meant defying the strictest decree of the Cult Leader, and so it was the gravest of all serious crimes.

They were prepared to die?

Geom Mugeuk thought differently. If they had simply gone senile with age, or intended to burn bright one last time before dying, that would actually have been better.

“Or perhaps they have a way not to die.”

Men like Mayeomgun and Baekgol Honma were the sort who cared about their own reputations as well.

“If they were the type to break the Geumma Order just to enjoy a mere seven days of freedom before dying, they would have caused trouble long ago.”

Therefore, there was only one reason they had moved.

“The bastards behind this must have promised to lift their restrictions.”

The three who were listening nodded. Yes, if not for that, there was no way not just one, but both of them would have moved at the same time.

“Is there a possibility the restrictions have already been lifted?”

At Geom Muyang’s question, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“If they intended to make use of those old demons, they wouldn’t have released them in advance. Instead, they must have given them certainty that the restrictions could be lifted.”

Just what sort of faith had they given to make those old demons move?

Geom Muyang said worriedly,

“Their target must be you.”

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“If that were the case, So Jeongrak would have administered a deadly poison that would have killed me instantly.”

“If you are not the target, then why are they drawing them to Wuhan?”

There was clearly another purpose. The first possible reason that came to mind was this.

“First, it must be because the person who can lift their restrictions is here.”

They would have to fulfill the demands of the bastards behind the scenes and have the restrictions lifted within seven days. They did not have much time.

“What if they come and kill righteous faction experts in Wuhan? Then how will the righteous martial artists view this situation?”

Geom Mugeuk, his elder brother, the Poison King, and Mabul were already in Wuhan.

And if it became known that those infamous old demons of the previous and even the prior generation had joined them as well? Fear and hatred would move the righteous murim.

“I don’t know exactly what their goal is.”

Geom Mugeuk calmly added, his gaze deepening. What made the two old demons coming here frightening was not their terrifying martial arts.

“People with nowhere to return to are coming.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 642

Chapter 642: Who They’re Coming to Kill
---------------------------------------
A dozen or so carriages raced in a single line.

The dragons painted on the carriage walls looked as though they might ascend to the heavens at any moment.

Martial artists passing by respectfully cupped their fists toward the carriages. In Wuhan, there was only one person who could use carriages emblazoned with dragons.

Sure enough, the carriage at the head bore the flag symbolizing the Murim Alliance Leader.

The carriages came to a stop before Musojang.

The doors of the ten carriages lined up in a row opened simultaneously.

Jin Paecheon stepped down from the seventh carriage. From the remaining carriages, the warriors of his escort guard alighted.

On an official procession of the Murim Alliance Leader, a dozen identical carriages moved together like this so no one could tell which one he was riding in.

When Jin Paecheon got out, the heroes guarding the front of Musojang all cupped their fists and bowed at once. They had been keeping watch there ever since Cheonae Geosa was ambushed.

“We greet the Alliance Leader!”

Receiving the martial artists’ welcome, Jin Paecheon entered Musojang. The warriors guarding the courtyard also greeted him with proper courtesy.

At the very least, the martial artists gathered here respected and followed Cheonae Geosa more than they did the Murim Alliance Leader.

Having heard of the Alliance Leader’s arrival, Cheonae Geosa came outside.

“I greet the Alliance Leader.”

Though they were old friends, since they were in the presence of the heroes, Cheonae Geosa showed the utmost courtesy due to the Alliance Leader.

“Is your body feeling any better?”

“Thanks to your concern, I am well. Come, please step inside.”

The two entered the building. Only after Cheonae Geosa dismissed everyone around them did they speak comfortably.

“I should have come sooner. I’m sorry.”

“Not at all. I’m grateful you came despite how busy you are.”

Hwa Yulcheong clasped Jin Paecheon’s hand. His hand was so dry it felt like gripping a withered branch.

Jin Paecheon handed him a small box he had prepared from within his robes.

“What is this?”

When Hwa Yulcheong opened the box, there was a small medicinal pill inside.

“It’s a Blood-Replenishing Pill.”

The Blood-Replenishing Pill was medicine taken after losing a great deal of blood, the best possible remedy for restoring vitality after an injury.

“Why did you bring something so precious?”

“What could be more precious to me than you?”

“Thank you. I’ll take it properly.”

“Take it now. Otherwise, won’t you sell it later and use it to help people? Take it in front of me.”

Their gazes tangled in midair. Jin Paecheon did not back down.

“Very well.”

Hwa Yulcheong took the Blood-Replenishing Pill on the spot and circulated his true qi through one full cycle.

Since there was no better medicine for an injury, Hwa Yulcheong’s complexion improved at once.

“Thank you.”

“No need for thanks.”

“It’s been so long since we last met. Will you have a drink?”

Jin Paecheon shook his head.

“Drinking after taking medicine? Don’t be ridiculous. I’m fine.”

“Then at least have some tea. I brewed some earlier.”

While Hwa Yulcheong prepared the tea, Jin Paecheon looked around the interior.

Nothing had changed since the last time he had come. The furniture, the desk—everything was old and cheap. He was the kind of man who spent all the money he had on the poor.

The martial artists who had come out of their own volition to protect him spoke to the life he had lived.

It was hard to believe that such a person was behind a conspiracy.

Hwa Yulcheong brought the teapot to the table.

To think a moment would come when he had to wonder whether poison had been put in the tea.

“How long has it been? This is the first time since we met after the three-party summit, isn’t it?”

Hwa Yulcheong remembered their last meeting precisely.

“You were quite worked up that day.”

“I was?”

I was worked up? Jin Paecheon could not quite remember whether he had been. Hwa Yulcheong, on the other hand, remembered that day clearly.

“That day, you spoke more about the Young Cult Leader than about the Demonic Cult Leader or the Evil Dao Alliance Leader. You said he would become terrifying once he grew.”

He remembered. Perhaps, to him, it had sounded like excitement. Or perhaps he really had been excited. Hwa Yulcheong was the only person to whom he could honestly reveal such feelings.

“That Young Cult Leader is in Wuhan.”

Hwa Yulcheong’s hand, which had been lifting a teacup, stopped. A look of surprise flashed across his face above the cup.

“Is that true?”

Jin Paecheon told him the facts as they were.

“There are people who forged and sold the Divine Cult’s hidden weapons. The very same hidden weapons that were used to attack you.”

“The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult came all the way here personally because of that?”

Hwa Yulcheong looked as though he could not understand.

Jin Paecheon recalled what Geom Mugeuk had told him.

Fight with the truth. Then you may see something that only you, Alliance Leader, can recognize.

“That is not the only reason.”

Jin Paecheon showed his hand first. He had not come here to play a battle of wits.

“They suspect you are behind it.”

Hwa Yulcheong looked as though he did not know how to take those words or how to react to them. In the end, he let out a hollow laugh.

“Why would the Demonic Cult try to frame me with such an absurd accusation?”

Hwa Yulcheong did not even ask, Surely you don’t believe that, do you? It seemed he naturally assumed Jin Paecheon would not.

“Then was the ambush on me related to that matter as well?”

“There is a high possibility.”

“It seems those bastards are plotting some scheme.”

The word scheme struck Jin Paecheon in the chest as though it had lodged there.

Whose scheme is this?

Geom Mugeuk’s? Hwa Yulcheong’s?

Jin Paecheon rose from his seat and walked to the window.

Hwa Yulcheong remained seated, watching his back.

For a moment, silence flowed through the room. Jin Paecheon’s back, different from usual, forced a single question.

“Do you suspect me?”

Compared to the difficulty with which the question had been asked, the answer came immediately.

“How could I? I’ve watched your life from your side all these years. It’s just… why would that clever man frame you with such an absurd accusation, as you put it? That thought keeps coming to me.”

But Hwa Yulcheong was looking precisely into his friend’s heart.

“No. You are wavering right now.”

Jin Paecheon did not deny it.

Not denying it at this moment.

This was exactly what Geom Mugeuk had intended. If he held tightly to the truth and faced the other man, he thought, at the very least he would not be swept away.

Jin Paecheon honestly revealed his feelings.

“Yes, I admit it. The Young Cult Leader is someone who has given me that much trust.”

If it had not been for Geom Mugeuk, he could say with certainty that he would never have suspected Hwa Yulcheong. Even if clear evidence had emerged, even if a joint petition written by him had appeared, he would have believed his friend.

Because of Geom Mugeuk, he was now doing something he absolutely should never do to a friend.

He was tearing down, in a single morning, the trust they had built over a lifetime.

“Do you remember the day we first met?”

At Jin Paecheon’s question, Hwa Yulcheong answered.

“How could I forget?”

Hwa Yulcheong added, his gaze filled with regret for the past.

“My life changed when I met you that day.”

Jin Paecheon, who had been looking out the window, slowly turned and looked at Hwa Yulcheong.

Was it because this situation pained him so much? Hwa Yulcheong said something he had never once said to Jin Paecheon all this time.

“Do you know why I have lived helping people like this?”

Other than his great and chivalrous nature, Jin Paecheon wondered what other reason there could possibly be.

“It was because of you.”

“Because of me?”

“Before I met you, I was not the sort of person who sacrificed himself for others to this extent.”

And yet, the reason he had lived such a life was something Jin Paecheon had never imagined.

“I wanted to become a friend worthy of you.”

“!”

It was a fact Jin Paecheon had not known. He had never once said anything like this before.

“I was always the little bird trying to keep pace with a stork until its legs split.”

Hwa Yulcheong’s gaze was directed at Jin Paecheon’s teacup, from which he had not taken a single sip. That look seemed to be saying this:

I lived my whole life like this because of you, and now you suspect me after listening to the words of a demonic man?

Because he did not argue or question him, that gaze looked all the more sorrowful.

Jin Paecheon calmly bade him farewell. He realized that his own emotions had grown too intense, and he could no longer look at him rationally.

“I sincerely hope the day comes when I can apologize to you from the heart. Then, take good care of yourself.”

As he slowly moved and passed by his side, Hwa Yulcheong spoke.

“You may suspect me, but suspect the Young Cult Leader as well. That Young Cult Leader is a terrifying man who can drive this much of a wedge between you and me.”

With genuine concern, Hwa Yulcheong conveyed his heart to his friend.

“I am fine, so please be careful.”

* * *

Jin Paecheon stood in a pavilion in Cheongunrim.

It was the very pavilion in the bamboo forest where he had previously met Geom Muyang. Today, too, fog lay thick over the place.

Geom Mugeuk arrived there. The person who had contacted him and asked to meet was Jin Paecheon. Since he had met his friend, he thought it was now time to meet Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk climbed up into the pavilion and stood side by side with Jin Paecheon.

“On my way here, I kept thinking about what the wisest choice I could make would be.”

“Did you find an answer?”

“Yes, I did.”

Their gazes, which had been directed toward the fog, turned to one another. Jin Paecheon’s eyes were still searching for the truth.

“Trust. Whatever you may think of me, Alliance Leader, I decided I must trust you. I should tell you everything. That way, there will be no misunderstanding between you and me. That is the conclusion I reached.”

No matter how clear and deep his eyes were as he spoke, it was not easy for that heart to be conveyed to Jin Paecheon as it was now.

“Sometimes it is precisely because of trust that the truth becomes obscured.”

It was a statement that applied to both Geom Mugeuk and Hwa Yulcheong.

“I am on my way back from meeting that friend.”

Geom Mugeuk already knew. It had been an official procession, after all.

“I told him everything honestly.”

“And what did he say?”

Geom Mugeuk was curious about Hwa Yulcheong’s reaction.

“I thought he would be angry, but he did not get angry.”

Geom Mugeuk thought that if Hwa Yulcheong had responded so calmly and composedly, then nothing could have been learned. But precisely because of that, there was something Jin Paecheon had discovered.

“Rather, while looking at that friend worrying about me, I realized one thing.”

Jin Paecheon’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk.

“That friend has never once gotten angry at me.”

Jin Paecheon spoke again, as though emphasizing it.

“We were friends for decades, yet that friend never once got angry at me. Astonishingly, I realized that today. Yes, it could be so. He is a friend with such a good nature, after all. Then do you know how I was?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled as he said,

“You must have gotten angry often, Alliance Leader.”

Though age had now tempered him and he controlled his emotions well, he had originally been a rough and domineering man.

“No. I never got angry at that man either.”

It was a result surprising to both Geom Mugeuk, who was listening, and Jin Paecheon, the person concerned.

“When I think of my temper in my youth, it was impossible. I thought about it on the way here. Why had I never once gotten angry at that man?”

And Jin Paecheon found the answer.

“Because that friend always matched my mood.”

Jin Paecheon had never once seriously thought about this in his life. He was a good-natured friend, and so he was comfortable to be with.

“When I was the successor, and even after I became the Alliance Leader, he always adjusted himself to me. If I became angry, he became angry with me. If I was happy, he was happy with me.”

In a word, he had been a truly good friend.

“But when I thought about it, that friend had never once revealed his own emotions to me. Neither joy nor sadness. Not even irritation.”

If that had been his original nature, then it might have made sense.

“He is a man who does not spare his tears for the poor. When he sees injustice, he grows angrier than anyone. And yet he has never expressed those overflowing emotions to me.”

Jin Paecheon asked Geom Mugeuk with eyes that demanded an answer.

“Because I am the Alliance Leader? Because he found me difficult?”

Jin Paecheon was agitated, and this was the first time he had ever spoken so much to someone. He truly had not expected that someone to be Geom Mugeuk.

At last, Geom Mugeuk, who had only been listening, opened his mouth.

“Is that not why you were able to come this far?”

“!”

“There was no reason for the relationship to be ruined, and you steadily built a friendship over decades, did you not?”

Even as the Murim Alliance Leader, how could he not feel the discomfort of suspecting an old friend? And this was a distortion in the relationship he had discovered on his own. Jin Paecheon’s suspicion turned back toward Geom Mugeuk.

“Are you not the same, treating people with fine words?”

Geom Mugeuk calmly persuaded him.

“If I were not the temporary Cult Leader but the real Cult Leader, then you would be right to suspect that our cult orchestrated this situation. I am someone skilled at sowing discord and quite good at petty schemes. But as long as my father is the Cult Leader, we do not use methods like this. My father would simply push his way in. ‘Go on, try to stop me,’ he would say.”

He could not deny those words. The Geom Ujin he knew was such a man.

“Moreover, he would not create a situation like this.”

Geom Mugeuk delivered the news to Jin Paecheon.

“The old demons who broke the Forbidden Demon Decree are coming here.”

“!”

Jin Paecheon knew better than anyone what the Forbidden Demon Decree was. It was an edict that had been passed down in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult for hundreds of years.

He had judged that the reason they did not kill the Demonic Cult experts who had committed crimes and instead left them alive was ultimately to use them one day when a battle against the Murim Alliance broke out.

“To have broken the Forbidden Demon Decree means they disobeyed the Cult Leader’s command. To use such people to plot something? If that is what you think, then you have misunderstood my father greatly.”

It was a moment when the old demons’ breaking of the Forbidden Demon Decree worked in his favor.

Jin Paecheon, too, agreed with Geom Mugeuk’s words.

“Who is coming?”

“Mayeomgun and Baekgol Honma are coming.”

Jin Paecheon’s expression hardened. If mishandled, they were men who would raise a storm of blood.

“Why are they coming?”

“We did not send them, so how would I know? But what they are best at is killing people, so they must be coming to kill someone.”

A cold energy flowed out from Jin Paecheon’s body.

Even until now, he had maintained his composure for the sake of the Alliance and the orthodox murim, despite his anger being on the verge of exploding. And now even criminals of the Demonic Cult were coming?

Toward that cold fury, Geom Mugeuk said something startling.

“I believe you know whom they are coming to kill, Alliance Leader.”

“I do?”

To Jin Paecheon, whose expression had gone beyond surprise to absurdity, Geom Mugeuk asked. If their goal was to split the main cult and the Murim Alliance apart.

“Whom would they have to kill for you to be unable to endure it?”

Absolute Regression Chapter 643

Chapter 643: Whether Right or Wrong
-----------------------------------
"Did he ask who I wouldn't be able to bear losing if they died?"

Jin Paecheon could not answer easily. It was not because he had never thought about it before.

Losing someone precious.

He was a man who had felt grief deeper than anyone else in the world because of this very matter. People try in some way to forget sad things, but how could one forget a child laid to rest in one’s heart?

That was why, throughout his life, worry had tormented him countless times.

What if he were to lose even his grandchildren?

How could Geom Mugeuk not know that heart?

Because they had once spoken openly about the death of Jin Paecheon’s son and daughter-in-law, he understood that heart better than anyone.

He knew his question would dredge up old wounds, but he had no choice.

Right now, they were facing treacherous enemies who sought only to find wounds and stab at them.

He knew all too well that it was a question far too inconsiderate of the Alliance Lord.

"Please just tell me the first person who comes to mind."

Jin Paecheon had no intention of answering. He had already said more than enough to this young cult leader today—things meant to be said and things best left unsaid.

"Do you truly believe their reason for coming is to kill my people?"

Geom Mugeuk nodded resolutely.

"Yes, that is what I believe."

"What is the reason for your certainty?"

"Because I cannot find any other reason."

He wished he could say, "Couldn’t it be for this reason?" but Jin Paecheon likewise could not find any other reason.

"I would rather they came to kill me or my brother."

But it was not so. If their goal had been to kill him, So Jeongrak’s movements would have been different from the start.

A name that had not come from Jin Paecheon came from Geom Mugeuk.

"Where is Young Miss Jin right now?"

The next moment!

A powerful aura burst forth from Jin Paecheon's body.

Swoooooosh!

The bamboo groves tilted all at once under Jin Paecheon's aura that extended in every direction.

Geom Mugeuk's hair and the hem of his clothes whipped violently, as if about to be blown away by a storm.

It was a terrifying aura, one that seemed ready to draw a blade and behead at any moment.

Thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud, thud!

The guard warriors who sensed Jin Paecheon’s aura flew in and landed around him. The aura had extended even to those guarding outside the bamboo forest.

Jin Paecheon spoke to the guard warriors.

"Find Haryeong and bring her to the Alliance Lord's residence."

Swoosh, swoosh, swoosh, swoosh, swoosh, swoosh, swoosh!

Half of the guard warriors flew off and disappeared from the spot.

Jin Paecheon did not bother to determine whether Geom Mugeuk's words were true or not.

"Next will be Great Lord Jin."

Again, the truth of these words was a matter for later.

"Deliver word that the Devil Flame Lord and the White Bone Soul Demon may be targeting Great Lord Jin and the Demon Extermination Squad."

Another guard warrior flew off and disappeared from the spot.

Only then did Jin Paecheon dismiss the guard warriors to a distance.

Jin Paecheon withdrew his blade-sharp aura and asked.

"Does this now serve as an answer to your question?"

"Yes, it is more than sufficient."

Though outwardly calm, turbulent waves were surging in Jin Paecheon's heart. The thought that his grandchildren's safety hung in the balance made him feel as if fog were clouding his mind.

When the question of who he would be most unable to bear losing had come up, it was an order he should have given right then.

When Geom Mugeuk asked where Haryeong was, his senses had snapped into focus.

"There is one more thing I must tell you."

This too would be a great shock to the Alliance Lord. But it was something he absolutely had to know.

"Do you know So Jeongrak of the Myeongsim Medical Hall?"

When So Jeongrak was mentioned, Jin Paecheon’s gaze sank coldly. It was not a name that should have emerged in this messy quagmire of conspiracy.

"I know him well."

"What kind of person is he?"

Jin Paecheon grew uneasy. What was he trying to say now?

"Do not beat around the bush. State your business."

After Cheonae Geosa, now even So Jeongrak. The Alliance Lord's trust in him might fall further, but Geom Mugeuk had no choice.

"He too is one of the forces behind this. He is also a master of poison arts."

He had expected an explosive, violent reaction.

Jin Paecheon, who had been quietly staring into Geom Mugeuk's eyes, turned his head to look at the bamboo forest. His gaze was endlessly calm.

"You are not surprised."

"When my only friend may be the enemy, what is there to be more surprised about?"

Though he spoke thus, Jin Paecheon's heart was complicated.

So Jeongrak was a physician who did as much good as Cheonae Geosa. Jin Paecheon had praised him to people countless times and had met him several times.

And yet, he too was behind this?

It was so absurd that he could not even get angry.

The fog that had scattered under his aura began to fill the surroundings again. The area became shrouded in fog once more.

"Do you have proof?"

"I have the most certain proof."

"What is it?"

"It is me."

With an expression asking what he meant, Jin Paecheon turned his head to look at Geom Mugeuk.

"He personally poisoned me."

!

Where could there be more certain proof than this? If one sincerely trusted Geom Mugeuk.

But if not?

"Is that not the most uncertain proof?"

Because he knew what the Alliance Lord meant, Geom Mugeuk merely formed a faint smile.

"If you were poisoned, how are you so unharmed?"

Geom Mugeuk let out a light sigh.

"You may become very angry at what I am about to say."

Having laid that groundwork.

"The Poison King has come to Muhan."

It would be a continuous series of shocks for the Alliance Lord.

Cheonae Geosa, the Golden Demon Command, So Jeongrak, and the Poison King.

Huu.

The low breath Jin Paecheon exhaled reverberated around them. Geom Mugeuk could feel how hard the Alliance Lord was striving to govern his heart right now.

The Poison King's presence was a different matter from other demonic ones arriving here.

"There was a powerful poison applied to the forged memorandum. Because it concerned my brother, I asked the Poison King to protect him."

Geom Mugeuk bowed respectfully.

"I apologize for not informing you in advance."

Jin Paecheon asked in a cold tone.

"You said you trusted me. Why did you not tell me?"

Geom Mugeuk raised his head and looked at Jin Paecheon. It was the coldest gaze since they had met.

"After learning that Cheonae Geosa was behind this, I judged that I had to move cautiously. Until then, I could not leave the cult empty."

It was an excuse spoken truthfully, but Geom Mugeuk did not end with that.

"At the time, I thought that was the best course, but thinking on it now, I should have found a way to inform the Alliance Lord first. I am sorry."

Geom Mugeuk conveyed his true feelings exactly.

"To be honest, I was worried. The one behind this is someone who has been friends with the Alliance Lord for decades, and I feared that if I spoke rashly, I might ruin everything. In short, I could not trust you. I am not qualified to speak of trust to the Alliance Lord."

Jin Paecheon silently looked down at the back of Geom Mugeuk's bowed head.

How much time passed like that?

"I accept your apology."

"Thank you."

The matter of the Poison King was something he could have been furious about.

No matter the circumstances, it should never have happened for the Poison King to enter this place, Muhan, without his permission.

Nevertheless, there was a reason Jin Paecheon accepted the apology.

"Looking at the back of your mischievous-looking head, a thought suddenly came to me. What if everything you said is true? If you had not told me these things? Then I would have faced this matter knowing nothing at all."

A truly unwelcome line of imagination followed.

"My friend Hwa Geosa being the enemy, So Jeongrak being the enemy, and the old demon who broke the Golden Demon Command coming to kill Haryeong and Hagun—what if I had known nothing of this?"

Jin Paecheon felt a chill run down his spine even as he spoke.

Jin Paecheon asked with his eyes.

Is it all really true?

Then Geom Mugeuk, who had been serious throughout, smiled brightly and spoke.

"If I were the Alliance Lord, I would truly be happy. I would think, 'Heaven has granted me the opportunity to protect that which I cherish,' and I would have danced in the middle of Maga Village."

Geom Mugeuk pulled Jin Paecheon into that dance.

"So if I rescue Great Lord Jin and Young Miss Jin, will the Alliance Lord not dance?"

Though bewildered by the unexpected question, Jin Paecheon’s answer was decisive.

"I'll dance. If the children are safe, what can't I do?"

"Yes, it is the same for me. If everything I say is true, and thanks to that, we rescue Young Miss Jin and Great Lord Jin, then dance with me! That is a promise!"

Seeing Geom Mugeuk smiling broadly, he could not bring himself to say, "No, I didn't promise!" Perhaps because it was a matter of the children's lives.

"If my words turn out to be false, wouldn't it be the perfect opportunity to pull this dangerous friend from the demonic cult away from your grandson? So please, be happy. Whether I am right or wrong, it is a joyous occasion either way."

The gaze with which Jin Paecheon looked at Geom Mugeuk deepened.

Was it because of those clear, profound eyes? Or because of those unhesitating words?

Listening to Geom Mugeuk's words, they all sounded like the truth. They were so captivating that they swept even an old man like himself along.

"There is one last thing I must say."

At those words, Jin Paecheon made an incredulous expression.

"You've spoken so much, and there's still more?"

"It is something I must tell the Alliance Lord."

It was a matter concerning trust with Jin Paecheon.

"From now on, I will trust the Alliance Lord. Therefore, no matter what happens going forward, I will tell you everything so that no misunderstandings arise. Please watch."

The Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult says he trusts the Murim Alliance Lord, asks him to watch, and asks him to dance together. Listening to him, he truly seemed like a man who would keep every word he uttered.

That was precisely why he had warned his grandson again and again to be careful of such a man.

And at that moment, Jin Paecheon thought inwardly,

'I should be careful myself.'

Geom Mugeuk bowed respectfully and turned to leave.

As he descended from the pavilion, Jin Paecheon spoke calmly from behind.

"Nevertheless...."

Geom Mugeuk stopped and turned to look at him.

"I hope your words are not all true."

Toward that gaze, half sincere and half in jest, Geom Mugeuk answered seriously.

"No, I hope all my words are true. If they are not... it means I am the enemy."

Geom Mugeuk let out a light sigh and added.

"Would that not be a problem in its own right?"

* * *

Geom Mugeuk, having returned to his quarters, summoned his brother and two demonic followers to one place.

"I told the Alliance Lord that Young Miss Jin and Great Lord Jin would be the targets, but there is one more person I have in mind."

Ma Bul read Geom Mugeuk's heart and named one person.

"Cheonae Geosa?"

Geom Mugeuk nodded with conviction.

"Yes, I believe he is the top priority."

The reason was easy enough to guess. If he died by the hands of the demonic ones, a great conflict would arise between the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Murim Alliance.

"But why is he the top priority? The death of his own flesh and blood would seem to be a greater shock to the Alliance Lord."

Geom Mugeuk nodded at Geom Muyang's words.

"Yes, that shock would be incomparable."

"But why? Is it because Cheonae Geosa is more respected by the martial artists?"

"In that regard, the repercussions of Great Lord Jin's death would also be no small matter. Think about it—it was not just anyone, but the Demon Extermination Squad Leader, killed by our cult's martial artist. Judging by the symbolism, though it may not equal Cheonae Geosa, the righteous faction martial artists would rise up like wildfire at his death too. Besides, he is the Alliance Lord's grandson."

"Then why is Cheonae Geosa the top priority?"

This was the reason for Geom Mugeuk's judgment.

"Because of the Alliance Lord's justification."

Geom Mugeuk knew Jin Paecheon's personality well.

"Could the Alliance Lord truly start a war just because his own flesh and blood died?"

He was a man who had devoted his life to chivalry, righteousness, and the justice of Murim.

"He lived for the righteous faction martial artists. Could he drive them to their deaths just because his own flesh and blood died? With that noble character? It would be easier if it were a stranger."

On the other hand, if Cheonae Geosa died?

"A person respected by all righteous faction martial artists was killed by the hands of the demonic ones. If he endured this, he would be condemned instead."

Only then did everyone nod. If the goal was to divide the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the righteous faction, Cheonae Geosa indeed deserved to be the top priority.

However, there was this question.

"No matter what, would they sacrifice someone they raised for decades?"

Geom Mugeuk answered Geom Muyang's doubt.

"That is precisely why the significance would be so great. If the Demonic-Righteous Great War breaks out from the sacrifice of a single person."

Geom Mugeuk added another possibility.

"What if there is more than one death? If Cheonae Geosa dies and Young Miss Jin dies? If Cheonae Geosa dies and Great Lord Jin dies? Or if all three die? Not only would the Alliance Lord's rage be uncontainable, but no one would be able to stop the Demonic-Righteous Great War."

No matter how insane the demonic ones were, no expert would commit such deeds. But what about those who had nowhere to return?

Silence flowed for a moment.

Ma Bul, who had been listening to the brothers' conversation, asked.

"What are you going to do about it?"

Ma Bul did not know how fortunate it was that the target of this question was Geom Mugeuk. Because he himself had not been able to think the thoughts Geom Mugeuk had.

"We will have to handle them one by one."

Geom Mugeuk had already decided on the first target.

"Who in this place, Muhan, could possibly undo the prohibition of the Golden Demon Command?"

Then the Poison King answered curtly, as if there was nothing to consider.

"So Jeongrak."

Geom Mugeuk had thought the same. It was likely So Jeongrak who had performed the great cure to save the Fighting King when he was poisoned by the Poison King.

The Dark King had said so at the time. That he had performed the great cure several times to save him. Being able to perform the great cure meant he could also undo the prohibition of the Golden Demon Command.

Whether he did it alone or someone else was involved, it was certain that So Jeongrak was at the center of it.

"He dared challenge the Poison King, so let us deal with So Jeongrak first."

Though everything seemed separate, this matter was all organically intertwined. And if one of those threads were cut?

"If they truly cannot undo the prohibition of the Golden Demon Command, how will the two old demons react?"

Absolute Regression, Episode 644.

Chapter 644: If He Dies War Will Surely Break Out
-------------------------------------------------
So Jeongrak was making his rounds through the medical ward again today, physicians at his left and right.

He examined the patients’ conditions, prescribed medicine, and set their bones.

“Thank you, Doctor!”

The eyes of the patients looking at him were filled with reverence. They truly treated him like a god.

Just then, someone spoke from behind him.

“Do you perhaps remember me?”

When So Jeongrak and the physicians turned around, Geom Mugeuk was standing there.

“You treated my brother, didn’t you?”

“I remember.”

“I was worried you might not, since you have so many patients.”

“Someone like you is hard to forget.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk said with a smile,

“Right? It would be hard to forget the guardian of such a handsome patient.”

The physicians standing beside him looked dumbfounded, while the patients and their guardians laughed.

“How is your brother?”

“He’s already walking around as if he’s completely recovered. At this rate, he’ll get poisoned again.”

From the way So Jeongrak’s gaze swiftly swept over him, Geom Mugeuk could tell. He was checking whether Geom Mugeuk had been detoxified.

Both the Poison King and So Jeongrak were experts who could discern a person’s condition just by looking.

“What brings you here?”

“You treated my brother. How could I simply leave? So I prepared a gift.”

Geom Mugeuk took a small pouch from his robes and held it out.

“It’s a token of my sincerity, so please accept it.”

So Jeongrak looked at the pouch Geom Mugeuk offered him.

“No need.”

“It’s a cheap thing I bought at the marketplace, but it contains my sincerity.”

“My principle is to accept nothing from patients.”

“If you say that, my hand will feel far too embarrassed.”

Just then, the physician beside him said to Geom Mugeuk,

“The Doctor is busy, so it would be best to visit again later.”

“Then at least take a look at what the gift is.”

Before anyone could stop him, Geom Mugeuk opened the pouch.

At that moment—

Puff.

White smoke burst out of the pouch.

The smoke spread in every direction in an instant.

And in the next moment, something astonishing happened.

Not only the physicians, but even the patients and their guardians all collapsed, one after another.

It had happened in the blink of an eye. The only people still standing there were Geom Mugeuk and So Jeongrak.

“What is the meaning of this?”

So Jeongrak examined the physician collapsed beside him. They were all asleep.

Geom Mugeuk waved the smoke away with his hand and exclaimed in admiration.

“The effect is incredible. I suppose this is the appeal of cultivating poison arts.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at So Jeongrak.

“You’re like this, and so is Cheonae Geosa. Why do you people so love using innocent people as shields? I had no choice.”

“Shields?”

“You’ve lived your whole life as a lie. Aren’t you sick of it? At least when it’s just the two of us, return to your true self.”

But So Jeongrak still wore the face of a physician shocked by the situation. He even checked on a sleeping patient.

“Every time I meet you, you really make me feel like I’m the villain.”

Geom Mugeuk picked up a sheet of paper the attending physician had dropped on the floor.

“A patient record, is it?”

Geom Mugeuk held it up toward the window, checking whether any other writing would appear.

“When you hold it up like this, there should be poison formulas written on it, or secret messages from your organization.”

Of course, there was no way anything like that would be written there.

“How do you endure such a boring daytime life?”

He was not making a baseless guess.

There was something he had felt while staying with the Poison King in the Thousand Poison Forest. Cultivating poison arts was completely different from cultivating ordinary martial arts.

Unless one immersed oneself wholly in that world, one could never survive.

Because the poison would have killed him.

Only one who thought of nothing but poison could become a master of poison arts.

So Jeongrak must have been the same. Had he really come this far with poison arts he had learned in his youth? Without honing them through bloody training, he could detoxify a poison administered by the Poison King?

Impossible. He must have practiced poison arts in secret, where no one knew. By day, he saved lives; by night, he killed them.

So Jeongrak turned toward the door.

“I do not know why you are doing this, but I will call for people. If you intend to harm me, then do so.”

For now, he was trying to escape this unexpected situation.

When an unexpected situation arises, first get away from that moment!

Even if you think you can control the situation, get away first. The feeling that you can accomplish something is a trap and an illusion created by that unexpected situation.

So Jeongrak was a man who had lived by this principle.

And now, a single word came from behind him that made him violate that principle.

“Three-Day Life.”

In that instant, So Jeongrak flinched and stopped moving.

“Our Poison King recognized it at a glance.”

So Jeongrak turned back toward Geom Mugeuk.

The fact that he could endure everything else but not talk of the Poison King showed that he was a man filled with the desire to defeat him.

“I may not have the talent to recognize poisons, but I do have a talent for recognizing something else.”

Geom Mugeuk quietly stared at So Jeongrak and added,

“A talent for recognizing evil men.”

The good-natured face of So Jeongrak stiffened slightly.

“You’ve been acting for more than a few decades, haven’t you? Stop now.”

After a brief pause, So Jeongrak answered.

“I have never acted. Saving people’s lives is my life.”

Geom Mugeuk did not deny those words.

“That’s right. You have lived for a long time as a good physician.”

Because it truly was the life he had lived.

“But I don’t think I can respect that life.”

Geom Mugeuk sat on the edge of a bed where a patient slept.

“If the Demonic-Orthodox Great War breaks out because of you people, hundreds of times more lives than you saved in your lifetime will die.”

Geom Mugeuk could see it. There was not the slightest trace of guilt anywhere on So Jeongrak’s face.

Looking at the patient lying on the bed, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Are you perhaps atoning in advance by saving these people?”

So Jeongrak slowly walked over and sat on the edge of the patient’s bed across from him.

Looking at the patient on the bed where he sat, he suddenly said,

“Bullshit.”

A deep sneer formed at the corner of So Jeongrak’s mouth.

“To think the word atonement would come from the mouth of the Young Cult Master of the Demonic Cult. Do you people even know what atonement is?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly.

“See? Isn’t this much better? Let’s take off the mask and have an honest conversation.”

Even So Jeongrak’s sunken gaze was different. So different that one wondered if he was truly the same man who had seemed so kind—his eyes alone made him look like a completely different person.

“How long have you been waiting for this moment?”

So Jeongrak raised his head, looked up at the ceiling, and exhaled heavily.

The sticky presence flowing from his body seized control of the space in an instant. It was a presence that made a person’s body feel heavy, as if sinking into a swamp.

And Geom Mugeuk saw it. More than twenty vine-like strands of energy extended from his body, connecting to the bodies of those sleeping there.

“If I die, they die with me.”

One might have thought the situation was already to his advantage, yet his personality was thorough to the extreme.

Geom Mugeuk sighed and said,

“A shield is still a shield, even when it’s asleep.”

It was an extension of what he had said earlier about So Jeongrak using them too much as shields.

“I have heard that the Young Cult Master of the Demonic Cult likes to imitate a chivalrous hero.”

He seemed certain that, as long as there were hostages, Geom Mugeuk would never be able to do anything.

Yet the emotion in his eyes as he looked at Geom Mugeuk was intense distrust.

“But blood will tell.”

Naturally, he was certain it was nothing but a dirty scheme to dominate the murim. Those of the demonic path were born that way.

“Be honest. You don’t care if those people die, do you?”

Geom Mugeuk readily admitted it. There was no need to show, in this situation, that he was determined to save them.

“As expected! You recognize me. Didn’t I say evil men recognize evil men?”

Geom Mugeuk looked down at his own hands and said,

“That’s right. Even now, the blood inside me is boiling. The desire to throw all this talk aside and just cut you down keeps tempting me endlessly. It tells me to just kill this sinister bastard. If the orthodox sects misunderstand, let them! Think about what comes after later!”

Geom Mugeuk raised his head and looked at So Jeongrak. So Jeongrak did not hide his emotions. The displeasure of having been cursed at was fully contained in his cold eyes.

As if adding more firewood to that anger, Geom Mugeuk smiled obnoxiously and said,

“I’ve been living while pretending to be good with all my might, trying to hide the fact that I’m this kind of person. Come to think of it, we have a lot in common.”

So Jeongrak nodded.

“I must admit it. I, too, am forcibly holding back the urge to kill you.”

“Why are you holding back?”

So Jeongrak did not answer, but Geom Mugeuk knew the answer.

“Because your conspiracy can only be completed if I’m alive?”

The corner of So Jeongrak’s eye twitched slightly.

“What specific role do I have? The moment you poisoned me, you revealed your own identity.”

It also meant the time had come. It had been a choice made while anticipating that Geom Mugeuk would come looking for him like this.

“Are you curious about your role? It is a very important role.”

Seeing his confidence, Geom Mugeuk spoke calmly.

“You think you’ve lived perfectly as a physician, don’t you? Treating the poor for free, saving countless martial artists, earning everyone’s respect. So you think we can’t do anything to you, don’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk rose from the bed and slowly approached him.

“Do you really have no flaws?”

So Jeongrak merely listened in silence to what Geom Mugeuk was saying.

“At first, perhaps it was perfect. But over all those long years, were there truly no mistakes? Was there never even once when you let your guard down? You lived such a long life without getting caught.”

The corner of So Jeongrak’s eye trembled faintly.

“I’m certain there’s a secret workshop of yours somewhere beneath this clinic. That’s where you studied and tested poisons. No matter how well you hid the entrance, if we bring in an expert to find it, it can be found.”

The corner of So Jeongrak’s mouth rose slightly.

“If there is evidence that the place belongs to me, then you could frame me for a crime.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk widened his eyes.

“Oh? There really is a secret place underground? I was only guessing.”

When he made an aggravating expression, So Jeongrak bit his lip slightly.

Geom Mugeuk stabbed at another point.

“Who did you conduct those experiments on? I couldn’t understand why you worked so diligently to see patients. Unless you were born with a kind nature, you couldn’t live so self-sacrificingly. But someone born with a kind nature would never cultivate poison arts like this, would they? Then one thought came to me. What if you were searching for people with the constitution you needed?”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes sharpened.

“I imagine patients with special constitutions among your patients have gone missing.”

So Jeongrak neither confirmed nor denied it, but his pupils were trembling.

“There’s a secret chamber underground where poisons were tested, and as it turns out, patients have gone missing. Wouldn’t that be enough reason to suspect you? Wouldn’t that be enough to kill you?”

But So Jeongrak was confident.

“Do you know how many people I have saved until now?”

“That doesn’t matter. There are far more people you didn’t save.”

“!”

“They will be the ones to create the rumors and reputation about you.”

Geom Mugeuk stared coldly at So Jeongrak.

“The conclusion is that nothing much will happen even if I kill you.”

After that strong threat, Geom Mugeuk was curious to see how he would react. Killing him would be easy. To those who used poison, Geom Mugeuk was quite literally the pinnacle of their natural enemy.

Not knowing that fact, So Jeongrak remained composed.

“The moment I first saw you earlier, I already poisoned you. The instant you use your internal energy, your qi and blood will swell and burst.”

Of course, Geom Mugeuk knew. The moment that poison entered his body, it had been burned away.

Geom Mugeuk deliberately put on a startled, flustered expression.

While the other man believed he held the advantage, Geom Mugeuk asked casually,

“Fine. Since it’s come to this, let me ask one thing. Just who are you trying to kill by using the old demons of the Geumma-ryeong?”

Geom Mugeuk asked probingly.

“Miss Jin?”

As he did, he carefully observed every detail of So Jeongrak’s expression.

“Captain Jin?”

Still, So Jeongrak only wore a strange expression.

Geom Mugeuk asked about the last person.

“It must be Cheonae Geosa.”

At last, So Jeongrak opened his mouth. It was the moment he would finally obtain new information from him.

“The target changed because of you.”

“Because of me?”

Then again, many things must have changed when he and the Demon Venerables came here to Wuhan.

He was curious who the changed target was.

“We considered many possibilities. We analyzed and reanalyzed every piece of information we had gathered until now.”

So Jeongrak spoke with a peculiar smile.

“And then, an astonishing result emerged. If that newly selected person were to die instead, the probability of war breaking out would be higher than with anyone else.”

It was just then, as an inexplicably ominous feeling came over him.

Creeak.

The door opened, and someone stepped inside.

He was an old master with white hair, and blood-red demonic energy blazed all over his body. The moment one saw him, his presence froze the entire body.

A supreme master who had reached the age of one hundred. Astonishingly, he was the Demon Flame Lord, who had broken through the Geumma-ryeong and come to Wuhan.

Another master appeared through the opposite door.

It was an old man whose body had withered away until he looked like a skeleton. It seemed as if his whole body would crumble if someone so much as tapped him.

But his two eyes alone were deep, immeasurably deep. The abyss of a hundred and fifty years was contained within them like death itself.

He was the White-Bone Soul Demon.

Astonishingly, the two of them had appeared in one place.

Just by looking at them, Geom Mugeuk could tell who they were.

So Jeongrak finished what he had been saying.

“We even concluded that it did not matter who killed him. If he dies, war will certainly break out.”

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes sank. He now knew for certain who that new person was.

“……It was me.”

So Jeongrak slowly nodded.

“Yes. We concluded that if you died, the Cult Master of the Demonic Cult would never endure it.”

Absolute Regression, Chapter 645

Chapter 645: A Greeting Is Fine, Right?
---------------------------------------
My father will never stand by if I die?

Geom Mugeuk thought of his father.

‘If I truly die, would Father start the Demonic Great War?’

Would he not sit upon the throne as usual and speak thus?

—From this day forth, the successor to our cult is Muyang.

Perhaps he would utter only this single sentence and carry on no differently than usual, while appending one more remark.

—Now the cult shall finally grow quiet.
